A company wants to implement a generative AI model using Amazon SageMaker to enhance its customer support chatbot. The model needs to be trained on a large dataset of customer interactions to provide real-time responses to customer queries.

Select and order the correct steps from the following list to complete the task. Each step should be selected one time or not at all. (Select and order THREE.)

- Create a training job in Amazon SageMaker.
- Implement the model to the Amazon SageMaker endpoint.
- Upload the dataset to Amazon S3.
- Create an Amazon SageMaker notebook instance.
- Configure the training job to use the dataset from Amazon S3.
- Monitor the endpoint for real-time inference performance.
- **Answer**
    1. Upload the dataset to Amazon S3.
    2. Create a training job in Amazon SageMaker.
    3. Configure the training job to use the dataset from Amazon S3.
    - **Amazon SageMaker Training** is a fully managed machine learning (ML) service provided by SageMaker. It helps you effectively train a wide range of ML models at scale. The main components of SageMaker jobs are the containerization of ML workloads, and the management of AWS compute resources. The SageMaker Training platform handles the heavy lifting involved in setting up and managing infrastructure for ML training workloads. With SageMaker Training, you can focus on developing, training, and fine-tuning your model.
        
        ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazon-sagemaker-training-073124.png)
        
        The first step is to ensure that the dataset of customer interactions is available in Amazon S3, as this is where Amazon SageMaker will access the data for training. Once the dataset is uploaded, the next step is to create a training job in Amazon SageMaker. This involves specifying the algorithm, the compute resources, and other configurations needed for training the chatbot model. Finally, you need to configure the training job to use the dataset stored in Amazon S3. This involves specifying the S3 bucket location in the training job configuration.
        
        Hence, the correct answers are:
        
        **– Step 1: Upload the dataset to Amazon S3.**
        
        **– Step 2: Create a training job in Amazon SageMaker.**
        
        **– Step 3: Configure the training job to use the dataset from Amazon S3.**
        
        The option that says: **Implement the model to the Amazon SageMaker endpoint** is incorrect because This step is necessary after the model has been trained, but it is not part of the initial steps required to prepare and train the model. Deploying the model to an endpoint comes after the training job is completed.
        
        The option that says: **Create an Amazon SageMaker notebook instance** is incorrect. While creating a notebook instance can be useful for developing and experimenting with models, it is not a required step for the specific task of training and deploying a model using SageMaker. The training job can be created directly without a notebook instance. This step is just an optional tool for more interactive development.
        
        The option that says: **Monitor the endpoint for real-time inference performance** is incorrect. Monitoring the endpoint is only done after deploying the model to see how it performs in real-time. It is not part of the initial steps needed to train the model.
        

An organization plans to implement an artificial intelligence (AI) system to assess and recommend individuals for eligibility in various public health initiatives and social welfare programs. The system analyzes data from multiple sources, including census data, employment records, and financial information. The organization needs to streamline the application process and ensure that eligible individuals receive the support they need.

Which core dimension of responsible AI should the organization prioritize to ensure that the machine learning model aligns with ethical principles and provides clarity on how decisions are made?

- Privacy and Security
- **Transparency**
    
    Responsible AI refers to the practice of developing and deploying AI systems in a way that promotes ethical principles, such as fairness, transparency, privacy, and accountability. It aims to mitigate potential risks and negative impacts associated with AI systems.
    
    **Transparency** refers to the ability to understand and inspect the inner workings, decision-making processes, and outputs of an AI system. It involves making the AI system’s behavior, decisions, and underlying logic visible and comprehensible to relevant stakeholders, such as developers, regulators, and end-users.
    
    **Explainability**: Providing clear explanations and interpretability of how AI systems make decisions, enabling accountability and trust.
    
    **Veracity and Robustness:** Ensuring AI systems operate reliably and consistently and are resilient to potential failures or adversarial attacks.
    
    **Fairness:** Ensuring AI models are unbiased and do not discriminate against individuals or groups based on protected characteristics.
    
    **Privacy and Security:** Safeguarding the privacy and security of the data used by AI systems and handling personal information responsibly.
    
    **Governance:** Establishing clear governance frameworks, policies, and processes for the responsible development and deployment of AI systems.
    
    **Safety:** Identifying and mitigating potential risks and unintended consequences associated with AI systems.
    
    **Controllability** Maintaining appropriate human control and oversight over AI systems, particularly in high-stakes decision-making scenarios.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-core-dimentions+-of-responsible-ai-31-July-2024.png)
    
    In this scenario, transparency is crucial because it ensures that stakeholders can understand how the AI system determines eligibility for public health initiatives and social welfare programs. By prioritizing transparency, the organization can demonstrate that the decisions made by the AI are explainable and aligned with ethical principles.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Transparency.**
    
    The option that says: **Safety** is incorrect because, while safety is important, it primarily focuses on ensuring the AI system operates without causing harm rather than clarifying how decisions are made.
    
    The option that says: **Privacy and Security** is incorrect because it only concerns the protection of data and maintaining confidentiality, which, although essential, does not directly address the clarity of decision-making processes.
    
    The option that says: **Fairness** is incorrect because fairness involves ensuring the AI system does not discriminate and provides equitable treatment, but it does not necessarily address the transparency of how decisions are made.
    
- Safety
- Fairness

An organization plans to implement an artificial intelligence (AI) system to assess and recommend individuals for eligibility in various public health initiatives and social welfare programs. The system analyzes data from multiple sources, including census data, employment records, and financial information. The organization needs to streamline the application process and ensure that eligible individuals receive the support they need.

Concerns about data quality and model trustworthiness have been raised. To address these, which TWO core dimensions of responsible AI should the organization focus on? (Select TWO)

- **Veracity and Robustness**
- Controllability
- **Explainability**
- Privacy and Security
- Safety
- **Evaluation**
    
    Responsible AI encompasses several key areas that organizations must address to develop and deploy AI systems ethically and mitigate potential risks. These core dimensions include:
    
    **Explainability**: Providing clear explanations and interpretability of how AI systems make decisions, enabling accountability and trust.
    
    **Veracity and Robustness:** Ensuring AI systems operate reliably and consistently and are resilient to potential failures or adversarial attacks.
    
    **Fairness:** Ensuring AI models are unbiased and do not discriminate against individuals or groups based on protected characteristics.
    
    **Privacy and Security:** Safeguarding the privacy and security of the data used by AI systems and handling personal information responsibly.
    
    **Governance:** Establishing clear governance frameworks, policies, and processes for the responsible development and deployment of AI systems.
    
    **Transparency:** Promoting openness and transparency about the capabilities, limitations, and potential biases of AI systems.
    
    **Safety:** Identifying and mitigating potential risks and unintended consequences associated with AI systems.
    
    **Controllability** Maintaining appropriate human control and oversight over AI systems, particularly in high-stakes decision-making scenarios.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-core-dimentions+-of-responsible-ai-31-July-2024.png)
    
    In the given scenario, the organization has received feedback raising concerns about the data quality and integrity used for training the ML model. By focusing on veracity and robustness, the organization can ensure that the data is accurate, reliable, and representative and that the model’s decision-making process is robust and free from biases or errors. Additionally, the organization aims to build trust and transparency in the AI system’s decision-making process for assessing and recommending individuals for public health initiatives and social welfare programs. By focusing on explainability, the organization can provide stakeholders with an understanding of how the model arrives at its decisions, enabling accountability and facilitating human oversight.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Veracity and Robustness**
    
    **– Explainability**
    
    **Controllability** is incorrect because controllability primarily refers to the ability to govern and control the behavior of AI systems, ensuring they operate within defined boundaries and adhere to established policies and regulations. In this case, the primary concerns are related to data quality, model integrity, and the trustworthiness of the decision-making process. While Controllability is important, it does not directly address these specific concerns.
    
    **Privacy and Security** is incorrect. Although privacy and security are important, they are primarily concerned with protecting data from unauthorized access and breaches rather than the quality and integrity of the data used for training and the model’s decision-making process.
    
    **Safety** is incorrect because safety only focuses on ensuring that the AI system does not cause harm or unintended consequences, which does not directly address data quality and model trustworthiness.
    

A financial expert is building a model to predict the future value of a portfolio based on historical performance, asset allocation, and market trends. The prediction model will help in making investment decisions and optimizing the portfolio allocation strategy.

Which machine-learning technique should be considered to meet this objective?

- **Linear regression**
    
    **Regression** is a supervised learning technique used for predicting continuous values. It involves determining the relationship between a dependent variable and one or more independent variables. By analyzing the patterns in historical data, regression models can predict future outcomes, making it ideal for tasks like forecasting stock prices, real estate values, or portfolio performance.
    
    **Linear regression** refers to supervised learning models that use one or more inputs to predict a value on a continuous scale. It is used to predict housing prices. After training a model using a set of historical sales training data that includes those characteristics, you could forecast the price of a property based on its location, age, and number of rooms.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/Linear_Regression_1August2024.png)
    
    Just like the red line in the image represents the best-fit relationship between the x and y values, regression models use historical performance, asset allocation, and market trends to predict the future value of a portfolio. This allows for accurate predictions, aiding in informed investment decisions and optimized portfolio strategies. Using these input features, regression models can effectively learn the relationship between these features and the portfolio value.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Linear** **Regression.**
    
    The option that says: **Probability density** is incorrect because this option is generally used to estimate the likelihood or possibility of a random variable falling within a particular range of values, not for predicting future values based on historical data.
    
    The option that says: **Dimensionality reduction** is incorrect because it is primarily used to reduce the number of features in a dataset while retaining as much information as possible. It is not used for prediction tasks directly.
    
    The option that says: **Anomaly detection** is incorrect because it is only used to identify outliers or unusual patterns in data, which is not the primary goal of predicting continuous portfolio values.
    
- Anomaly detection
- Dimensionality reduction
- Probability density

A company is incorporating different generative AI technologies to enhance its internal operations and customer interactions. The company must pair each AI application with the appropriate responsible AI principle to ensure ethical integration and alignment with responsible AI practices.

Select the responsible AI principle that best matches each description of AI application practices. (Select THREE.)

- The application ensures that customer support responses are unbiased and fair, avoiding any form of discrimination or prejudice.
    - Safety
    - Privacy and Security
    - Controllability
    - Transparency
    - **Fairness**
    - Explainability
- The company openly shares information about the AI system’s overall functioning, data sources, and development process used for generating new product design recommendations.
    - Safety
    - Privacy and Security
    - Controllability
    - **Transparency**
    - Fairness
    - Explainability
- The application is designed to prevent unauthorized access to sensitive information while generating personalized customer recommendations.
    - Safety
    - **Privacy and Security**
    - Controllability
    - Transparency ****
    - Fairness
    - Explainability
- **Evaluation**
    
    Responsible AI refers to the practice of developing and deploying AI systems in a way that promotes ethical principles, such as fairness, transparency, explainability, privacy and security, safety, controllability, veracity and robustness, and governance. It aims to mitigate potential risks and negative impacts associated with AI systems.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-core-dimentions+-of-responsible-ai-31-July-2024.png)
    
    **Fairness** in AI ensures that the system’s outcomes are unbiased and equitable across all demographic groups. This principle aims to prevent discrimination based on race, gender, age, or other characteristics, promoting equal treatment and opportunities. Fairness is critical in applications such as customer support, where responses must be unbiased and fair to avoid any form of discrimination or prejudice. Implementing fairness in AI involves continuously monitoring and adjusting algorithms to mitigate bias and ensure equitable treatment for all users.
    
    **Transparency** in AI refers to the practice of being open and clear about how AI systems work, what data they use, and how they make decisions. It involves providing clear, understandable information. Transparency aims to make AI systems more understandable and accountable to users, stakeholders, and the general public. This helps build trust, enables informed decision-making about the use of AI, and allows for better scrutiny and improvement of AI systems.
    
    **Privacy and security** are important for protecting personal and sensitive data from unauthorized access and ensuring that data handling practices comply with regulations and ethical standards. This is crucial for maintaining user trust and protecting sensitive information. In applications that generate personalized customer recommendations, preventing unauthorized access to sensitive information is essential. Ensuring privacy and security involves implementing strong data protection measures, such as encryption and access controls, to safeguard user data and maintain their trust.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Fairness** – The application ensures that customer support responses are unbiased and fair, avoiding any form of discrimination or prejudice.
    
    **– Transparency** – The company openly shares information about the AI system’s overall functioning, data sources, and development process used for generating new product design recommendations.
    
    **– Privacy and security** – The application is designed to prevent unauthorized access to sensitive information while generating personalized customer recommendations.
    
    The option that says: **Safety** is incorrect because it primarily focuses on preventing harm and ensuring the overall well-being of users, not necessarily addressing bias and fairness.
    
    The option that says: **Controllability** is incorrect. This principle refers to the ability to control the AI system’s operations, which is not directly related to providing explanations for its recommendations.
    
    The option that says: **Explainability** is incorrect because it only focuses on the ability to explain or interpret the specific decisions or predictions made by an AI system in a way that humans can understand.
    

A tech company is integrating various generative AI models to enhance its products and services.

Select the correct type of generative AI model to meet each requirement. Each model can be selected one or more times. (Select FOUR.)

- The company needs a model that can generate detailed and contextually accurate technical documentation based on sparse input data
    - Stable Diffusion model
    - **Large language model**
    - Multimodal model
    - Foundation model
- The design team wants to create photorealistic images from abstract concepts described in text, ensuring high fidelity and detail
    - **Stable Diffusion model**
    - Large language model
    - Multimodal model
    - Foundation model
- The company requires a model that can simultaneously analyze and generate content involving text, images, and audio for an immersive virtual assistant
    - Stable Diffusion model
    - Large language model
    - **Multimodal model**
    - Foundation model
- The company needs a robust and adaptable model that can be fine-tuned for a wide range of tasks, including natural language understanding, image recognition, and predictive analytics
    - Stable Diffusion model
    - Large language model
    - Multimodal model
    - **Foundation model**
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Large language models (LLMs)** are extensive deep learning models pre-trained on massive datasets. They utilize a transformer architecture, which includes neural networks composed of an encoder and a decoder with self-attention mechanisms. These components work together to derive meaning from text sequences and comprehend the relationships between words and phrases.
    
    **Stable Diffusion** is a generative AI model that creates distinctive photorealistic images based on text and image prompts.
    
    **Multimodal** models are AI systems that can process and generate content across multiple modalities, such as text, images, and audio. These models are designed to understand and integrate information from diverse data types, enabling more comprehensive and contextually rich outputs.
    
    Researchers introduced the term “**foundation model**” to describe machine learning models that are trained on a diverse range of generalized and unlabeled data. These models are capable of performing a wide array of tasks, including language comprehension, text and image generation, and natural language conversation.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/ai-models-080224.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    – The company needs a model that can generate detailed and contextually accurate technical documentation based on sparse input data: **Large language model.**
    
    – The design team wants to create photorealistic images from abstract concepts described in text, ensuring high fidelity and detail: **Stable Diffusion model.**
    
    – The company requires a model that can simultaneously analyze and generate content involving text, images, and audio for an immersive virtual assistant: **Multimodal model.**
    
    – The company needs a robust and adaptable model that can be fine-tuned for a wide range of tasks, including natural language understanding, image recognition, and predictive analytics: **Foundation model.**
    

A healthcare insurance company manually extracts sensitive information from claims forms and accompanying attachments. This manual process has led to significant delays for customers seeking healthcare benefits. To improve customer service and reduce the manual labor involved, the company wants to automate the extraction process to expedite the handling and processing of claims.

Which AWS service will help meet the company’s objectives?

- **Amazon Textract**
    
    **Amazon Textract** is a machine learning service designed to automatically extract text and structured data from scanned documents. Unlike traditional optical character recognition (OCR) tools, Textract goes beyond simple text extraction to understand the layout and context of documents, including forms and tables. It identifies and processes various elements such as fields, checkboxes, and tables with unparalleled precision, enabling businesses to convert complex documents into highly accurate, actionable data. This advanced capability makes Textract especially useful for handling intricate forms and attachments, such as insurance claims, where precision and structured data extraction are crucial.
    
    ![Amazon Textract](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-textract-08-07-2024.png)
    
    By automating the extraction process, Amazon Textract significantly reduces the need for manual data entry, thus accelerating workflows and minimizing the potential for human error. The service seamlessly integrates with other AWS solutions, providing a convenient and efficient way for streamlined data processing and integration into broader business systems.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon Textract.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Comprehend** is incorrect. This service is primarily used for natural language processing (NLP) tasks such as sentiment analysis, entity recognition, and language detection. It is not designed to extract data from forms and tables.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Personalize** is incorrect because it is a service that only provides personalized recommendations and user experiences by analyzing user behavior and preferences. It is primarily used to create recommendations for applications such as e-commerce or content platforms. Thus, this service does not address the need to extract and process text from documents or forms.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Lex** is incorrect. This option is for building conversational interfaces using voice and text. It is used to create chatbots that can interact with users through natural language. Additionally, it focuses on dialogue management and natural language understanding rather than document text extraction.
    
- Amazon Lex
- Amazon Comprehend
- Amazon Personalize

A company is planning to build a chatbot to analyze customer reviews using large language models (LLMs). They are evaluating various LLMs to find the one that best predicts whether a review is positive, negative, or neutral while also ensuring it removes sensitive information before processing. This step is crucial for preventing unintended consequences and complying with privacy regulations

Which of the following options will meet the requirements? (Select TWO.)

- **Amazon Bedrock Guardrails**
- **Amazon Bedrock Model Evaluation**
- Amazon Lex
- Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth
- Amazon Comprehend
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Model Evaluation** on Amazon Bedrock enables you to assess, compare, and choose the most suitable foundational models for your specific needs. Amazon Bedrock provides the option of automatic evaluation and human evaluation. You can utilize automatic evaluation with predefined algorithms for metrics like accuracy, robustness, and toxicity. Moreover, for subjective or custom metrics such as friendliness, style, and alignment to brand voice, you can establish a human evaluation workflow with just a few clicks. Human evaluation workflows can make use of your own employees or an AWS-managed team as reviewers. Model evaluation offers preselected curated datasets or allows you to bring your own datasets.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazon-bedrock-model-evaluation-080724.png)
    
    Amazon Bedrock’s **Guardrails** allow you to set up protections for your AI applications, aligning with your specific use cases and responsible AI guidelines. You can customize multiple guardrails for various scenarios and apply them to different foundation models (FM), ensuring a uniform user experience and consistent safety and privacy measures across your AI applications. These guardrails can be used with both user inputs and model responses based on text.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazon-bedrock-guardrails-080724.png)
    
    You can set up the following policies in a guardrail to prevent harmful content and remove sensitive information for privacy protection:
    
    – Content filters: Adjust filter strengths to block input prompts or model responses containing harmful content.
    
    – Denied topics: Identify topics that are undesirable for your application. If detected in user queries or model responses, these topics will be blocked.
    
    – Word filters: Configure filters to block undesirable words, phrases, and profanity, including offensive terms and competitor names.
    
    – Sensitive information filters: Block or mask sensitive information such as personally identifiable information (PII) or custom regex in user inputs and model responses.
    
    – Contextual grounding check: Identify and filter hallucinations in model responses based on grounding in a source and relevance to the user query.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Amazon Bedrock Model Evaluation.**
    
    **– Amazon Bedrock Guardrails.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Comprehend** is incorrect because this service primarily focuses on analyzing text data and extracting insights. It doesn’t inherently handle model evaluation for foundational models. Additionally, it doesn’t automatically remove sensitive information.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth** is incorrect because this is mainly used for creating high-quality labeled datasets through human review workflows. It doesn’t directly address model evaluation and automatically removes sensitive information.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Lex** is incorrect. Lex chatbot enables natural language understanding and conversation management. While it’s commonly used for building conversational interfaces, it doesn’t directly handle sentiment analysis or privacy concerns.
    

A technology company is exploring the use of generative AI models, such as large language models (LLMs), for various applications. However, the company is committed to ensuring that these models are deployed in accordance with the principles of responsible AI.

Which of the following are significant challenges of generative AI that need to be considered by the company? (Select THREE)

- Explainability and transparency
- Computational resources
- **Hallucinations**
- Privacy and security
- **Toxicity**
- **Intellectual property**
- **Evaluation**
    
    Generative AI models bring innovative capabilities and exciting possibilities, but they also come with a unique set of challenges that must be addressed to ensure responsible deployment. Among these challenges are toxicity, hallucinations, intellectual property concerns, and risks related to plagiarism and cheating. Understanding and mitigating these issues are essential to harnessing the benefits of generative AI while maintaining ethical standards and legal compliance.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-challenges-of-generative-ai-7-Aug-2024.png)
    
    **Toxicity** refers to the risk of generating content that is offensive, disturbing, or inappropriate. This challenge is compounded by the subjective nature of defining what constitutes toxic content, which can vary based on context and culture. Furthermore, detecting and mitigating subtle or indirect offensive language adds to the complexity of managing toxicity in generative AI outputs.
    
    **Hallucinations** are assertions or claims made by generative AI models that sound plausible but are factually incorrect. Due to the nature of LLMs, which generate text based on patterns rather than factual verification, they may produce convincing yet erroneous information. For instance, LLMs can generate fictitious scientific citations or non-existent references that appear legitimate but are inaccurate.
    
    **Intellectual Property** concerns arise when generative AI models produce content that mimics or reproduces elements of their training data. This can lead to legal issues if the generated content closely resembles copyrighted material or reproduces it verbatim. For example, an AI-generated image in the style of a famous artist could raise objections if it closely mimics the artist’s original work.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Toxicity**
    
    **– Hallucinations**
    
    **– Intellectual Property**
    
    The option that says: **Privacy and security** is incorrect because while it is indeed a critical aspect of responsible AI, it is a broader issue applicable to all types of AI systems, not specifically a challenge unique to generative AI.
    
    The option that says: **Computational resources** is incorrect because it only pertains to the operational aspects of running AI models and is less about the specific ethical or legal challenges posed by generative AI.
    
    The option that says: **Explainability and transparency** is incorrect. While they are relevant in understanding how AI models make decisions, they are not unique challenges specific to generative AI.
    

An AI specialist is working with a deep-learning model in Amazon SageMaker. The model is too large to fit into the memory of a single GPU, and the training dataset is also quite extensive. The specialist must choose a SageMaker built-in option to optimize the training process and manage the large model and dataset.

Which SageMaker built-in options should the specialist use to handle training of large model sizes?

- Pipe Mode
- **Model Parallelism**
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Model Parallelism** is a feature designed to help train large deep-learning models that cannot fit into the memory of a single GPU. This feature automatically partitions the model across multiple GPUs, efficiently training very large models. By distributing the model layers across different GPUs, SageMaker Model Parallelism enables the more effective utilization of memory and computational resources. This distribution helps reduce the training time and allows for handling models that are otherwise too large to be trained on a single GPU.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-model-parallelism-08-08-2024.png)
    
    The Amazon SageMaker Model Parallelism feature includes automated optimization techniques to minimize communication overhead between GPUs. It combines techniques such as pipeline parallelism, tensor parallelism, and data parallelism. These optimizations are crucial for maintaining high training throughput and ensuring that the distributed training process is efficient. By leveraging these techniques, Amazon SageMaker Model Parallelism makes it feasible to train increasingly large and complex state-of-the-art deep learning models.
    
    Hence, the correct answers is: **Model Parallelism.**
    
    The option that says: **Managed Spot Training** is incorrect. This built-in option is designed to reduce the cost of training jobs by utilizing spare EC2 capacity at a lower price compared to on-demand instances. Thus, Managed Spot Training focuses on cost-efficiency rather than the distribution of large models across multiple GPUs, making it irrelevant to the problem of training very large models.
    
    The option that says: **Incremental Training** is incorrect because it only enables a model to be further trained with additional data, leveraging previously learned weights. While this method is beneficial for updating a model with new data without retraining from the beginning, it doesn’t aid in distributing a large model across multiple GPUs. Therefore, Incremental Training isn’t designed to address memory constraints or optimize the training process for very large models that cannot fit into the memory of a single GPU.
    
    The option that says: **Pipe Mode** is incorrect. This option optimizes the input data pipeline by streaming data directly from Amazon S3 to the training instances. This helps to efficiently handle large datasets by reducing data loading times and improving the overall throughput of the training process. However, Pipe Mode does not address the challenge of distributing a large model across multiple GPUs. Its primarily used on data handling rather than model distribution.
    
- Managed Spot Training
- Incremental Training

A company is developing a language model to assist with various text-generation tasks. The company wants to use different prompt engineering techniques to optimize the model’s performance across different types of prompts.

Match the prompt engineering technique to the appropriate description of its application from the options provided. Each technique may be used once, more than once, or not at all. (Select THREE)

- Use a prompt that includes several examples of the task to guide the model in producing accurate responses.
    - Self-refine prompting
    - **Few-shot prompting**
    - Chain-of-thought prompting
    - Zero-shot prompting
- Use a prompt that breaks down a complex task into smaller, logical steps for the model to follow.
    - Self-refine prompting
    - Few-shot prompting
    - **Chain-of-thought prompting**
    - Zero-shot prompting
- Use a prompt where the model performs a task based only on its general knowledge, without any examples provided.
    - Self-refine prompting
    - Few-shot prompting
    - Chain-of-thought prompting
    - **Zero-shot prompting**
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Prompt engineering** is the process of designing and crafting prompts (input text) to guide language models to generate desired outputs. Some of the Prompt Engineering Techniques are:
    
    **Few-shot prompting** is a technique that involves providing a language model with contextual examples to guide its understanding and expected output for a specific task. This is technique is particularly helpful for models with limited training data or when adapting the model to new domains or tasks.
    
    **Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting** is a technique that divides intricate reasoning tasks into smaller intermediary steps. This technique can help the model reason through the task in a structured manner and improve its ability to handle complex tasks.
    
    **Zero-shot prompting** is a technique where a user presents a task to a generative model without providing any examples or explicit training for that specific task. Instead, it relies solely on the general knowledge acquired during pre-training to perform the task based on the prompt.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-prompt-engineerin-12-Aug-2024.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    Use a prompt that includes several examples of the task to guide the model in producing accurate responses: **Few-shot prompting**
    
    Use a prompt that breaks down a complex task into smaller, logical steps for the model to follow: **Chain-of-thought prompting**
    
    Use a prompt where the model performs a task based only on its general knowledge, without any examples provided: **Zero-shot prompting**
    
    The option that says: **Self-refine prompting** is incorrect. Self-refine prompting is a technique where a model iteratively solves a problem, critiques its own solution, and then revises the solution based on the critique. This cycle continues until a predetermined stopping condition, such as running out of tokens or time, is met.
    

A data science team is working on a computer vision project that involves training a deep learning model using a large dataset of labeled images.

Which of the following best practices should the team follow to ensure the security and integrity of its training data? (Select TWO.)

- Conduct exploratory data analysis to identify and remove outliers and anomalies.
- Perform data normalization to standardize the image formats and dimensions.
- **Use cryptographic hashing techniques to verify the authenticity of the data.**
- **Implement role-based access controls to restrict data access to authorized personnel only.**
- Leverage versioning and audit trails to track changes to the dataset.
- **Evaluation**
    
    Secure data engineering practices are essential for ensuring the safety and reliability of AI and generative AI systems. The following are some best practices to consider.
    
    **Assessing Data Quality:** Data quality assessment is the process of evaluating the fitness, accuracy, completeness, consistency, and reliability of data used for training and deploying AI/ML models. It involves identifying and mitigating potential issues such as biases, errors, inconsistencies, and missing values in the data.
    
    **Implementing Privacy-Enhancing Technologies:** Privacy-enhancing technologies are a set of techniques and tools designed to protect personal data and individual privacy while allowing data to be used for legitimate purposes, such as training AI/ML models. Examples of PETs include data anonymization, pseudonymization, encryption, tokenization, differential privacy, and secure multi-party computation.
    
    **Data Access Control:** Data access control refers to the mechanisms and policies that govern who can access, modify, or delete data used in AI/ML systems. It involves implementing role-based access controls, authentication, and authorization measures to ensure that only authorized individuals or services can access sensitive data.
    
    **Data Integrity:** Data integrity refers to the accuracy, completeness, and consistency of data throughout its lifecycle, from ingestion to processing, storage, and analysis. It involves implementing measures to protect data from unauthorized modifications, accidental or malicious corruption, and ensuring that data remains trustworthy and reliable for AI/ML applications.
    
    ![secure-data-engineering-practices](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-secure-data-engineering-practices-05-July-2024.png)
    
    Role-based access controls restrict data access to only authorized personnel, preventing unauthorized individuals or systems from accessing or modifying the sensitive training data. By implementing granular access controls based on roles and responsibilities, the data science team can ensure that the valuable image dataset is protected from accidental or malicious data breaches or misuse.
    
    Complementing access controls, cryptographic hashing techniques like SHA-256 or MD5 provide a way to verify the authenticity and integrity of the training data. By generating unique hash values for the dataset, the team can detect any unauthorized modifications or tampering attempts. If the hash values change unexpectedly, it indicates that the data has been altered, allowing the team to take appropriate actions to investigate and mitigate potential security incidents.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Implement role-based access controls to restrict data access to authorized personnel only.**
    
    **– Use cryptographic hashing techniques to verify the authenticity of the data.**
    
    The option that says: **Perform data normalization to standardize the image formats and dimensions** is incorrect. While data normalization is an important step in data preprocessing for computer vision tasks, it does not directly address the security and integrity aspects of the training data. Normalization is typically performed to ensure consistent data formats and dimensions, which can improve model performance, but it does not provide any security or integrity guarantees.
    
    The option that says: **Conduct exploratory data analysis to identify and remove outliers and anomalies** is incorrect. Exploratory data analysis (EDA) is a valuable technique for understanding the characteristics of the data and identifying potential issues, such as outliers or anomalies. However, like data normalization, EDA is primarily focused on data quality and preparation for model training rather than directly addressing security and integrity concerns.
    
    The option that says: **Leverage versioning and audit trails to track changes to the dataset** is incorrect. While versioning and audit trails can be useful for tracking changes to the dataset, they do not directly prevent unauthorized access or modifications to the data. Versioning and audit trails provide a record of changes, but they do not actively protect the data from security threats or ensure its integrity. They can be complementary to other security measures but do not directly address the security and integrity requirements mentioned in the question.

Which business metric would best evaluate the model’s performance?

It represents the company’s health by demonstrating how well the products or services resonate with customers. Moreover, Accordingly, customer satisfaction is a critical metric that reflects the success of service delivery, ensuring that customer needs and expectations are met effectively. High customer satisfaction indicates that the system is providing valuable and accurate responses, which is essential for maintaining customer trust and loyalty.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/Customer_satisfaction_8July2024.png)
    
    Customer satisfaction metrics typically involve surveys, feedback forms, and direct customer ratings, providing a direct measure of how effectively the system supports and engages customers. For example, customers’ ratings for the practice exams offered by Tutorials Dojo show customer satisfaction regarding how the practice exams helped students pass the AWS Certification. This type of feedback directly reflects the value and effectiveness of the service provided, making it a suitable metric for evaluating the performance of a generative model system in customer support.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Customer satisfaction.**
    
    The option that says: **AWS Customer Reviews** is incorrect because this metric only provides feedback on AWS as a platform, not on the specific performance of the generative model system. While reviews can offer insights, they are not directly tied to the system’s response accuracy or efficiency.
    
    The option that says: **Conversion rate** is incorrect because it primarily focuses on the percentage of users who purchased the products from the company rather than the quality of support they receive. While it can be an important metric for sales and marketing, it does not directly measure the effectiveness of the customer support system.
    
    The option that says: **Average response time** is incorrect. While average response time is an important efficiency metric for customer support systems, it does not fully encompass the accuracy or the quality of the responses provided by the generative model, which are critical for achieving high customer satisfaction.
    
- Average response time

A retail company wants to improve its customer engagement by providing personalized recommendations to online shoppers. They decide to leverage generative AI to achieve this goal.

Which characteristics of a Generative AI system are relevant for creating personalized product recommendations? (Select THREE.)

- Simplicity
- **Personalization**
- **Data efficiency**
- **Scalability**
- Responsiveness
- Adaptability
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Personalization** is a critical capability for generative AI. It involves tailoring content or recommendations to individual users based on their preferences or characteristics, which enhances user experiences and engagement.
    
    **Scalability** can handle increasing workloads efficiently. It’s crucial for large-scale applications.
    
    **Data efficiency** relates to how well a model can learn from limited data.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/capabilities-of-generative-ai-081024.png)
    
    In the context of personalized product recommendations, generative AI can analyze user data, including browsing history, purchase patterns, and demographics, to generate personalized suggestions. For instance, an e-commerce platform can suggest products based on a user’s previous interactions, resulting in a more engaging and relevant shopping experience.
    
    Once trained, generative AI models can generate recommendations for a vast user base without compromising performance. Scalability ensures that the system can handle millions of users and deliver personalized recommendations promptly. Without scalability, providing personalized suggestions to a large audience would be challenging.
    
    Some generative AI models excel at learning from relatively small datasets. This is valuable for personalized recommendations because user-specific data may be scarce. Efficiently utilizing available data ensures accurate and relevant recommendations even when the training dataset is not extensive.

Personalization requires domain-specific knowledge and context, which goes beyond adaptability.
    
    The option that says: **Responsiveness** is incorrect. While responsiveness matters for chatbots and virtual assistants, it’s not directly tied to personalized recommendations. An organization plans to implement an artificial intelligence (AI) system to assess and recommend individuals for eligibility in various public health initiatives and social welfare programs. The system analyzes data from multiple sources, including census data, employment records, and financial information. The organization needs to streamline the application process and ensure that eligible individuals receive the support they need.

Which core dimension of responsible AI should the organization prioritize to ensure that the machine learning model aligns with ethical principles and provides clarity on how decisions are made?

- **Transparency**
    
    Responsible AI refers to the practice of developing and deploying AI systems in a way that promotes ethical principles, such as fairness, transparency, privacy, and accountability. It aims to mitigate potential risks and negative impacts associated with AI systems.
    
    **Transparency** refers to the ability to understand and inspect the inner workings, decision-making processes, and outputs of an AI system. It involves making the AI system’s behavior, decisions, and underlying logic visible and comprehensible to relevant stakeholders, such as developers, regulators, and end-users.
    
    **Explainability**: Providing clear explanations and interpretability of how AI systems make decisions, enabling accountability and trust.
    
    **Veracity and Robustness:** Ensuring AI systems operate reliably and consistently and are resilient to potential failures or adversarial attacks.
    
    **Fairness:** Ensuring AI models are unbiased and do not discriminate against individuals or groups based on protected characteristics.
    
    **Privacy and Security:** Safeguarding the privacy and security of the data used by AI systems and handling personal information responsibly.
    
    **Governance:** Establishing clear governance frameworks, policies, and processes for the responsible development and deployment of AI systems.
    
    **Safety:** Identifying and mitigating potential risks and unintended consequences associated with AI systems.
    
    **Controllability** Maintaining appropriate human control and oversight over AI systems, particularly in high-stakes decision-making scenarios.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-core-dimentions+-of-responsible-ai-31-July-2024.png)
    
    In this scenario, transparency is crucial because it ensures that stakeholders can understand how the AI system determines eligibility for public health initiatives and social welfare programs. By prioritizing transparency, the organization can demonstrate that the decisions made by the AI are explainable and aligned with ethical principles.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Transparency.**
    
    The option that says: **Safety** is incorrect because, while safety is important, it primarily focuses on ensuring the AI system operates without causing harm rather than clarifying how decisions are made.
    
    The option that says: **Privacy and Security** is incorrect because it only concerns the protection of data and maintaining confidentiality, which, although essential, does not directly address the clarity of decision-making processes.
    
    The option that says: **Fairness** is incorrect because fairness involves ensuring the AI system does not discriminate and provides equitable treatment, but it does not necessarily address the transparency of how decisions are made.
    
- Privacy and Security
- Safety
- Fairness

An organization plans to implement an artificial intelligence (AI) system to assess and recommend individuals for eligibility in various public health initiatives and social welfare programs. The system analyzes data from multiple sources, including census data, employment records, and financial information. The organization needs to streamline the application process and ensure that eligible individuals receive the support they need.

Concerns about data quality and model trustworthiness have been raised. To address these, which TWO core dimensions of responsible AI should the organization focus on? (Select TWO)

- Safety
- **Explainability**
- Privacy and Security
- **Veracity and Robustness**
- Controllability
- **Evaluation**
    
    Responsible AI encompasses several key areas that organizations must address to develop and deploy AI systems ethically and mitigate potential risks. These core dimensions include:
    
    **Explainability**: Providing clear explanations and interpretability of how AI systems make decisions, enabling accountability and trust.
    
    **Veracity and Robustness:** Ensuring AI systems operate reliably and consistently and are resilient to potential failures or adversarial attacks.
    
    **Fairness:** Ensuring AI models are unbiased and do not discriminate against individuals or groups based on protected characteristics.
    
    **Privacy and Security:** Safeguarding the privacy and security of the data used by AI systems and handling personal information responsibly.
    
    **Governance:** Establishing clear governance frameworks, policies, and processes for the responsible development and deployment of AI systems.
    
    **Transparency:** Promoting openness and transparency about the capabilities, limitations, and potential biases of AI systems.
    
    **Safety:** Identifying and mitigating potential risks and unintended consequences associated with AI systems.
    
    **Controllability** Maintaining appropriate human control and oversight over AI systems, particularly in high-stakes decision-making scenarios.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-core-dimentions+-of-responsible-ai-31-July-2024.png)

Additionally, the organization aims to build trust and transparency in the AI system’s decision-making process for assessing and recommending individuals for public health initiatives and social welfare programs. By focusing on explainability, the organization can provide stakeholders with an understanding of how the model arrives at its decisions, enabling accountability and facilitating human oversight.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Veracity and Robustness**
    
    **– Explainability**

In this case, the primary concerns are related to data quality, model integrity, and the trustworthiness of the decision-making process. While Controllability is important, it does not directly address these specific concerns.
    
    **Privacy and Security** is incorrect. Although privacy and security are important, they are primarily concerned with protecting data from unauthorized access and breaches rather than the quality and integrity of the data used for training and the model’s decision-making process.
    
    **Safety** is incorrect because safety only focuses on ensuring that the AI system does not cause harm or unintended consequences, which does not directly address data quality and model trustworthiness.
    

A healthcare insurance company manually extracts sensitive information from claims forms and accompanying attachments. This manual process has led to significant delays for customers seeking healthcare benefits. To improve customer service and reduce the manual labor involved, the company wants to automate the extraction process to expedite the handling and processing of claims.

Which AWS service will help meet the company’s objectives?

- Amazon Personalize
- Amazon Lex
- Amazon Comprehend
- **Amazon Textract**
    
    **Amazon Textract** is a machine learning service designed to automatically extract text and structured data from scanned documents. Unlike traditional optical character recognition (OCR) tools, Textract goes beyond simple text extraction to understand the layout and context of documents, including forms and tables. It identifies and processes various elements such as fields, checkboxes, and tables with unparalleled precision, enabling businesses to convert complex documents into highly accurate, actionable data. This advanced capability makes Textract especially useful for handling intricate forms and attachments, such as insurance claims, where precision and structured data extraction are crucial.
    
    ![Amazon Textract](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-textract-08-07-2024.png)
    
    By automating the extraction process, Amazon Textract significantly reduces the need for manual data entry, thus accelerating workflows and minimizing the potential for human error. The service seamlessly integrates with other AWS solutions, providing a convenient and efficient way for streamlined data processing and integration into broader business systems.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon Textract.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Comprehend** is incorrect. This service is primarily used for natural language processing (NLP) tasks such as sentiment analysis, entity recognition, and language detection. It is not designed to extract data from forms and tables.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Personalize** is incorrect because it is a service that only provides personalized recommendations and user experiences by analyzing user behavior and preferences. It is primarily used to create recommendations for applications such as e-commerce or content platforms. Thus, this service does not address the need to extract and process text from documents or forms.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Lex** is incorrect. This option is for building conversational interfaces using voice and text. It is used to create chatbots that can interact with users through natural language. Additionally, it focuses on dialogue management and natural language understanding rather than document text extraction.
    

A company is planning to build a chatbot to analyze customer reviews using large language models (LLMs). They are evaluating various LLMs to find the one that best predicts whether a review is positive, negative, or neutral while also ensuring it removes sensitive information before processing. This step is crucial for preventing unintended consequences and complying with privacy regulations

Which of the following options will meet the requirements? (Select TWO.)

- **Amazon Bedrock Guardrails**
- Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth
- **Amazon Bedrock Model Evaluation**
- Amazon Comprehend
- Amazon Lex
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Model Evaluation** on Amazon Bedrock enables you to assess, compare, and choose the most suitable foundational models for your specific needs. Amazon Bedrock provides the option of automatic evaluation and human evaluation. You can utilize automatic evaluation with predefined algorithms for metrics like accuracy, robustness, and toxicity. Moreover, for subjective or custom metrics such as friendliness, style, and alignment to brand voice, you can establish a human evaluation workflow with just a few clicks. Human evaluation workflows can make use of your own employees or an AWS-managed team as reviewers. Model evaluation offers preselected curated datasets or allows you to bring your own datasets.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazon-bedrock-model-evaluation-080724.png)
    
    Amazon Bedrock’s **Guardrails** allow you to set up protections for your AI applications, aligning with your specific use cases and responsible AI guidelines. You can customize multiple guardrails for various scenarios and apply them to different foundation models (FM), ensuring a uniform user experience and consistent safety and privacy measures across your AI applications. These guardrails can be used with both user inputs and model responses based on text.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazon-bedrock-guardrails-080724.png)
    
    You can set up the following policies in a guardrail to prevent harmful content and remove sensitive information for privacy protection:
    
    – Content filters: Adjust filter strengths to block input prompts or model responses containing harmful content.
    
    – Denied topics: Identify topics that are undesirable for your application. If detected in user queries or model responses, these topics will be blocked.
    
    – Word filters: Configure filters to block undesirable words, phrases, and profanity, including offensive terms and competitor names.
    
    – Sensitive information filters: Block or mask sensitive information such as personally identifiable information (PII) or custom regex in user inputs and model responses.
    
    – Contextual grounding check: Identify and filter hallucinations in model responses based on grounding in a source and relevance to the user query.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Amazon Bedrock Model Evaluation.**
    
    **– Amazon Bedrock Guardrails.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Comprehend** is incorrect because this service primarily focuses on analyzing text data and extracting insights. It doesn’t inherently handle model evaluation for foundational models. Additionally, it doesn’t automatically remove sensitive information.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth** is incorrect because this is mainly used for creating high-quality labeled datasets through human review workflows. It doesn’t directly address model evaluation and automatically removes sensitive information.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Lex** is incorrect. Lex chatbot enables natural language understanding and conversation management. While it’s commonly used for building conversational interfaces, it doesn’t directly handle sentiment analysis or privacy concerns.
    

A technology company is exploring the use of generative AI models, such as large language models (LLMs), for various applications. However, the company is committed to ensuring that these models are deployed in accordance with the principles of responsible AI.

Which of the following are significant challenges of generative AI that need to be considered by the company? (Select THREE)

- **Intellectual property**
- **Hallucinations**
- Explainability and transparency
- Privacy and security
- Computational resources
- **Toxicity**
- **Evaluation**
    
    Generative AI models bring innovative capabilities and exciting possibilities, but they also come with a unique set of challenges that must be addressed to ensure responsible deployment. Among these challenges are toxicity, hallucinations, intellectual property concerns, and risks related to plagiarism and cheating. Understanding and mitigating these issues are essential to harnessing the benefits of generative AI while maintaining ethical standards and legal compliance.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-challenges-of-generative-ai-7-Aug-2024.png)
    
    **Toxicity** refers to the risk of generating content that is offensive, disturbing, or inappropriate. This challenge is compounded by the subjective nature of defining what constitutes toxic content, which can vary based on context and culture. Furthermore, detecting and mitigating subtle or indirect offensive language adds to the complexity of managing toxicity in generative AI outputs.
    
    **Hallucinations** are assertions or claims made by generative AI models that sound plausible but are factually incorrect. Due to the nature of LLMs, which generate text based on patterns rather than factual verification, they may produce convincing yet erroneous information. For instance, LLMs can generate fictitious scientific citations or non-existent references that appear legitimate but are inaccurate.
    
    **Intellectual Property** concerns arise when generative AI models produce content that mimics or reproduces elements of their training data. This can lead to legal issues if the generated content closely resembles copyrighted material or reproduces it verbatim. For example, an AI-generated image in the style of a famous artist could raise objections if it closely mimics the artist’s original work.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Toxicity**
    
    **– Hallucinations**
    
    **– Intellectual Property**
    
    The option that says: **Privacy and security** is incorrect because while it is indeed a critical aspect of responsible AI, it is a broader issue applicable to all types of AI systems, not specifically a challenge unique to generative AI.
    
    The option that says: **Computational resources**is incorrect because it only pertains to the operational aspects of running AI models and is less about the specific ethical or legal challenges posed by generative AI.
    
    The option that says: **Explainability and transparency** is incorrect. While they are relevant in understanding how AI models make decisions, they are not unique challenges specific to generative AI.
    

An AI specialist is working with a deep-learning model in Amazon SageMaker. The model is too large to fit into the memory of a single GPU, and the training dataset is also quite extensive. The specialist must choose a SageMaker built-in option to optimize the training process and manage the large model and dataset.

Which SageMaker built-in options should the specialist use to handle training of large model sizes?

- Incremental Training
- Pipe Mode
- Managed Spot Training
- **Model Parallelism**
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Model Parallelism** is a feature designed to help train large deep-learning models that cannot fit into the memory of a single GPU. This feature automatically partitions the model across multiple GPUs, efficiently training very large models. By distributing the model layers across different GPUs, SageMaker Model Parallelism enables the more effective utilization of memory and computational resources. This distribution helps reduce the training time and allows for handling models that are otherwise too large to be trained on a single GPU.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-model-parallelism-08-08-2024.png)
    
    The Amazon SageMaker Model Parallelism feature includes automated optimization techniques to minimize communication overhead between GPUs. It combines techniques such as pipeline parallelism, tensor parallelism, and data parallelism. These optimizations are crucial for maintaining high training throughput and ensuring that the distributed training process is efficient. By leveraging these techniques, Amazon SageMaker Model Parallelism makes it feasible to train increasingly large and complex state-of-the-art deep learning models.
    
    Hence, the correct answers is: **Model Parallelism.**
    
    The option that says: **Managed Spot Training** is incorrect. This built-in option is designed to reduce the cost of training jobs by utilizing spare EC2 capacity at a lower price compared to on-demand instances. Thus, Managed Spot Training focuses on cost-efficiency rather than the distribution of large models across multiple GPUs, making it irrelevant to the problem of training very large models.
    
    The option that says: **Incremental Training** is incorrect because it only enables a model to be further trained with additional data, leveraging previously learned weights. While this method is beneficial for updating a model with new data without retraining from the beginning, it doesn’t aid in distributing a large model across multiple GPUs. Therefore, Incremental Training isn’t designed to address memory constraints or optimize the training process for very large models that cannot fit into the memory of a single GPU.
    
    The option that says: **Pipe Mode** is incorrect. This option optimizes the input data pipeline by streaming data directly from Amazon S3 to the training instances. This helps to efficiently handle large datasets by reducing data loading times and improving the overall throughput of the training process. However, Pipe Mode does not address the challenge of distributing a large model across multiple GPUs. Its primarily used on data handling rather than model distribution.
    

A company uses a machine learning model to recommend employee promotions. The model favors employees from certain departments or backgrounds due to the training data mainly coming from those groups.

What type of bias could impact the fairness of the tool’s recommendations?

- Reporting bias
- **Sampling bias**
    
    **Sampling Bias** is a common issue in machine learning that occurs when the training data used to develop a model does not represent the entire population. This leads to biased outcomes because the model learns patterns that reflect the imbalances in the data rather than accurately capturing the relationships within the whole population. Sampling bias can happen when certain groups, attributes, or conditions are overrepresented or underrepresented in the training data. This can lead to unfair or inaccurate predictions in real-world scenarios, as seen in cases like employee promotions or loan approvals where certain groups are favored due to biased training data.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-sampling-bias-09-05-2024.png)
    
    Addressing sampling bias requires ensuring the training dataset is diverse and representative of the entire population. Techniques such as resampling, data augmentation, or careful data collection can help mitigate this issue. Identifying and addressing sampling bias in machine learning workflows is crucial for building fair and accurate models, especially in sensitive areas like employee promotions, where biased decisions could lead to unequal opportunities and reinforce existing disparities.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Sampling bias.**
    
    The option that says: **Reporting bias** is incorrect because it only occurs when certain data points or outcomes are selectively reported or emphasized while others are omitted. In this scenario, the issue is not related to selective reporting of data, but rather the overrepresentation of certain groups in the training data, which leads to biased model predictions.
    
    The option that says: **Automation bias** is incorrect. This type of bias simply refers to the tendency of people to over-rely on automated systems and trust their decisions without questioning their accuracy or fairness. This bias pertains to human behavior, not the data itself.
    
    The option that says: **Implicit Bias** is incorrect because it refers to unconscious attitudes or stereotypes that affect human decision-making. While implicit bias can influence the design or selection of features in a machine learning model, this scenario focuses on the data used, where certain groups are overrepresented. This makes implicit bias less relevant in this context.
    
- Implicit Bias
- Automation bias

A technology firm is implementing a new generative AI model for customer interactions and needs to ensure the system’s security against various vulnerabilities.

The ML security team is tasked with identifying the most critical security vulnerabilities that could impact the AI model’s performance and integrity. The team also considered configuring AWS Snowball to optimize data transfer speeds during training.

Which vulnerabilities should the firm prioritize? (Select THREE.)

- **Prompt Injection**
- Model theft
- Overreliance on AI capabilities
- **Training data poisoning**
- **Model denial of service**
- Excessive agency
- **Evaluation**
    
    Ensuring security and privacy is paramount when developing and deploying AI systems, particularly generative AI models. Key considerations include protecting the system from malicious inputs and ensuring the confidentiality and integrity of data. This involves employing prompt filtering, sanitization, and validation techniques to prevent prompt injection attacks and malicious content from influencing the AI’s behavior.
    
    The Open Web Application Security Project (OWASP) is a well-known nonprofit organization focused on improving software security. They provide resources, including the OWASP Top 10, which outlines the most critical security vulnerabilities that could affect software applications, including generative AI models.
    
    ![gen-ai-vulnerabilities](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-gen-ai-vulnerabilities-13-Aug-2024.png)
    
    **Prompt Injection**
    
    involves manipulating the AI model’s input prompts to cause it to produce unintended or harmful outputs. It is crucial to address this vulnerability to prevent malicious users from exploiting the model’s behavior.
    
    **Training data poisoning** involves attackers manipulating the training data to corrupt the AI model’s learning process. This vulnerability can significantly impact the model’s accuracy and reliability, making it a critical concern.
    
    **Model denial of service** involves overwhelming the AI system with excessive or malicious requests to exhaust its computational resources. This can lead to degraded performance or unavailability, a critical issue for customer-facing applications.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Prompt Injection**
    
    **– Training data poisoning**
    
    **– Model denial of service**
    
    **Excessive agency** is incorrect because excessive agency refers primarily to the idea that AI might overstep or be given too much authority. It is not typically categorized as a direct security vulnerability that affects the model’s integrity.
    
    **Model theft** is incorrect because it does not immediately impact the model’s performance, behavior, or availability. Attacks such as prompt injection or denial of service can cause real-time disruptions, like altering outputs or blocking access to services. In contrast, model theft is typically a covert, gradual process aimed at intellectual property, without directly affecting system operations. Although it is a serious concern, it is less urgent in customer-facing environments where uptime and response integrity are critical.
    
    **Overreliance on AI capabilities** is incorrect because while overreliance might lead to decision-making or operational efficiency issues, it does not directly pertain to a specific security vulnerability.
    

A data science team uses Amazon SageMaker to classify customer feedback by sentiment.

What should the team use to convert the textual data into numerical representations for their NLP model?

- One-Hot Encoding
- Tokenization
- Feature Scaling
- **Embeddings**
    
    **Embeddings** are a way of representing words or phrases as dense vectors of real numbers, where semantically similar words or phrases are represented by vectors that are close together in the vector space. This numerical representation is widely used in NLP tasks, such as sentiment analysis, enabling machine learning models to capture the semantic relationships between words and phrases.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-embeddings-04-09-2024.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Embeddings.**
    
    The option that says: **Tokenization** is incorrect. Tokenization involves breaking down a sequence of text into smaller units called tokens, such as words, subwords, or characters. While it is a crucial initial step in preparing textual data for natural language processing (NLP) tasks by converting raw text into a structured sequence, it does not capture the semantic meaning of the text. Tokenization typically segments the text and does not provide numerical representations that reflect the relationships and meanings between words. For capturing semantic meaning, additional techniques such as embeddings are required.
    
    The option that says: **One-Hot Encoding** is incorrect because this is a technique typically used to convert categorical data into a numerical format that can be understood by machine learning models. However, it is not suitable for textual data, as it does not capture the semantic relationships effectively between words or the context in which they appear.
    
    The option that says: **Feature Scaling** is incorrect because it is a technique typically used to normalize numerical features to a common scale, typically between 0 and 1 or -1 and 1. This is often done to prevent features with larger values from dominating the learning process. However, feature scaling is not applicable to textual data, as it does not capture the semantic information present in the text.
    

An e-commerce company needs to analyze customer reviews to determine the overall sentiment (positive, negative, neutral, or mixed) and extract key phrases and entities from the text.

Which of the following AWS services should you use?

- Amazon Textract

Additionally, it can extract key phrases, entities (like names, dates, and locations), and other relevant information from the text. This makes it ideal for analyzing customer reviews.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazon-comprehend-080124.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon Comprehend.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Lex** is incorrect because this service is used to build conversational interfaces using voice and text. It is primarily used to create chatbots and virtual assistants. While it can understand and process natural language, it is not designed for sentiment analysis or extracting key phrases and entities from text.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Textract** is incorrect. This service is designed to automatically extract text and data from scanned documents. It is commonly used for optical character recognition (OCR) to convert text images into machine-readable text. However, it does not conduct sentiment analysis or extract key phrases and entities from the text.
