Which factors can directly influence the latency of a machine learning model’s inference? (Select TWO.)

- **Length of the generated output sequence**
- **Length of the input data sequence**
- Batch size used during inference
- Complexity of the model architecture
- Configuration of the inference parameter
- **Evaluation**
    
    Inference is the process where a model takes an input and creates an output based on that input. In foundation models, this involves predicting the next sequence of words by using probabilities. Importantly, the model tries to determine the most likely sequence of words or tokens that should follow the given input and generates the output accordingly.
    
    When using Amazon Bedrock, you can perform inference on various foundation models, choosing the one that best suits your needs. During this process, you also need to set the throughput, which is the speed at which the model processes the input and generates the output. You can select the throughput through the console or by specifying it in the API request. The throughput setting controls how many tokens the model can handle and how quickly it can process them.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-Amazon-Bedrock-Inference-3Sept2024.png)
    
    Inference latency can be significantly affected by how long the input data is and how long the generated output needs to be. If the input is longer, the model has more data to work through, which naturally takes more time. Also, if the output needs to be detailed or extensive, it takes longer for the model to generate that response. These factors such as how much data the model starts with and how much it needs to produce—are key in determining how quickly or slowly the model can deliver an output.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Length of the input data sequence.**
    
    **– Length of the generated output sequence.**
    
    The option that says: **Complexity of the model architecture** is incorrect. While a more complex model architecture can increase training time, it doesn’t directly impact inference latency. Inference latency is primarily determined by the amount of data processed during the input and output phases, not the underlying structure of the model itself.
    
    The option that says: **Configuration of the inference parameter** is incorrect because inference parameters like Top K, Top P, and temperature only influence the style and diversity of the model’s responses. These parameters adjust how the model selects the next token but do not significantly affect the speed at which the model processes inputs or generates outputs during inference.
    
    The option that says: **Batch size used during inference** is incorrect because batch size is simply more relevant to how many inputs are processed together, which is crucial during training or batch processing. However, during real-time inference, the latency is more influenced by the length of the input and output sequences, not by how many samples are processed at once.
    

A home automation company wants to enhance its customer support chatbot, powered by a large language model (LLM), by integrating information from its product documentation and knowledge base.

Which technique should the company employ to achieve this goal with minimal development effort?

- **RAG (Retrieval Augmented Generation)**
    
    **Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG)**
    
    is a technique that enhances a large language model’s output by referencing an external, authoritative knowledge base before generating responses. It improves the model’s relevance and accuracy without requiring retraining, making it a cost-effective way to leverage specific domain or organizational knowledge.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-RAG-30-08-2024-b.png)
    
    Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) enhances the LLM’s responses by referencing external data sources like a company’s knowledge base, making it the simplest method for integrating up-to-date information with minimal development effort.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **RAG (Retrieval Augmented Generation)**
    
    The option that says: **Fine-tuning** is incorrect. While fine-tuning can be an effective technique for adapting an LLM to a specific domain, it would require retraining the model with product-specific data, which can be time-consuming and involve more effort.
    
    The option that says: **Knowledge Graph Integration** is incorrect. Integrating knowledge graphs with LLMs can be a powerful approach, but it primarily requires significant effort in knowledge graph construction, entity linking, and model integration.
    
    The option that says: **Active Learning** is incorrect. Active learning is a useful technique for iteratively improving the performance of machine learning models, including conversational AI systems. However, it is typically used in conjunction with other techniques, such as fine-tuning or knowledge base expansion. It will not directly address the integration of existing product documentation and knowledge bases.
    
- Fine-tuning
- Active Learning
- Knowledge Graph Integration

A retail company wants to improve its product recommendation system using machine learning. To make more accurate recommendations, the company plans to use vector embeddings of product features and user preferences. The system must also support real-time queries to find the most relevant products quickly.

Which AWS service would best fulfill these needs? (Select TWO.)

- Amazon DynamoDB
- Amazon ElastiCache
- AWS Lake Formation
- **Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility)**
- **Amazon OpenSearch Service**
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility)** is a scalable and highly available NoSQL document database service well-suited for large-scale, mission-critical applications. It is designed to work efficiently with JSON-like documents, making it an ideal choice for storing complex data structures. Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility) can handle queries on these documents effectively, allowing the system to provide personalized recommendations based on user behavior and product attributes. Its automatic scaling and resource management capabilities ensure it can support demanding workloads without manual intervention.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-documentDB-09-02-2024.png)
    
    **Amazon OpenSearch Service** is a managed service that simplifies the deployment, operation, and scaling of OpenSearch clusters in the cloud. It offers robust search, analytics, and real-time filtering capabilities crucial for handling large datasets, such as those used in recommendation systems. One of the standout features of Amazon OpenSearch Service is its support for k-nearest neighbor (k-NN) search, which enables efficient vector similarity searches. This capability is essential for retrieving the most relevant products based on vector embeddings, ensuring accurate and timely recommendations.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-opensearch-09-02-2024.png)
    
    Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility) and Amazon OpenSearch Service work together to provide a complete solution for creating a strong, real-time product recommendation system. Amazon DocumentDB is great at storing and managing the complex data structures required for machine learning models. At the same time, Amazon’s OpenSearch Service offers the necessary tools for fast, scalable, and accurate data querying.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Amazon DocumentDB (with MongoDB compatibility).**
    
    **– Amazon OpenSearch Service.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon DynamoDB** is incorrect. Although this service is suitable for key-value and document data models, it lacks native support for efficient vector similarity searches, which are essential for product recommendations based on embeddings.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon ElastiCache** is incorrect. This option is a managed caching service that provides Redis and Memcached with in-memory data storage. While it is highly effective for speeding up data retrieval and reducing latency in applications, it does not offer native support for storing or querying vector embeddings. ElastiCache is primarily for scenarios where frequently accessed data needs to be cached for fast retrieval.
    
    The option that says: **AWS Lake Formation** is incorrect because it is a service designed to simplify the process of building secure data lakes. It enables the ingestion, cataloging, and secure access to large volumes of data. Therefore, AWS Lake Formation is simply for scenarios where large datasets must be organized and analyzed in batch processes.
    

An AI developer is working on an AI-powered tutoring system with a foundational model (FM) to help students understand complex scientific concepts. The system aims to provide correct answers and explain underlying principles clearly. The developer is exploring techniques to improve the model’s performance.

Which prompt engineering technique will MOST enhance the foundational model’s ability to provide step-by-step explanations for complex scientific concepts?

- **Chain-of-thought**
    
    **Chain-of-Thought** is a technique that enhances the capability of fundamental models to offer detailed, step-by-step explanations by explicitly depicting the reasoning process needed to solve a problem. This method involves breaking down complex tasks into smaller, manageable steps, enabling the model to produce more precise and structured explanations. By prioritizing intermediate reasoning and logical progression, the Chain of Thought ensures that each part of the solution is thoroughly understood and articulated, making it especially beneficial in educational settings where detailed explanations are essential.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-chain-of-thought-09-02-2024.png)
    
    The primary advantage of Chain-of-thought prompting lies in its ability to produce detailed, sequential explanations, making it an effective tool for scenarios requiring deep reasoning and clear communication. CoT prompting enables the AI to articulate the reasoning behind each answer, offering more transparent and understandable explanations when applied to a foundational model.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Chain-of-thought.**
    
    The option that says: **Tree-of-thought** is incorrect because it is a technique that involves organizing information in a hierarchical structure, just like a decision tree. Tree of Thought helps visualize relationships and pathways rather than breaking down complex problems into sequential, explainable steps.
    
    The option that says: **Complexity-based** is incorrect. This technique focuses on adapting the model’s approach based on the complexity of the task or problem. This can only be useful for adjusting the model’s strategy according to varying difficulty levels but does not inherently address the need for providing step-by-step explanations.
    
    The option that says: **Directional-stimulus** is incorrect because it involves guiding the model’s responses based on specific cues or directions. This technique can influence the direction or focus of the responses but does not specifically enhance the model’s ability to deliver structured, step-by-step explanations. Therefore, this technique focuses on guiding the model toward specific types of responses rather than providing extensive details about the reasoning process.
    
- Tree-of-thought
- Complexity-based
- Directional-stimulus

A healthcare company is developing an AI-driven system to assist doctors with medical diagnoses based on patient data. The system must comply with HIPAA regulations and follow responsible AI principles to prevent bias and ensure data privacy.

Which actions should the company take to align with responsible AI principles while developing the diagnostic system? (Select TWO.)

- Exclude patients with rare conditions to improve model accuracy
- **Obfuscate sensitive patient information before model training**
- Use historical medical records without preprocessing
- **Perform regular audits**
- Use third-party pre-trained models without modification
- **Evaluation**
    
    Responsible AI refers to the practice of developing and deploying AI systems in a way that promotes ethical principles, such as fairness, transparency, privacy, and accountability. It aims to mitigate potential risks and negative impacts associated with AI systems.
    
    **Fairness:** Ensuring AI models are unbiased and do not discriminate against individuals or groups based on protected characteristics.
    
    **Privacy and Security:** Safeguarding the privacy and security of the data used by AI systems and handling personal information responsibly.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-core-dimentions+-of-responsible-ai-31-July-2024.png)
    
    Ensuring data privacy is a cornerstone of responsible AI, particularly in healthcare where patient information is highly sensitive. To safeguard this data, it is crucial to implement robust data handling practices, including preprocessing steps such as cleaning, transforming, and anonymizing sensitive information. These measures help protect patient privacy and ensure compliance with regulations like HIPAA.
    
    Moreover, AI systems are at risk of inheriting and amplifying biases from their training data or the algorithms themselves. Responsible AI practices focus on identifying and mitigating these biases to promote fairness and equity in outcomes. Regular auditing and monitoring of the AI system are vital for verifying its performance, detecting potential issues, and making necessary adjustments to align with ethical standards.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Obfuscate sensitive patient information before model training.**
    
    **– Perform regular audits.**
    
    The option that says: **Use historical medical records without preprocessing** is incorrect because using historical medical records without preprocessing or anonymizing sensitive patient information would typically violate HIPAA regulations and responsible AI principles related to data privacy.
    
    The option that says: **Exclude patients with rare conditions to improve model accuracy** is incorrect. Excluding patients with rare conditions from the training data can only introduce significant bias and potentially lead to inaccurate diagnoses for these patients, which goes against the responsible AI principles of fairness and inclusivity.
    
    The option that says: **Use third-party pre-trained models without modification** is incorrect. Using third-party pre-trained models without understanding their inner workings, potential biases, and compliance with responsible AI principles can be risky and may lead to unintended consequences or violations of regulations.
    

An ML Engineer is looking for a relational database service that supports efficient vector data storage and querying, specifically embeddings from machine learning models.

Which of the following AWS services would be the most suitable for this use case?

- Amazon DynamoDB
- Amazon DocumentDB
- Amazon RDS for MySQL
- **Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL**
    
    **Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL** supports the `pgvector` extension, which is designed for storing embeddings from machine learning models and performing efficient similarity searches. Embeddings are numerical representations (vectors) that capture the semantic meaning of text or other data, making this service highly suitable for ML applications.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/Amazon-RDS-PostgreSQL-090224.png)
    
    **With pgvector on Amazon RDS, you can easily set up, manage, and expand databases for your machine learning (ML) applications. The pgvector extension empowers you to integrate ML features into various applications such as e-commerce, media, and healthcare to identify similar items within a catalog. For instance, a streaming service can utilize pgvector to offer a selection of movie recommendations similar to the one you just viewed.**
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon RDS for PostgreSQL.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon DocumentDB** is incorrect. While it supports vector search, Amazon DocumentDB is a document database and lacks the relational features needed for managing structured data effectively
    
    The option that says: **Amazon DynamoDB** is incorrect because it is a NoSQL database service that is typically designed for key-value and document data models. While it is highly scalable and performant for certain types of data, it does not natively support vector data storage and querying.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon RDS for MySQL** is incorrect because it does not have built-in support for vector data operations like the `pgvector` extension available in PostgreSQL.
    

An e-commerce company wants to implement a machine learning application to analyze product reviews and determine whether each review is favorable or unfavorable.

Which type of machine learning model is most appropriate for the application?

- **Binary classification model**
    
    Binary classification is a supervised machine learning model specifically designed to distinguish between two distinct categories or classes. This model is widely used in various applications, such as sentiment analysis, fraud detection, and medical diagnosis, where the objective is to classify data points into one of two predefined categories. In e-commerce, binary classification can effectively analyze customer reviews by categorizing them as favorable or unfavorable, allowing businesses to gain insights into customer satisfaction and product performance.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-binary-classification-09-03-2024.png)
    
    The binary classification process typically involves training the model on a labeled dataset, where each data point is associated with one of the two classes. The model learns to identify patterns and relationships in the data that differentiate one class from another. Once trained, the model can make predictions on new, unlabeled data, determining which of the two categories a given data point belongs to. This approach is essential for tasks like sentiment analysis, where understanding customer feedback plays a crucial role in improving products and services.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Binary classification model.**
    
    The option that says: **Clustering model** is incorrect because this model is used for grouping similar data points based on their features without predefined labels. Clustering is typically unsupervised and unsuitable for binary classification tasks like analyzing whether a review is favorable or unfavorable.
    
    The option that says: **Multiclass classification model** is incorrect. This option is only applies when there are more than two categories to predict. Since the scenario involves only two possible outcomes, multiclass classification would be unnecessary and inefficient.
    
    The option that says: **Text embedding model** is incorrect because it is only used to convert text data into numerical vectors that can be processed by machine learning models. The scenario requires a model that can classify reviews, specifically a binary classification model, making this option inappropriate.
    
- Text embedding model
- Clustering model
- Multiclass classification model

A publishing company uses a foundation model (FM) to generate text summaries from lengthy documents. The company needs to evaluate the effectiveness of the summaries.

Which metric can be used to evaluate the performance of the foundation model for text summarization?

- **Recall-Oriented Understudy for Gisting Evaluation-N (ROUGE-N)**
    
    ROUGE-N is a widely recognized metric for evaluating text summarization models, including those generated by foundation models. ROUGE stands for Recall-Oriented Understudy for Gisting Evaluation, and the “N” in ROUGE-N represents the length of the n-grams (sequences of N words) being compared. The primary purpose of ROUGE-N is to measure the similarity between the generated text and the human-written reference, with a higher overlap indicating better quality. This metric is handy for assessing the performance of models in tasks where the preservation of key information and linguistic patterns from the original text is crucial, such as in text summarization.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/Recall-Oriented_Understudy_for_Gisting_Evaluation_14Aug2024.png)
    
    ROUGE-N can be used in Amazon SageMaker to evaluate text summarization models, especially those utilizing foundation models (FMs). This metric helps data scientists and developers understand how closely their model-generated summaries match the expected outputs, providing insights into the model’s performance. By using ROUGE-N, organizations can refine their models to generate more accurate and relevant summaries, ensuring that the outputs benefit end-users. This metric is widely recognized and part of the evaluation tools available in AWS for natural language processing tasks.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Recall-Oriented Understudy for Gisting Evaluation-N (ROUGE-N)**
    
    The option that says: **Balanced classification accuracy** is incorrect because this metric is primarily used to evaluate the performance of classification models, particularly in situations where classes are imbalanced. It measures the model’s accuracy by considering both the true positive and true negative rates.
    
    The option that says: **Recall** is incorrect because it is a metric used in classification tasks to measure a model’s ability to correctly identify all relevant instances. It calculates the ratio of true positives to the sum of true positives and false negatives. In text summarization, the objective is to create summaries that capture the main points of the input text. However, in classification tasks, the goal is to accurately identify relevant instances.
    
    The option that says: **Precision** is incorrect. This option is typically used in classification and information retrieval tasks to measure the accuracy of relevant items retrieved or classified correctly. Precision does not directly evaluate the quality or effectiveness of text summarization.
    
- Recall
- Balanced classification accuracy
- Precision

A content creator uses generative AI to produce marketing material that closely resembles existing text without proper attribution.

What challenges of generative AI does this situation highlight?

- Toxicity
- Disruption of the nature of work
- **Intellectual Property**
    
    Generative AI models bring innovative capabilities and exciting possibilities, but they also come with a unique set of challenges that must be addressed to ensure responsible deployment. Among these challenges are toxicity, hallucinations, intellectual property concerns, and risks related to plagiarism and cheating. Understanding and mitigating these issues are essential to harnessing the benefits of generative AI while maintaining ethical standards and legal compliance.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-challenges-of-generative-ai-7-Aug-2024.png)
    
    **Intellectual Property** concerns arise when generative AI models produce content that mimics or reproduces elements of their training data. This can lead to legal issues if the generated content closely resembles copyrighted material or reproduces it verbatim. For example, Generative AI can produce text that might closely mimic existing works, raising concerns about intellectual property rights and proper attribution.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Intellectual Property.**
    
    The option that says: **Disruption of the nature of work** is incorrect because this typically refers to how generative AI can change job roles or automate tasks rather than issues of ownership and attribution.
    
    The option that says: **Hallucinations** is incorrect because it primarily refers to the phenomenon where generative AI models produce nonsensical, inconsistent, or factually incorrect outputs.
    
    The option that says: **Toxicity** is incorrect because Toxicity typically refers to the potential for AI systems to generate or amplify harmful, offensive, or abusive content.
    
- Hallucinations

A company is developing a model for generating text-based recommendations. The company noticed that the training data contains skewed examples, affecting the quality of the recommendations.

What approach will enhance the training data to address this issue?

- Ensemble learning
- Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG)
- Transfer learning with a pre-trained model
- **Data augmentation**
    
    **Data augmentation** is a technique for expanding a dataset by generating new samples from existing data through various transformations. This technique addresses challenges in acquiring diverse real-world data by artificially increasing the dataset’s size and variety, with recent advances in generative AI enhancing its efficiency and quality.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-data-augmentatio-03-09-2024.png)
    
    Addressing skewed training data typically involves improving the diversity of the training data or enriching the dataset. For skewed data, **data augmentation** is the most effective strategy, as it introduces more variability into the training dataset, helping to mitigate the effects of data imbalance. This technique involves creating new training examples through various transformations or generating synthetic examples to balance the dataset.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Data Augmentation.**
    
    The option that says: **Transfer learning with a pre-trained model** is incorrect because transfer learning primarily focuses on leveraging existing knowledge from a pre-trained model rather than directly addressing data skewness in the training set.
    
    The option that says: **Ensemble learning** is incorrect. This learning combines multiple models to improve overall performance and robustness. However, it doesn’t change the underlying training data. If the training data is skewed, even an ensemble of models trained on that data will likely still reflect those biases.
    
    The option that says: **Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG)** is incorrect as RAG typically focuses on combining retrieval and generation techniques to produce responses and does not directly correct data skewness.
    

A healthcare organization is developing an AI-driven diagnostic application leveraging Amazon Bedrock. The application is deployed within a VPC that must comply with strict data privacy regulations. These regulations prohibit any internet connectivity to or from the VPC.

What AWS service or feature will satisfy these objectives?

As a result, this meets the organization’s requirement to prohibit any internet connectivity to or from the VPC.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **AWS PrivateLink.**
    
    The option that says: **AWS Direct Connect** is incorrect because this service only provides a dedicated network connection from on-premises to AWS but does not inherently prevent internet connectivity within the VPC.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon S3 VPC Endpoint** is incorrect because it simply allows private connectivity to Amazon S3 and would not cover the need to connect to other AWS services like Amazon Bedrock.
    
    The option that says: **Internet gateway** is incorrect because it primarily enables internet connectivity, which directly violates the organization’s requirement to prohibit internet access to or from the VPC.
    
- AWS Direct Connect
- Amazon S3 VPC Endpoint

A company has deployed a generative AI application using Amazon SageMaker. They must log and audit all API activities for compliance and security, including interactions with Amazon S3 and DynamoDB.

Which AWS service should the company use to achieve this?

- **AWS CloudTrail**
    
    The AWS Account Management APIs are integrated with AWS CloudTrail, a service that keeps a record of actions performed by a user, role, or an AWS service that triggers an Account Management operation. CloudTrail captures all Account Management API calls as events, including all calls to the Account Management operations. By creating a trail, you can enable continuous delivery of CloudTrail events to an Amazon S3 bucket, which includes events for Account Management operations. Even without configuring a trail, you can still view the most recent events in the CloudTrail console under Event history. CloudTrail collects information that helps identify the request that triggered an Account Management operation, the IP address used to make the request, details about who made the request and when, and additional information.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/cloudtrail-090324.png)
    
    **AWS CloudTrail** is designed specifically for logging and monitoring API calls made within your AWS account. It provides a detailed record of actions taken by users, roles, or AWS services, which is essential for compliance and security auditing. CloudTrail logs interactions with services like Amazon S3 and DynamoDB, making it the ideal choice for tracking and auditing API activities.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **AWS CloudTrail.**
    
    The option that says: **AWS Audit Manager** is incorrect because this service only helps you continuously audit your AWS usage to simplify how you assess risk and compliance with regulations and industry standards. While it is useful for compliance, it does not specifically log API activities or interactions with services like S3 and DynamoDB.
    
    The option that says: **AWS Config** is incorrect. AWS Config is primarily used to assess, audit, and evaluate the configurations of your AWS resources. It helps you track configuration changes and compliance over time. However, it does not log API activities or provide detailed records of actions taken by users or services.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon CloudWatch Logs** is incorrect. This service is used for monitoring, storing, and accessing log files from various AWS services and applications. While it can be used to collect and monitor logs, it does not provide the comprehensive API activity logging and auditing capabilities that CloudTrail offers.
    
- AWS Audit Manager
- AWS Config
- Amazon CloudWatch Logs

An e-commerce company has implemented an AI model to categorize products based on images uploaded by sellers. The company must determine how well the model correctly classifies the product categories.

What is the most appropriate metric to assess the effectiveness of the image classification model?

- Root mean squared error (RMSE)
- Recall
- Precision
- **Accuracy**
    
    **Accuracy** is a commonly used metric to evaluate the performance of classification models in machine learning. It measures the proportion of correctly classified instances from the total instances in the dataset, providing a general sense of how well the model performs across all classes. In image classification, accuracy is particularly useful when the dataset has a balanced distribution of classes. This metric helps organizations assess the overall effectiveness of their model in correctly identifying categories from the provided images. It is one of the most straightforward and intuitive metrics for classification tasks, offering a broad view of model performance.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-accuracy-09-04-2024.png)
    
    In the AWS services, accuracy is commonly used in models deployed in Amazon SageMaker to evaluate the performance of classification algorithms. SageMaker offers built-in metrics to help users easily monitor and optimize their models’ accuracy. Using accuracy as a key performance indicator, data scientists can fine-tune their models to ensure they make correct predictions across all categories, leading to more reliable and practical AI-driven applications. For cases with imbalanced data, SageMaker also supports using other metrics such as precision, recall, and F1 score to provide a more nuanced understanding of model performance.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Accuracy.**
    
    The option that says: **Recall** is incorrect because this metric only measures the proportion of actual positive instances (true positives) correctly identified by the model. In image classification, the objective is to accurately categorize images into multiple categories. Focusing solely on recall might cause the model to prioritize certain categories, potentially overlooking overall accuracy.
    
    The option that says: **Precision** is incorrect because it is a metric that measures the proportion of correct predicted positive instances. Precision is particularly valuable in scenarios where the cost of false positives is high, such as in spam detection or targeted advertising. However, like recall, precision only provides a partial view of the model’s performance, focusing on the correctness of positive predictions.
    
    The option that says: **Root mean squared error (RMSE)** is incorrect. This metric is typically used for regression models, not classification models. RMSE measures the average magnitude of the error between predicted and actual values, making it ideal for assessing continuous variables. Since image classification involves discrete categories rather than continuous outputs, RMSE is not applicable in this scenario.
    

A finance company uses Amazon Bedrock to deploy a generative AI-based chatbot to provide personalized financial advice and answer related queries. The company needs a solution to maintain control over the chatbot’s generated content to ensure accuracy, appropriateness, and compliance with regulatory standards.

What AWS service will help ensure that the chatbot’s advice meets accuracy and regulatory compliance standards?

- **Guardrails for Amazon Bedrock**
    
    **Guardrails for Amazon Bedrock** are an essential feature designed to ensure that content generated by AI models adheres to specific guidelines and complies with regulatory standards. This capability is significant for industries like finance, where the accuracy and appropriateness of generated content are critical. Guardrails allow organizations to establish boundaries around the AI-generated outputs, ensuring that the chatbot’s responses are relevant, accurate, and conform to the company’s policies and industry regulations. By implementing these guardrails, companies can mitigate risks associated with incorrect or non-compliant advice, which is crucial for maintaining user trust and avoiding potential legal issues.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-bedrock-guardrails-09-04-2024.png)
    
    Furthermore, Guardrails for Amazon Bedrock provides a way to implement customizable content moderation rules and ensure that the AI model’s behavior is predictable and reliable. This is particularly important in sensitive domains like finance, where the consequences of incorrect or non-compliant advice can be significant. Companies can build trust with their customers using these guardrails, knowing that the AI-generated content is monitored rigorously. This helps maintain compliance and enhances the overall user experience by ensuring that the chatbot consistently provides accurate and relevant information.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Guardrails for Amazon Bedrock.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Bedrock Agents** is incorrect. While useful for building and configuring autonomous agents that can interact with users and orchestrate actions, Amazon Bedrock Agents don’t specifically focus on ensuring the accuracy and regulatory compliance of the content generated by AI models. Their primary function is to facilitate the integration of foundation models with data sources and applications, rather than to impose guidelines or controls on the output of those models.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Comprehend** is incorrect because it is a natural language processing (NLP) service that primarily allows for text analysis, including sentiment analysis, entity recognition, and language detection. While Amazon Comprehend is valuable for understanding and extracting insights from text, it does not provide the functionality needed to enforce rules or guidelines on the content generated by AI models.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Lex** is incorrect. This service simply builds conversational interfaces, such as chatbots, that can interact with users through text and voice. While Amazon Lex enables the creation of chatbots and conversational experiences, it does not offer specific tools for controlling the accuracy, appropriateness, or regulatory compliance of the content generated by the AI models used within the company’s chatbots.
    
- Amazon Lex
- Amazon Bedrock Agents
- Amazon Comprehend

A logistics company wants to forecast delivery times based on traffic conditions, weather data, and route information. They need an ML algorithm that produces interpretable results with a clear breakdown of how each factor influences the predicted delivery time through a hierarchical decision-making process.

Which machine learning (ML) algorithm satisfies the company’s needs?

- **Decision Trees**
    
    **Decision trees** are a popular machine learning algorithm known for their interpretability and simplicity. They operate by recursively splitting the data based on features like traffic conditions, weather data, and route information. This creates a tree-like structure where each node represents a decision based on a specific feature. This hierarchical structure allows decision trees to clearly illustrate how different factors influence the outcome, making it easy to understand and interpret the predictions. The transparency of decision trees makes them an ideal choice for applications where it is essential to see the breakdown of decisions.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-decision-tree-09-04-2024.png)
    
    In addition to being interpretable, decision trees can handle both categorical and numerical data, making them flexible for various predictive tasks. They can also capture non-linear relationships between features, which can be crucial when multiple factors interact in complex ways like traffic and weather impacting delivery times. However, decision trees can be prone to overfitting, especially with complex datasets, but techniques like pruning or using ensemble methods (e.g., Random Forests) can mitigate this issue. Overall, decision trees balance simplicity, flexibility, and interpretability, making them a strong choice for forecasting problems that require clear explanations of model behavior.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Decision Trees.**
    
    The option that says: **K-Nearest Neighbors (KNN)** is incorrect because it is a non-parametric algorithm that makes predictions based on the similarity between instances. It simply finds the closest data points in the feature space. It makes predictions based on their values without clearly explaining how individual factors like traffic or weather influence the predicted delivery time.
    
    The option that says: **Linear Regression** is incorrect. Although this algorithm provides interpretable coefficients, it may not capture the complex interactions between traffic conditions, weather data, and route information, which are often non-linear. Additionally, linear regression does not provide a hierarchical decision-making process, which is what the company needs to understand how each factor affects the predicted delivery time in a step-by-step manner.
    
    The option that says: **Support Vector Machine (SVM)** is incorrect. This option only focuses on creating a decision boundary that maximizes the margin between classes (or predictions in regression) without showing how individual features contribute to the final prediction. This complexity makes it difficult to interpret how traffic, weather, and route conditions impact the predicted delivery time, which is a key requirement for the logistics company.
    
- Support Vector Machine (SVM)
- K-Nearest Neighbors (KNN)
- Linear Regression

A small e-commerce company wants to use machine learning to improve its churn prediction. However, the company does not have a dedicated data science team and is looking for a low-code or no-code solution to get started with machine learning.

Match the company’s requirements to the most suitable Amazon SageMaker feature. (Select THREE.)

- Start quickly with pre-built solutions and models to accelerate development
    - **Amazon SageMaker JumpStart**
    - Amazon SageMaker Canvas
    - Amazon SageMaker Clarify
    - Amazon SageMaker Studio Lab
- Prepare and transform data for machine learning models using an intuitive interface
    - Amazon SageMaker Canvas
    - Amazon SageMaker Model Monitor
    - **Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler**
    - Amazon SageMaker JumpStart
- Build ML models with no code by simply interacting with data and obtaining predictions
    - Amazon SageMaker JumpStart
    - Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler
    - Amazon SageMaker Clarify
    - **Amazon SageMaker Canvas**
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Amazon SageMaker JumpStart** provides an easy way to start with pre-built models and solutions, allowing the company to accelerate its machine-learning efforts without needing extensive expertise. JumpStart offers access to a wide variety of pre-trained models and end-to-end solutions that can be quickly deployed and customized, making it ideal for a company with limited data science resources.
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler** simplifies the process of preparing and transforming data for machine learning. It offers a visual interface for data wrangling, enabling users to clean, transform, and visualize data without writing complex code. This makes it a perfect match for the company’s requirement of a low-code solution.
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Canvas** is a no-code tool that enables users to build machine-learning models by simply interacting with data. It allows business analysts and non-technical users to generate accurate predictions without needing to write a single line of code, addressing the company’s need for a no-code solution to improve churn prediction.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD_Amazon_SageMaker_13Aug2024.png)
    
    These services are aligned with the company’s need for a low-code or no-code solution to improve churn prediction without requiring a dedicated data science team.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Start quickly with pre-built solutions and models to accelerate development: Amazon SageMaker JumpStart**
    
    **–** Prepare and transform data for machine learning models using an intuitive interface: **Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler**
    
    **– Build ML models with no code by simply interacting with data and obtaining predictions: Amazon SageMaker Canvas**

The scenario, however, focuses on finding a low-code or no-code solution for improving churn prediction, which is not the primary use case for Amazon SageMaker Clarify.

While valuable for maintaining model performance, it does not provide a low-code or no-code interface to build or deploy models, as required by the scenario.

Which solution would be the most suitable for this use case?

- Use Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth to label the data, and then apply Amazon SageMaker’s built-in algorithms to train a churn prediction model.

- Store the data in Amazon S3 and use Amazon SageMaker’s built-in algorithms to create churn prediction models.

On the other hand, Amazon Personalize is a service for building personalization and recommendation systems, which is unsuitable for a churn prediction use case.

It is primarily used for tasks like image and text annotation. Like the previous option, using SageMaker’s built-in algorithms directly may not be the most user-friendly approach for a company with limited machine-learning expertise.
    

A retail company must process several terabytes of historical sales data to forecast seasonal trends. Immediate access to predictions is not required.

Which Amazon SageMaker inference option will best meet these requirements?

- Model Parallelism and Large Model Inference
- Elastic Inference
- Serverless Inference
- **Batch Transform Inference**

    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-batch-transform-inference-04-09-2024.png)

Batch Transform Inference is an ideal choice for this use case as it allows processing large datasets asynchronously and in a cost-effective manner.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Batch Transform Inference.**
    
    The option that says: **Serverless Inference** is incorrect. While typically useful for intermittent and unpredictable workloads, it is more suited for real-time inference rather than batch processing of large datasets.
    
    The option that says: **Model Parallelism and Large Model Inference** is incorrect because this is primarily designed for training and inference on very large models that cannot fit into the memory of a single GPU. It is not optimized for batch processing of historical data.

A large e-commerce company is using Amazon Bedrock to develop a chatbot powered by a large language model (LLM) for customer support. The development team is evaluating the limits for integrating customer query data into a single prompt used by the model.

Which factor should the company consider?

- Temperature
- **Context window**
    
    A **context window** is the amount of text an AI model can handle and respond to at once; in most LLMs, this text is measured in tokens. This window defines the limit of how much input text the model can consider when generating its output. In most LLMs, such as GPT models, the input is measured in tokens rather than characters or words. Tokens can represent words, parts of words, or punctuation, and they are the basic units the model processes.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-Context-Windows-4Sept2024.jpg)
    
    The context window size determines how much information (e.g., words or phrases) can be contained in a single prompt. The broader the context window, the more information the model may use to generate outputs.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Context window.**
    
    The option that says: **Temperature** is incorrect because it only affects the variability or creativity of the model’s responses by adjusting the likelihood of different outputs. It does not influence the amount of data the model can process in a single interaction. Moreover, temperature settings are important for generating varied or more deterministic responses, but they don’t impact the data volume handled by the model.
    
    The option that says: **Response latency** is incorrect because it is simply concerned with the delay between the model receiving a prompt and delivering a response. While it’s important for ensuring a smooth user experience, it does not affect the size of the input that can be processed. The context window is the determinant factor for input size, not how quickly the model can respond.
    
    The option that says: **Model architecture** is incorrect. Although model architecture can influence the model’s performance, efficiency, and ability to process certain types of data, it does not specifically determine the amount of text that can be processed in a single interaction. The context window is directly responsible for this aspect.
    
- Model architecture
- Response latency

An AI specialist is developing an AI model trained and hosted in Amazon SageMaker. The specialist must choose a SageMaker inference option that provides immediate, low-latency predictions for a recommendation system and real-time responses to user queries

Which of the following options is the most suitable for the given requirements?

You can deploy your model to SageMaker hosting services and obtain an endpoint for performing inference. These endpoints are fully managed and support autoscaling, making them well-suited for applications like recommendation systems that need real-time responses to user queries. SageMaker offers fully managed endpoints for real-time inference, ensuring that the model can handle interactive and low-latency workloads.

    The option that says: **Asynchronous inference** is incorrect because it is only suitable for batch processing or scenarios where immediate responses are not required. It allows you to queue inference requests and process them as resources become available, which is not ideal for real-time, low-latency requirements.
