A company wants to customize a foundational model using Amazon Bedrock.

Select and order the prerequisites for customizing a foundational model. Each step should be selected one time. (Select and order THREE.)

- Prepare the training dataset
- Set the maximum tokens for model responses
- Purchase Provisioned Throughput
- Evaluate the results of model training
- Configure AWS KMS keys
- Create a fine-tuning or pre-training job
- **Answer**
    1. Prepare the training dataset
    2. Create a fine-tuning or pre-training job
    3. Purchase Provisioned Throughput
    - Model customization is the process of taking a large, pre-trained model and further training it on a smaller, specialized dataset related to your use case. This approach leverages the knowledge acquired during the initial pre-training phase while adapting the model to your requirements without losing the original capabilities.
        
        Amazon Bedrock currently provides the following customization methods:
        
        **Continued Pre-training**
        
        Provide unlabeled data to pre-train a foundation model by familiarizing it with certain types of inputs. You can provide data from specific topics in order to expose a model to those areas. The Continued Pre-training process will tweak the model parameters to accommodate the input data and improve domain knowledge.
        
        For example, you can train a model with private data, such as business documents, that are not publicly available for training large language models. Additionally, you can continue to improve the model by retraining the model with more unlabeled data as it becomes available.
        
        **Fine-tuning**
        
        Provide labeled data to train a model to improve performance on specific tasks. By providing a training dataset of labeled examples, the model learns to associate what types of outputs should be generated for certain types of inputs. The model parameters are adjusted in the process and the model’s performance is improved for the tasks represented by the training dataset.
        
        ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazon-bedrock-custom-model-console.jpg)
        
        To customize a foundational model using Amazon Bedrock, the prerequisites should be ordered as follows:
        
        **Prepare the training dataset**
        
        Before you can start customizing a model, you must prepare a training dataset to fine-tune or train the model according to your specific needs.
        
        **Create a fine-tuning or pre-training job**
        
        Depending on your training data and objectives, you might either fine-tune or pre-train the model. If fine-tuning, the training data is usually labeled and tailored to specific domains, helping to adapt the foundational model to your needs. Conversely, pre-training involves training the model on a broader, often unlabeled dataset to build foundational knowledge.
        
        **Purchase Provisioned Throughput**
        
        Once the custom model is created, you must purchase provisioned throughput to use it for inference.
        
        Hence, the correct answers are:
        
        **– Step 1: Prepare the training dataset**
        
        **– Step 2: Create a fine-tuning or pre-training job**
        
        **– Step 3: Purchase Provisioned Throughput**
        
        The option that says: **Set the maximum tokens for model responses** is incorrect. This step is related to configuring how the model responds to queries. It is usually done after the model has been fine-tuned and is ready for deployment.
        
        The option that says: **Evaluate the results of model training** is incorrect. Although this step is crucial for improving model performance and ensuring it meets the desired objectives, it’s not a required step for customizing a foundational model.
        
        The option that says: **Configure AWS KMS keys** is incorrect because this is just an optional step that you can do if you want model artifacts to be encrypted. While it’s something that you must consider for security purposes, it’s not a mandatory step for customizing a model itself.
        
    

A fintech company is developing an AI-based system for detecting fraudulent activities. The company must implement security measures to ensure data integrity, safeguard user privacy, and comply with regulations. The goal is to align security strategies with relevant foundational security capabilities.

Match each foundational security capability with its relevant strategy for securing the AI-powered fraud detection system. Each capability should be selected once only. (Select THREE.)

- It ensures that visibility, secure access, and control over the data used for AI development and implementation are maintained.
    - Application security
    - Infrastructure protection
    - **Data protection**
    - Threat detection
- Implement real-time monitoring and anomaly detection tools to identify and respond to potential security incidents in AI workloads.
    - **Threat detection**
    - Data protection
    - Infrastructure protection
    - Application security
- It ensure that vulnerabilities are identified and addressed throughout the software development lifecycle for AI workloads.
    - **Threat detection**
    - **Data protection**
    - **Infrastructure protection**
    - **Application security**
- **Evaluation**
    
    Effective security practices are essential for protecting AI systems from various threats, ensuring they operate securely, and complying with relevant regulations. Organizations can instill confidence in their AI solutions by aligning strategies with foundational security capabilities, thereby effectively safeguarding their systems.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-foundational-AI-08-20-2024.png)
    
    **Data protection** involves safeguarding sensitive information from unauthorized access and breaches to ensure data remains confidential and intact. This strategy focuses on implementing strong data control measures, including encryption, access restrictions, and monitoring to prevent unauthorized access. Organizations can avoid data breaches and maintain data integrity by ensuring that only authorized individuals can view or modify data and by protecting data both in transit and at rest.
    
    **Threat detection** involves identifying and addressing potential security threats in real-time, which is crucial for responding quickly to possible security issues and minimizing their impact. This approach requires setting up tools and systems to monitor AI applications for unusual or suspicious activities continuously. Organizations can promptly investigate and address potential security threats by detecting anomalies and issuing alerts before they cause significant harm.
    
    **Application security** involves identifying and addressing vulnerabilities within software applications to prevent exploitation by attackers and ensure the security of applications. This strategy includes integrating security practices throughout the development process, such as conducting regular code reviews, security testing, and vulnerability assessments. Addressing security issues early in the development process helps organizations reduce the risk of vulnerabilities being exploited after the application is deployed.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **–** It ensures that visibility, secure access, and control over the data used for AI development and implementation are maintained: **Data protection**
    
    **–** Implement real-time monitoring and anomaly detection tools to identify and respond to potential security incidents in AI workloads: **Threat detection**
    
    **–** It ensures that vulnerabilities are identified and addressed throughout the software development lifecycle for AI workloads: **Application security**
    
    The option that says: **Infrastructure protection** is incorrect. This foundational capability primarily worked on securing the systems and services used to operate AI workloads. This includes securing endpoints, applying network and storage security controls, and using encryption for data in transit. However, while this practice is important for maintaining the overall security of systems and services, it does not directly align with the specific strategies outlined for the foundational security capabilities.
    

A startup is developing a new AI-driven application using Amazon Bedrock. Due to unpredictable usage patterns, they want to minimize upfront costs and only pay for the resources they consume.

What is the MOST suitable pricing model in Amazon Bedrock to meet the company’s needs?

- Model evaluation
- Provisioned Throughput
- **On-Demand**
    
    **Amazon Bedrock** is a fully managed service that simplifies the process of building and scaling generative AI applications for organizations. It utilizes pre-trained foundational models from different AI providers, allowing developers to access them via an API and integrate them into their applications without handling the underlying infrastructure. The service supports models for text generation, image creation, and other generative AI tasks, making it a versatile tool for developing AI-driven applications across various industries.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-bedrock-09-05-2024.png)
    
    Amazon Bedrock offers flexible pricing models, including On-Demand, Batch, Provisioned Throughput, Model customization, and Model evaluation. On-demand allows customers to only pay for the computing resources they use. This is perfect for applications with unpredictable workloads. The flexibility of this pricing model helps businesses, such as startups, to reduce upfront costs while scaling their AI capabilities based on real-time needs. By offering a pay-as-you-go model, Bedrock allows companies to focus on innovation and application development without worrying about resource management.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **On-Demand.**
    
    The option that says: **Batch** is incorrect. This pricing model is intended only for making a large number of predictions at the same time. In this model, you provide a set of prompts in a single input file, and you receive the results in an output file. Although this mode is cost-effective (offering a 50% lower price than On-Demand), it is better suited for batch processing rather than handling unpredictable, real-time workloads.
    
    The option that says: **Model evaluation** is incorrect because this model is primarily for testing and assessing the performance of machine learning models. It charges based on the inference from the model during the evaluation process and includes options for human-based evaluation. This pricing model is focused on assessment tasks rather than operational workloads.
    
    The option that says: **Provisioned Throughput** is incorrect because this pricing model is designed for large, consistent inference workloads where guaranteed throughput is required. It involves purchasing model units with commitment terms of 1 or 6 months, making it less flexible for applications with unpredictable usage. Since the startup wants to avoid upfront commitments and only pay for what it consumes, Provisioned Throughput does not meet its requirements.
    
- Batch

A streaming service wants to analyze its user data to improve content recommendations. The data science team needs to group users with similar viewing habits, identify common patterns in the types of shows watched together, and estimate how users’ preferences are spread across different genres.

Match each unsupervised learning approach the data science team must consider with its correct objective. (Select THREE.)

- To estimate how users’ preferences are spread across different genres.
    - Neural network
    - **Probability density**
    - Linear regression
    - Decision tree
- To identify common patterns in the types of shows watched together.
    - **Association rule learning**
    - Linear regression
    - Decision tree
    - Logistic regression
- To group users with similar viewing habits.
    - Association rule learning
    - Probability density
    - **Clustering**
    - Dimensionality reduction
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Unsupervised learning** is a kind of machine learning in which the algorithm is developed on data without labeled results. Unsupervised learning, as opposed to supervised learning, which requires the model to be given both input data and output labels, works with data that only contains input features. Unsupervised learning seeks to find underlying patterns, structures, or relationships in data without explicit instruction on the desired result.
    
    Machine learning is teaching a computer to make predictions or judgments. To train a model, you first need an algorithm and some example data. Then, you integrate your model into your application to generate real-time and scalable insights. Supervised and unsupervised learning are two distinct types of algorithms.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD_supervised-vs-unsupervised-learning_13Aug2024.jpg)
    
    In unsupervised learning, the model is tasked with exploring the data and finding hidden structures within it. This can involve grouping similar data points together (clustering), discovering associations between different features (association rule learning), or estimating the probability distribution of the data (probability density estimation).
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    – To group users with similar viewing habits: **Clustering**
    
    **–** To identify common patterns in the types of shows watched together: **Association rule learning**
    
    – To estimate how users’ preferences are spread across different genres: **Probability density**
    
    The option that says: **Dimensionality reduction** is incorrect because, while it is a valuable technique for simply reducing the number of features or variables in a dataset, it does not directly achieve the specific objectives mentioned in the scenario.
    
    The option that says: **Neural network** is incorrect because it is just a supervised learning method that requires labeled data to train a model, which does not align with the unsupervised nature of the tasks in the scenario.
    
    The option that says: **Decision tree** is incorrect because this option is primarily used in supervised learning for classification and regression tasks, making them unsuitable for the unsupervised learning objectives outlined in the scenario.
    
    The option that says: **Linear regression** is incorrect because it is a supervised learning technique only used to model relationships between input features and a continuous output variable, which does not fit the unsupervised learning tasks described.
    
    The option that says: **Logistic regression** is incorrect because it is another supervised learning algorithm, typically used for binary classification, and is not applicable for the unsupervised tasks mentioned in the scenario.
    

Which machine learning model is known for producing artificial data by learning from existing examples?

- **Generative adversarial network (GAN)**
    
    Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) are a type of machine learning model designed to generate new data by learning from an existing dataset. GANs consist of two neural networks, the generator, and the discriminator, that work together in a competitive process. The generator creates synthetic data samples resembling the original training data, while the discriminator tries to distinguish between real and fake samples. As the two networks compete, the generator improves its ability to create realistic data, and the discriminator becomes better at identifying fake data. This adversarial training allows GANs to generate highly realistic data, such as images, audio, or text.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-GAN-09-05-2024.png)
    
    Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) have found applications in various fields, including image generation, video synthesis, data augmentation, and more. For example, GANs have been used in fields like computer vision to generate new images for training data, enhance image quality, and even create art. Their ability to learn from existing data and produce new, realistic samples makes GANs a powerful tool for data generation tasks. However, GANs can be computationally expensive to train and may be difficult to stabilize during the training process.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Generative adversarial network (GAN).**
    
    The option that says: **Recurrent neural network (RNN)** is incorrect because it is primarily used for tasks that involve sequential or time-series data, such as speech recognition, language modeling, and time series forecasting. RNNs are effective for learning temporal patterns in data but are not designed to generate new data based on an adversarial process.
    
    The option that says: **Convolutional neural networks (CNN)** is incorrect because it is only specialized for processing structured grid-like data, such as images. CNNs are primarily used in tasks like image classification, object detection, and facial recognition, where the model needs to learn spatial hierarchies of features. CNNs do not generate new data but extract important features from existing data.
    
    The option that says: **Reinforcement learning** is incorrect because it focuses on decision-making in environments where an agent takes actions to maximize cumulative rewards. Reinforcement learning algorithms are not designed to create new data but to improve an agent’s performance in a given environment through trial and error.
    
- Reinforcement learning
- Recurrent neural network (RNN)
- Convolutional neural networks (CNN)

A tech company has trained a model to classify products in a manufacturing line as defective or non-defective. To assess the model’s performance on unseen data, the team requires a solution that provides insights into its accuracy and ability to differentiate between the two categories.

Which tools or metrics should be used?

- Precision
- **Confusion Matrix**
    
    A **Confusion Matrix** is a table that summarizes the performance of a classification model by comparing the predicted labels with the true labels. It provides a detailed breakdown of the model’s predictions, including true positives (TP), true negatives (TN), false positives (FP), and false negatives (FN). The confusion matrix is particularly useful for understanding the types of errors the model makes and can help identify potential biases or imbalances in the data.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-confusion-matrix-table-example-05-09-2024.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Confusion Matrix.**
    
    The option that says: **F1 Score** is incorrect because it typically combines precision and recall but does not provide a detailed breakdown of model performance across different classes.
    
    The option that says: **MSE (Mean Squared Error)**is incorrect because it is primarily used for regression problems and does not apply to classification tasks.
    
    The option that says: **Precision** is incorrect because it only measures the accuracy of positive predictions without offering a detailed breakdown of performance across all classes.
    
- F1 Score
- MSE (Mean Squared Error)

An AI practitioner evaluates the cost implications of using Amazon Bedrock to perform inferences with an LLM. The practitioner must determine the primary cost driver for inference operations.

Which of the following factors directly influence the cost of inference when using Amazon Bedrock?

- Model architecture complexity
- Number of training epochs completed
- Batch size used during training
- **Number of tokens processed during inference**
    
    LLMs operate on tokenized text, where the input and output are broken down into smaller units called **tokens**. Tokens can be words, subwords, or individual characters, depending on the tokenization method.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-bedrock-2-July-2024.png)
    
    Amazon Bedrock is a service that allows users to build and scale machine learning models, including large language models (LLMs). Regarding inference operations, the primary cost driver is related to the operational aspect of processing data through the model, rather than factors tied to training.
    
    The number of tokens processed during inference directly impacts the cost because pricing in services like Amazon Bedrock is often based on the amount of data (tokens) being processed. More tokens lead to higher computational requirements and, therefore, higher costs.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Number of tokens processed during inference.**
    
    The option that says: **Batch size used during training** is incorrect because it only pertains to the training phase rather than inference. Training costs are influenced by batch size but not inference costs.
    
    The option that says: **Model architecture complexity** is incorrect because while complex architectures can impact training and inference performance, they do not directly drive the cost like the volume of data (tokens) processed does.
    
    The option that says: **Number of training epochs completed** is incorrect as it only affects the training process, not inference. Inference costs are independent of the number of epochs used during training.
    

Which hyperparameter defines the number of times the model will go through the entire training dataset?

- `learningRateWarmupSteps`
- **`epochCount`**
- `learningRate`
- `batchSize`
- **Answer**
    - **`epochCount`**
    - **Hyperparameters** are configuration settings that are set before the training process begins and control the behavior of the machine learning algorithm. These settings are not learned from the data but are tuned by the developer or data scientist to optimize the model’s performance.
        
        An **epoch** is a single pass through the entire training dataset. During each epoch, the model sees the entire dataset once and updates its internal parameters (weights and biases) based on the errors it makes on the training data.
        
        The **`epochCount`** hyperparameter defines the number of times the model will go through the entire training dataset during the training process.
        
        ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-epoch-Count-graph-05-09-2024.png)
        
        In machine learning, the training process involves iteratively updating the model’s parameters to minimize a loss function (a measure of the model’s error) on the training data. The number of epochs determines how often the model will see the entire training dataset during the training process.
        
        A higher number of epochs generally leads to better model performance, as the model has more opportunities to learn from the data. However, training for too many epochs can also lead to overfitting, where the model memorizes the training data too well and fails to generalize to new, unseen data.
        
        Hence, the correct answer is: **`epochCount`**
        
        The option that says: **`learningRateWarmupSteps`** is incorrect because this typically defines the number of steps where the learning rate gradually increases before stabilizing, not the number of passes through the dataset.
        
        The option that says: **`learningRate`** is incorrect because the learning rate only determines how much to adjust the model’s weights in response to errors but does not define how often the dataset is processed.
        
        The option that says **`batchSize`** is incorrect. The batch size primarily defines how many training examples are processed in one iteration but does not indicate how many complete passes are made over the entire dataset.
        

Which metric evaluates the responsiveness of a machine learning model in real-time applications?

- Model accuracy
- Memory utilization during inference
- Customer satisfaction score (CSAT)
- **Average response time**
    
    **Average response time** is simply the time it takes for a model to process an input and give an output. It’s a key factor when determining how well an AI model works in real-time scenarios. Suppose a model has a short average response time. In that case, it can handle requests quickly, making it more efficient and responsive in environments where speed matters, like live applications or services.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-Average-Response-Time-5Sept2024.jpg)
    
    Average response time is the correct answer, as it measures the speed at which an AI model can process a request and return a result during deployment. This metric is essential for ensuring that real-time applications run efficiently and meet low-latency requirements.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Average response time.**
    
    The option that says: **Customer satisfaction score (CSAT)** is incorrect because it only measures user satisfaction after interacting with a service or product. It evaluates customer experiences, not the operational or runtime efficiency of an AI model.
    
    The option that says: **Memory utilization during inference** is incorrect because it simply tracks how much memory a model uses while making predictions. It doesn’t directly measure how quickly the model produces a result. Reducing memory usage might help improve speed, but the metric focuses on hardware performance, not the runtime efficiency of the model.
    
    The option that says: **Model accuracy** is incorrect because it typically assesses how well the model makes predictions compared to actual outcomes, not how fast it produces predictions. Therefore, it doesn’t measure the efficiency of real-time predictions or how quickly the model operates in production environments.
    

A financial services company needs to analyze customer feedback from survey responses to understand the overall sentiment. The solution must automatically detect positive, negative, or neutral sentiments in the text data.

What are the most suitable AWS services for this solution? (Select TWO.)

- Amazon Polly
- **Amazon Bedrock**
- **Amazon Comprehend**
- Amazon Textract
- Amazon Translate
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Amazon Comprehend** is an AWS service that uses machine learning to perform natural language processing (NLP) tasks, including sentiment analysis. It is specifically designed to analyze text and understand the sentiment behind it, whether it’s positive, negative, neutral, or mixed. In this scenario, where the goal is to analyze customer feedback from survey responses, Amazon Comprehend is perfectly suited because it can automatically detect and categorize sentiment in text data. This makes it an ideal solution for the company’s requirement to understand customer sentiments.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD_Amazon-Comprehend-5Sept2024.jpg)
    
    **Amazon Bedrock** is a fully managed service that provides access to foundational AI models for natural language processing (NLP), including sentiment analysis. It simplifies the process of integrating AI into applications by offering pre-trained models capable of understanding text sentiment. Bedrock helps with tasks such as identifying emotions and tone in customer feedback, making it an excellent choice for analyzing survey responses to detect positive, negative, or neutral sentiments.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD_Amazon-Bedrock-5Sept2024.jpg)
    
    The most suitable for analyzing customer feedback and automatically detecting sentiment in text data are **Amazon Comprehend** and **Amazon Bedrock**. **Amazon Comprehend** is a natural language processing (NLP) service that uses machine learning to identify sentiment (positive, negative, neutral) in text, making it ideal for sentiment analysis. **Amazon Bedrock** provides foundation models that can be used for NLP tasks, including sentiment analysis, making it complementary to Amazon Comprehend in advanced text processing scenarios.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Amazon Comprehend**
    
    **– Amazon Bedrock**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Textract** is incorrect because it is primarily designed to automatically extract text and data from scanned documents and images, such as PDFs or images containing forms and tables. However, it cannot perform sentiment analysis or understand the context of the extracted text.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Polly** is incorrect because this service is simply a text-to-speech service that turns written content into spoken word. While it’s useful for creating speech-enabled applications, it doesn’t offer any features for sentiment analysis or text interpretation given in the scenario.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Translate** is incorrect because this option is typically designed to translate text between different languages in real time. It is useful for translating customer feedback into multiple languages but does not provide any sentiment analysis features. Therefore, it does not meet the requirement of detecting whether feedback is positive, negative, or neutral.
    

A technology company is working on multiple cutting-edge projects that require various machine learning models to solve distinct challenges. These projects cover areas such as natural language processing, customer behavior prediction, and content creation for marketing. The team must decide which type of machine learning model best fits each specific project.

For each of the following use cases, choose the most appropriate machine learning model type from the list provided. Some model types may be used more than once. ( Select FOUR.)

- Translate patents from English to French, including embedded images like technical diagrams.
    - Traditional ML model
    - **Generative AI model**
- Predict customer subscription cancellations for a telecom provider based on historical usage data.
    - **Traditional ML model**
    - **Generative AI model**
- Create innovative visual designs from text-based advertising briefs.
    - **Generative AI model**
    - **Traditional ML model**
- Identify the sentiment behind customer feedback and social media posts.
    - **Generative AI model**
    - **Traditional ML model**
- **Evaluation**

These models often require human intervention for feature engineering and are ideal for tasks such as regression, classification, and grouping. Traditional machine learning models include linear regression, decision trees, and logistic regression.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-Traditional-ML-&-Generative-AI-Models_19Aug2024.jpg)
    
    On the other hand, **Generative AI** models, including Generative Adversarial Networks (GANs) and large-scale language models, focus on creating new content such as images, text, or even entire datasets. Which Amazon EC2 instance provides high-performance instances explicitly optimized for machine learning model training, utilizing custom-built chips for cost-efficient deep learning?

- Amazon EC2 G5 Instances
- Amazon EC2 P4 Instances
- **Amazon EC2 Trn1 Instances**
    
    Amazon EC2 Trn1 Instances are specifically designed for high-performance machine learning (ML) model training and utilize AWS Trainium chips. Trainium is a custom-built silicon by AWS optimized for deep learning workloads, making it cost-efficient and scalable for training large and complex models. These instances support major ML frameworks such as PyTorch, TensorFlow, and Apache MXNet, making them versatile for ML practitioners. The Trn1 instances offer high network throughput and fast interconnects, which improve the efficiency of distributed training workloads and reduce overall training time.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-ec2-trn1-09-06-2024.png)
    
    By using Trn1 instances, organizations can achieve lower cost-per-inference and higher energy efficiency. This significantly improves the speed and economics of training deep learning models. Trn1 instances are available in multiple sizes to fit various training workloads and offer integration with Amazon SageMaker to simplify ML development and deployment. Enterprises can benefit from greater performance for their machine learning needs while reducing training costs, especially for tasks like natural language processing, computer vision, and recommendation systems.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon EC2 Trn1 Instances.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon EC2 P4 Instances** is incorrect. Although this instance is GPU-based and optimized for high-performance machine learning model training, it uses NVIDIA A100 Tensor Core GPUs, which are expensive for deep learning model training compared to the custom-built Trainium chips used in Trn1 instances.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon EC2 M6g Instances**is incorrect because it’s just general-purpose instances that use AWS Graviton2 processors. While these instances are ideal for a wide range of workloads, including web servers, microservices, and databases, they are not optimized for machine learning model training, particularly for deep learning, which requires more specialized hardware like GPUs or custom silicon such as Trainium.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon EC2 G5 Instances**is incorrect because it is designed for graphics-intensive applications like video rendering, gaming, and AI inference tasks. While they can handle machine learning inference workloads, they are not explicitly optimized for deep learning model training, as the question asks. G5 instances use NVIDIA GPUs, but for cost-efficient training at scale, the Trainium-powered Trn1 instances are the better choice.
    
- Amazon EC2 M6g Instances

An AI Specialist is tasked with developing an AI model that generates SQL queries from natural language descriptions. The specialist needs a model that understands context and produces coherent, human-like text.

Which of the following AI models would be best suited for this task?

- **Generative Pre-trained Transformer**
    
    The **Generative Pre-trained Transformer** is a language model that understands and generates natural language. It is based on the Transformer architecture, which enables it to process large volumes of text and comprehend the contextual relationships between words, sentences, and paragraphs. GPT models are pre-trained on diverse datasets and can produce coherent and contextually accurate text when tailored for specific tasks. This capability makes GPT ideal for AI applications requiring human-like text generation, such as customer service chatbots, automated content generation, and more.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD_GPT-6Sept2024.png)

SageMaker equips developers with the necessary tools to construct, train, and implement machine learning models at a large scale. Organizations can utilize SageMaker to customize GPT models for tasks like generating SQL queries or other text-based applications. SageMaker offers managed infrastructure, model optimization, and deployment options, empowering AI specialists to concentrate on application logic without concerning themselves with the underlying infrastructure. This seamless integration allows efficient utilization of GPT models in real-world applications, ensuring that AI-generated text is both coherent and contextually accurate.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Generative Pre-trained Transformer.**
    
    The option that says: **Convolutional Neural Network** is incorrect because it is primarily designed for processing grid-like data such as images and videos. It excels at tasks like image recognition, object detection, and image classification but is unsuitable for natural language understanding or generation tasks.
    
    The option that says: **Recurrent Neural Network** is incorrect because it is only used for sequence-based tasks such as time series data, speech recognition, and some forms of natural language processing (NLP). It is not as effective as Transformer-based models like GPT. RNNs struggle with capturing long-range dependencies and context in text, which is critical when generating coherent and contextually accurate SQL queries.
    
    The option that says: **K-Nearest Neighbors** is incorrect. This option is simply a machine learning algorithm used for classification and regression tasks. KNN finds the most similar data points (neighbors) to make predictions but cannot understand or generate language.

- K-Nearest Neighbors
- Recurrent Neural Network

Which of the following can Amazon SageMaker Clarify perform? (Select TWO.)

- **Detect bias in pre-training data**
- Perform automatic hyperparameter optimization to improve model accuracy
- Perform data augmentation to increase the size of the training dataset
- **Identify bias in post-training data during or after model training**
- Generate synthetic data to improve model training
- **Evaluation**
    
    Machine learning (ML) models play a significant role in decision-making across various domains, such as financial services, healthcare, education, and human resources. There is growing concern among policymakers, regulators, and advocates regarding the ethical and policy challenges presented by ML and data-driven systems. **Amazon SageMaker Clarify** offers tools to help comprehend the specific reasoning behind a model’s prediction and to identify and address any bias during the model’s training or inference. Additionally, SageMaker Clarify can assist in constructing more transparent and less biased machine learning models. Furthermore, it can generate model governance reports for risk and compliance teams as well as external regulators.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/sagemaker-clarify-08-27-24.png)
    
    With SageMaker Clarify, you can:
    
    **– Detect and Explain Bias in Model Predictions**: SageMaker Clarify detects bias during data preparation, after model training, and in deployed models. It identifies bias and provides explainability insights into how the model makes predictions.
    
    **– Identify Bias in Pre-training Data**: Assess the training data for potential bias before model training. This helps ensure that any inherent biases in the data are understood and addressed.
    
    **– Monitor Post-training Bias**: Continuously monitor the model’s behavior in production. Amazon SageMaker Clarify can help identify biases that may emerge during training or when the model is deployed.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Detect bias in pre-training data**
    
    **– Identify bias in post-training data during or after model training**
    
    The option that says: **Perform automatic hyperparameter optimization to improve model accuracy** is incorrect because this is typically handled by Amazon SageMaker Automatic Model Tuning, not Clarify.
    
    The option that says: **Generate synthetic data to improve model training** is incorrect because SageMaker Clarify does not generate synthetic data for model training. Its purpose is to only analyze existing data and model outputs for bias, not to create new data.
    
    The option that says: **Perform data augmentation to increase the size of the training dataset** is incorrect. Amazon SageMaker Clarify does not perform data augmentation or increase the size of training datasets. Its primary purpose is to detect and explain bias in machine learning models and datasets.
    

A tech company needs its LLM (Large Language Model) to generate concise responses in a specific language for a virtual assistant.

What is the best way to ensure the LLM produces concise responses in the desired language?

- **Modify the instruction prompt.**
    
    **Prompt engineering** is the process of designing and crafting prompts (input text) to guide language models to generate desired outputs. Some of the Prompt Engineering Techniques are:
    
    **Few-shot prompting** is a technique that involves providing a language model with contextual examples to guide its understanding and expected output for a specific task. This is technique is particularly helpful for models with limited training data or when adapting the model to new domains or tasks.
    
    **Chain-of-thought (CoT) prompting** is a technique that divides intricate reasoning tasks into smaller intermediary steps. This technique can help the model reason through the task in a structured manner and improve its ability to handle complex tasks.
    
    **Zero-shot prompting** is a technique where a user presents a task to a generative model without providing any examples or explicit training for that specific task. Instead, it relies solely on its general knowledge acquired during pre-training to perform the task based on the prompt.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-prompt-engineerin-12-Aug-2024.png)
    
    In this scenario, modifying the instruction prompt aligns with the principle of prompt engineering by allowing direct control over the LLM’s output. Crafting a precise prompt ensures that the responses are both concise and in the desired language, addressing the company’s requirements effectively.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Modify the instruction prompt.**
    
    The option that says: **Opt for a model with more parameters** is incorrect because Larger models with more parameters typically have increased capacity for understanding and generating text but do not inherently produce more concise responses.
    
    The option that says: **Increase the top-p (nucleus) sampling parameter** is incorrect because this parameter only affects the diversity of the generated text by considering the cumulative probability of the top tokens. While adjusting this can affect creativity and coherence, it does not specifically target conciseness.
    
    The option that says: **Adjust the frequency penalty parameter** is incorrect because increasing the frequency penalty can discourage the model from repeating the same words or phrases too often, but it does not specifically address ensuring concise and language-specific responses.
    
- Increase the top-p (nucleus) sampling parameter.
- Adjust the frequency penalty parameter.
- Opt for a model with more parameters.

An AI practitioner uses Amazon Bedrock for daily Natural language processing (NLP) tasks, leveraging few-shot prompting with 20 examples in the current prompt. The practitioner wants to cut monthly costs.

Which action will effectively reduce the monthly costs?

- Add more examples to increase token usage.
- Decrease the batch size for processing prompts.
- **Reduce the number of tokens used.**
    
    **Amazon Bedrock** is a service that provides access to foundation models for various NLP tasks. Several factors can influence the cost when using foundation models for NLP tasks, including the number of tokens processed. **Tokens** are the units of text (words or characters) that the model processes and higher token usage generally results in higher costs.
    
    **Few-shot prompting** is used in NLP, where a model is trained on a small number of examples (typically less than 100) to perform a specific task.
    
    Here is an example of reducing the number of tokens:
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-token-reduction-example-09-09-2024.png)
    
    Reducing the number of tokens used in the prompts and responses can help lower costs as it directly impacts the billing, which often scales with token usage. Therefore, optimizing the length of the prompt and the responses to reduce token consumption is an effective strategy for cost reduction.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Reduce the number of tokens used.**
    
    The option that says: **Decrease the batch size for processing prompts** is incorrect. This might not lead to significant cost savings and could increase costs per prompt if it leads to more requests.
    
    The option that says: **Add more examples to increase token usage** is incorrect because increasing the number of examples directly increases the number of tokens, leading to higher costs rather than reducing them.
    
    The option that says: **Use Provisioned Throughput** is incorrect. This might result in higher costs due to guaranteed performance levels. It’s more suited for high-demand workloads, making it less effective for reducing monthly expenses.
    
- Use Provisioned Throughput.

A team is working with a Large Language Model (LLM) to generate responses for customer support queries. However, it is observed that the model occasionally provides answers that are factually incorrect or nonsensical.

What issue is the model experiencing?

- Underfitting
- Data augmentation
- **Hallucination**
    
    **Hallucination** in AI refers to when the model generates information that is not grounded in the training data or factual context. This can lead to factually incorrect or nonsensical answers. It’s like the model is “imagining” details that aren’t true or relevant to the question.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/prevent-hallucination-amazon-bedrock-guardrails-090924.png)
    
    You can use Guardrails for Amazon Bedrock to address and manage hallucinations in generative AI models. This feature includes a Contextual Grounding Check that helps detect and filter out hallucinated responses.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Hallucination.**
    
    The option that says: **Underfitting** is incorrect because it occurs when a model is too simple to capture the underlying patterns in the data, leading to poor performance on both training and test data. This would typically result in consistently poor predictions, not necessarily generating incorrect or nonsensical answers.
    
    The option that says: **Model drift** is incorrect. This phenomenon refers to the degradation of a model’s performance over time due to changes in the underlying data distribution. While this can cause a model to become less accurate, it doesn’t typically explain the generation of nonsensical or incorrect responses directly.
    
    The option that says: **Data augmentation** is incorrect because it primarily pertains to creating new training examples by applying transformations to the existing data. This technique is used to improve model performance and generalization. It is not related to the issue of generating incorrect or nonsensical answers.
    
- Model drift

Match the appropriate AWS service or feature from the given options to each requirement related to building and securing a generative AI application using Amazon Bedrock. Each option can be used once, more than once, or not at all. (Select THREE)

- Implement role-based access control for your AI application resources.
    - AWS Key Management Service (KMS)
    - AWS CloudTrail
    - Amazon Bedrock Content Guardrails
    - **AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM)**
- Define policies to prevent the generation of explicit or offensive content.
    - AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM)
    - **Amazon Bedrock Content Guardrails.**
    - AWS CloudTrail
    - AWS Key Management Service (KMS)
- Encrypt data used for training and inference in your AI models
    - **AWS Key Management Service (KMS)**
    - Amazon Bedrock Content Guardrails
    - AWS CloudTrail
    - AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM)

IAM roles and policies can be defined to control who can access and modify AI application resources, ensuring secure and appropriate access.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-ml-AI-IAM-14-Aug-2024.png)
    
    **Amazon Bedrock Content Guardrails**
    
    is a feature specifically designed to define policies that prevent the generation of explicit or offensive content. It helps in setting up guardrails to ensure that the content produced by the generative AI models adheres to the desired content guidelines and standards.

    **AWS Key Management Service (KMS)**
    
    provides encryption for data used in training and inference processes. By using KMS, you can manage the encryption keys that protect your data, ensuring that data at rest and in transit is secure and compliant with encryption standards.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-ai-using-kms-14-Aug-2024.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    – Implement role-based access control for your AI application resources: **AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM)**
    
    – Define policies to prevent the generation of explicit or offensive content: **Amazon Bedrock Content Guardrails**
    
    – Encrypt data used for training and inference in your AI models: **AWS Key Management Service (KMS)**

This document explains data, extracts key insights and visuals, and recommends the best actions to improve your business.
    
    ![Amazon Q and QuickSight](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazonq-quicksight-090924.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon Q in Amazon QuickSight.**
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Q Developer in AWS Chatbot** is incorrect. AWS Chatbot is a service that typically integrates with Slack to provide notifications and run commands. While it can be useful for operational tasks and alerts, it is not designed to generate visualizations or handle natural language queries for business analytics.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Q in Amazon Redshift** is incorrect. Amazon Redshift is a data warehousing service that allows for fast querying and analysis of large datasets. However, it does not natively support Amazon Q for natural language queries and visualization. Redshift is more suited for complex queries and data processing rather than direct interaction through natural language.
    
    The option that says: **Amazon Q in AWS Glue** is incorrect. AWS Glue is a fully managed ETL (extract, transform, load) service that primarily prepares data for analytics. It does not include Amazon Q for natural language querying and visualization. Glue is used for data preparation and transformation rather than direct querying and visualization.
    
- Amazon Q in AWS Glue
- Amazon Q Developer in AWS Chatbot
- Amazon Q in Amazon Redshift

Which machine learning approach is used to classify and organize unlabeled data by identifying hidden patterns without requiring predefined categories or labels?

- Transfer Learning
- Reinforcement Learning
- **Unsupervised Learning**
    
    **Unsupervised learning** is a machine learning technique used to analyze and cluster unlabeled data. Unlike supervised learning, where models are trained with labeled datasets, unsupervised learning algorithms operate without any predefined categories or labels. These algorithms discover hidden patterns, structures, and relationships in the data. One of the most common applications of unsupervised learning is clustering, where data points with similar characteristics are grouped. Another use case includes dimensionality reduction, which simplifies data by reducing the number of variables while maintaining its important features. This is highly effective when handling large datasets.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-unsupervised-learning-09-10-2024.png)
    
    Amazon SageMaker supports unsupervised learning for various tasks, such as clustering, anomaly detection, and association rule learning. Amazon SageMaker provides pre-built algorithms such as K-Means and Principal Component Analysis (PCA) that can be used to analyze unlabeled datasets. By utilizing SageMaker’s built-in unsupervised learning capabilities, users can quickly build and deploy models for tasks like customer segmentation, recommendation engines, or detecting anomalies in system logs. SageMaker streamlines the entire machine learning workflow, from data preparation to deployment, making it easier to leverage unsupervised learning for real-world business needs.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Unsupervised Learning.**
    
    The option that says: **Transfer Learning** is incorrect because this type of learning uses a pre-trained model from one task or domain and applies it to a different but related task. Transfer learning is typically used when there is a shortage of labeled data in the target domain but an abundance of labeled data in a related domain. This approach is not designed for working with unlabeled data or discovering hidden patterns, making it unsuitable for the scenario described.
    
    The option that says: **Reinforcement Learning** is incorrect because this primarily focuses on learning through an agent’s interactions with an environment. In reinforcement learning, the agent receives feedback in the form of rewards or penalties based on its actions and learns to maximize cumulative rewards over time. This approach is not meant for classifying or grouping unlabeled data, but rather for optimizing decision-making processes in dynamic environments.
    
    The option that says: **Few-shot Learning** is incorrect because it involves training models with a very limited amount of labeled data, often just a few examples. Few-shot learning is particularly useful when labeled data is scarce, but it still requires some level of labeled data to function. It does not apply to situations with no labeled data, as is the case with unsupervised learning.
    
- Few-shot Learning

Which foundation model (FM) type best suits this recommendation system?

- Natural language processing (NLP) mode
- Image embedding model

By embedding visual and textual inputs into a shared representation space, the model can uncover correlations and relationships between different data types. For example, an e-commerce platform can analyze product images and descriptions to create more accurate recommendations. Multi-modal models are essential in use cases where visual and linguistic content must be processed and understood in combination.

Amazon SageMaker provides the necessary infrastructure to train and deploy such models, allowing businesses to harness the power of multi-modal embeddings for their AI and machine learning applications. With the ability to handle large-scale data across different formats, multi-modal models offer a more comprehensive approach to generating insights than single-modal models focusing on text or images alone.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Multi-modal embedding model.**
    
    The option that says: **Natural language processing (NLP) model** is incorrect because it only focuses on processing and understanding text-based inputs, such as language parsing, sentiment analysis, and entity recognition. While useful for handling product descriptions, an NLP model would not be able to process image data, which is a key requirement in the given scenario.

An AI practitioner fine-tunes an FM (Foundation Model) to achieve higher accuracy and meet a certain acceptance level. The practitioner is considering several adjustments to the training process.

Which approach will be MOST effective in achieving this goal?

- Decrease the `epochCount`
- Increase the `learningRateWarmupSteps`
- Reduce the model size
- **Increase the `epochCount`**
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Hyperparameters** are configuration settings that are set before the training process begins and control the behavior of the machine learning algorithm. These settings are not learned from the data but are tuned by the developer or data scientist to optimize the model’s performance.
    
    An **epoch** is a single pass through the entire training dataset. During each epoch, the model sees the entire dataset once and updates its internal parameters (weights and biases) based on the errors it makes on the training data.
    
    The **`epochCount`** hyperparameter defines the number of times the model will go through the entire training dataset during the training process.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-epoch-Count-graph-05-09-2024.png)
    
    Fine-tuning Foundation Models (FMs) often requires adjusting hyperparameters to optimize model performance.
    
    Increasing the number of epochs allows the model to train for more iterations, which provides more opportunities for the model to learn from the data and improve its accuracy. This method aims to enhance the model’s performance to meet a specified accuracy level.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Increase the `epochCount`**
    
    The option that says: **Decrease the `epochCount`** is incorrect because this typically limits the model’s exposure to the training data, leading to underfitting and poor accuracy.
    
    The option that says: **Increase the `learningRateWarmupSteps`** is incorrect. While adjusting the learning rate warmup steps can typically help stabilize training, it does not directly address increasing accuracy as effectively as increasing the number of epochs.
    
    The option that says: **Reduce the model size** is incorrect because reducing the model size would likely decrease the model’s capacity to learn complex patterns, potentially reducing its accuracy.
    

A financial institution is enhancing its cybersecurity measures using AI solutions. The solution must automatically detect suspicious IP addresses by analyzing patterns of unusual behavior in network traffic to prevent potential threats or attacks.

- Set up a predictive system for forecasting customer behavior.

    This is important in sectors such as finance, retail, and cybersecurity, but it is beneficial to companies in many industries. This feature provides an automated way to detect potentially dangerous anomalies and protect data.

    Anomaly detection systems are specifically designed for identifying unusual network behaviors, making them the most suitable solution for monitoring and preventing potential threats based on IP address behavior.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Deploy an anomaly detection system for identifying unusual patterns.**
    
    The option that says: **Implement a biometric system for user authentication** is incorrect because this solution is primarily used for verifying individual identities based on physical traits, not for detecting network-based threats or suspicious IP addresses.
    
    The option that says: **Use a natural language processing (NLP) model for text sentiment analysis** is incorrect because this approach typically focuses on interpreting text for emotions or opinions and does not apply to detecting unusual IP address behavior in network traffic.
    
    The option that says: **Set up a predictive system for forecasting customer behavior** is incorrect because this option is only focused on forecasting future business activities such as customer trends and preferences, rather than identifying cybersecurity threats in real-time network traffic.

A retail company leverages machine learning models to predict quarterly sales and optimize inventory management. In response to stakeholder requests, the data science team has been tasked with providing a comprehensive report that ensures transparency and explains the rationale behind the models’ decisions.

What should the data science team present to clearly explain the model’s recommendation process?

- Model convergence tables
- **Partial dependence plots (PDPs)**
    
    Amazon SageMaker Clarify, which includes using Partial Dependence Plots (PDPs), helps visualize the impact of different features on a model’s predictions. This allows stakeholders to better understand each feature’s role in the model’s decision-making process, enhancing overall model transparency.
    
    Amazon SageMaker Clarify generates partial dependence plots (PDPs) to display the marginal effect of features on a machine learning model’s predicted outcome. These plots help explain the target response with specific input features. Clarify also extends explainability to both computer vision (CV) and natural language processing (NLP) by utilizing the same Shapley values (SHAP) algorithm that is used for explaining tabular data models.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-Partial_Dependence-Plots-10Sept2024.jpg)
    
    Partial Dependence Plots (PDPs) are a powerful tool for explaining machine learning models by showing the relationship between features and the model’s predictions. They provide transparency by displaying how changing a feature affects the prediction while keeping other features constant. PDPs help understand the influence of each feature on the model’s decisions, which is particularly useful for explaining the model’s behavior to stakeholders.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Partial dependence plots (PDPs).**
    
    The option that says: **Hyperparameter tuning results** is incorrect because it primarily focuses on model optimization rather than explaining the decision-making process to stakeholders. Hyperparameter tuning affects performance but does not directly contribute to model explainability.
    
    The option that says: **Feature engineering scripts** is incorrect because they only involve data preprocessing steps and transformations applied to the raw data. While these scripts are crucial for building the model, they do not directly explain how the model makes decisions.
    
    The option that says: **Model convergence tables** is incorrect because they just provide information on the model’s training progress but do not offer insights into the rationale behind the model’s predictions.
    
- Hyperparameter tuning results
- Feature engineering scripts

An AI practitioner is developing a machine-learning model for a research lab to identify and categorize rare plant species from high-resolution images. Due to the complexity of the species and the need for high accuracy, the practitioner seeks a solution that minimizes the risk of incorrect annotations while ensuring human oversight in the labeling process.

Which solution should the practitioner use to ensure high accuracy and minimize the risk of incorrect annotations?

- Utilize Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler to prepare and analyze the dataset for machine learning.
- **Use Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth Plus for Human-in-the-loop validation.**
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth Plus** is a data labeling service that helps users build high-quality training datasets for machine learning (ML) models. It integrates a Human-in-the-loop (HITL) mechanism, where machine learning automation and human annotators work together to ensure highly accurate data labeling. This service is particularly useful for complex tasks like image annotation, where subject matter expertise may be required. Incorporating human review minimizes the risk of incorrect annotations, which is crucial in cases where even minor mislabeling can significantly impact the performance of an ML model. Ground Truth Plus also offers built-in workflows, quality checks, and automatic data augmentation to streamline the labeling process.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/sagemaker-ground-truth-labelling-jobs-08-26-24.png)
    
    The service allows customers to reduce labeling costs by using automated tools, and human reviewers only intervene when necessary. Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth Plus supports multiple data types, including images, text, and video, making it flexible across various use cases. The service handles the management and assignment of tasks to human reviewers, ensuring that the labeling process is scalable and meets the high accuracy requirements demanded by the project. Overall, this service is designed to improve data quality, directly impacting machine learning models’ accuracy and effectiveness.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Use Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth Plus for Human-in-the-loop validation.**
    
    The option that says: **Utilize Amazon Comprehend to categorize the plant species based on image metadata** is incorrect. This option is a natural language processing (NLP) service designed to extract insights only from text data, such as sentiment analysis, entity recognition, and language translation. It cannot analyze images or perform tasks related to image classification.
    
    The option that says: **Use Amazon Rekognition Custom Labels to train the model for species identification** is incorrect. Although Amazon Rekognition Custom Labels provides tools to train custom image recognition models, it lacks the Human-in-the-loop (HITL) validation needed to ensure high accuracy. Rekognition Custom Labels automates the labeling process but does not account for the human oversight required to minimize the risk of incorrect annotations
    
    The option that says: **Utilize Amazon SageMaker Data Wrangler to prepare and analyze the dataset for machine learning** is incorrect because it is primarily for data preparation and visualization for ML workflows. While it helps clean, transform, and analyze data, it does not offer labeling or model training capabilities. It is not designed to handle the specific task of image labeling and model training required for accurately categorizing plant species from images. Therefore, it does not address the practitioner’s need for high-accuracy labeling.
    
- Utilize Amazon Comprehend to categorize the plant species based on image metadata.
- Use Amazon Rekognition Custom Labels to train the model for species identification.

For example, Amazon GuardDuty can monitor network traffic and identify malicious activity in real-time. By continuously scanning for threats, this service allows the institution to detect abnormal behavior or unauthorized access attempts, fulfilling security requirements under compliance frameworks like PCI DSS, which mandate ongoing monitoring for threats.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-sagemaker-jumpstart-09-11-2024.png)

Additionally, fine-grained access control with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) helps restrict who can access and manipulate the data. These features help institutions meet GDPR’s strict data privacy and security requirements while ensuring secure model operations.

These frameworks require specific measures to protect customer data and detect potential security threats, which advanced model monitoring alone cannot fulfill.
