Although important for transparency, especially in regulated industries, this capability does not directly address the critical security and data protection mandates outlined by PCI DSS and GDPR. These regulations prioritize securing sensitive data, monitoring for threats, and ensuring that data is protected from unauthorized access, areas where model interpretability does not contribute.
    
    The option that says: **Cost optimization** is incorrect because optimizing costs is not relevant to regulatory compliance frameworks. While managing costs is essential for operational efficiency, it does not directly impact fulfilling security and privacy requirements under regulations.
    

A company is developing an LLM (Large Language Model) to process sensitive customer documents. The company must ensure responsible development and deployment to avoid potential risks.

Which actions should the company take to ensure responsible AI deployment of the LLM? (Select TWO.)

- Tune the model's hyperparameters.
- Optimize the LLM's performance by leveraging advanced hardware accelerators.
- Apply prompt engineering techniques.
- **Address bias by improving the diversity of the training dataset.**
- **Implement fairness metrics checks during the model evaluation.**
- **Evaluation**
    
    Responsible AI refers to the practice of developing and deploying AI systems in a way that promotes ethical principles, such as fairness, transparency, privacy, and accountability. It aims to mitigate potential risks and negative impacts associated with AI systems.
    
    **Explainability**: Providing clear explanations and interpretability of how AI systems make decisions, enabling accountability and trust.
    
    **Veracity and Robustness:** Ensuring AI systems operate reliably and consistently and are resilient to potential failures or adversarial attacks.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-core-dimentions+-of-responsible-ai-31-July-2024.png)
    
    LLMs can exhibit biases and unfair treatment towards certain groups or individuals based on their training data and model architecture. Fairness metrics are quantitative measures that assess the fairness of a model’s predictions across different demographic groups or protected attributes (e.g., race, gender, age). Fairness metrics can include statistical measures like demographic parity, equal opportunity, and disparate impact. Implementing fairness checks during evaluation allows for identifying and mitigating biases before deploying the LLM in production environments.
    
    Lastly, LLMs are trained on vast amounts of text data, and the quality and diversity of this training data can significantly impact the model’s performance and potential biases. Ensuring that the training data represents a diverse range of perspectives, backgrounds, and demographics is crucial for reducing biases in the LLM’s outputs. By improving the diversity of the training dataset, the LLM is exposed to a broader range of perspectives, reducing the likelihood of exhibiting biases towards specific groups or individuals.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Implement fairness metrics checks during the model evaluation.**
    
    **– Address bias by improving the diversity of the training dataset.**
    
    The option that says: **Apply prompt engineering techniques** is incorrect because it is primarily for improving the quality of the LLM’s outputs by carefully crafting the input prompts, but it does not directly address responsible deployment or bias mitigation.
    
    The option that says: **Optimize the LLM’s performance by leveraging advanced hardware accelerators** is incorrect. While optimizing the LLM’s performance using hardware accelerators can typically improve efficiency, it does not directly address the responsible development and deployment of the LLM or mitigate potential risks associated with sensitive customer documents.
    
    The option that says: **Tune the model’s hyperparameters** is incorrect. While tuning hyperparameters can primarily improve the model’s accuracy on the training dataset, it does not necessarily ensure responsible deployment or address potential risks associated with sensitive customer documents. Accuracy alone is not sufficient for responsible AI deployment, as it does not account for fairness, bias, or other ethical considerations.
    

An AI specialist is developing a project to analyze images from a retail store’s security cameras. The goal is to automatically categorize items on the shelves to help with inventory management.

Which of the following options is the most appropriate technique for this task?

- **Object detection**
    
    The object detection algorithm can identify and locate all instances of objects in an image from a known collection of object categories. It takes an image as input and then outputs the category that the object belongs to, along with a confidence score indicating the likelihood that it belongs to that category. Additionally, the algorithm predicts the object’s location and scale using a rectangular bounding box. Amazon SageMaker Object Detection utilizes the Single Shot Multibox Detector (SSD) algorithm, which employs a pre-trained convolutional neural network (CNN) for the base network. SSD uses the output of intermediate layers as features for detection.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/sagemaker-object-detection-091024.png)
    
    Object detection is the most suitable technique for the given scenario because it involves identifying and locating objects within an image. In the context of a retail store, object detection can be used to recognize and categorize different items on the shelves, which is essential for inventory management. This technique can detect multiple objects in a single image and provide bounding boxes around each detected item, making it easier to track inventory levels.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Object detection.**
    
    The option that says: **Text Classification** is incorrect because it is primarily used to categorize text data into predefined categories. It is unsuitable for analyzing images, as it deals with textual information rather than visual content.
    
    The option that says: **Inpainting** is incorrect because it is a technique that is typically used to fill in missing sections of an image. While it is useful for image restoration and editing, it does not serve the purpose of identifying and categorizing objects within an image.
    
    The option that says: **Semantic segmentation** is incorrect. Semantic segmentation tags every pixel in an image with a class label from a predefined set of classes. While useful for detailed scene understanding, it is more complex and computationally intensive than object detection. For categorizing items on shelves, object detection is more straightforward and efficient.
    
- Text Classification
- Semantic Segmentation
- Inpainting

An AI specialist is working on a project involving Large Language Models (LLMs) to translate text from English to Filipino. To evaluate the quality of the translations produced by the model, the specialist must choose an appropriate metric that compares machine-generated translations to human translations.

Which of the following metrics is the most suitable?

- **Bilingual Evaluation Understudy (BLEU)**
    
    **Bilingual Evaluation Understudy (BLEU)** is a metric that is specifically designed for evaluating machine translation. It compares the n-grams of the machine-generated translation to those of a human reference translation. The closer the machine translation is to the human translation, the higher the BLEU score. This metric is widely used in the field of natural language processing for its effectiveness in assessing translation quality.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/sagemaker-bleu091024.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Bilingual Evaluation Understudy (BLEU).**
    
    The option that says: **Recall-Oriented Understudy for Gisting Evaluation (ROUGE)** is incorrect because ROUGE is primarily used to evaluate text summarization rather than translation. It assesses the similarity of n-grams, word sequences, and word pairs between the machine-generated summary and the reference summary. Although it has some similarities with BLEU, it is not specifically designed for translation tasks.
    
    The option that says: **Mean Absolute Percentage Error (MAPE)** is incorrect because MAPE is a metric used to measure the accuracy of a forecasting model. It calculates the average absolute percentage error between predicted and actual values. This metric is not suitable for evaluating translation quality as it is designed for numerical data rather than text.
    
    The option that says: **Root Mean Squared Error (RMSE)** is incorrect because RMSE is a statistical measure that is typically used to evaluate the differences between predicted and observed values in regression tasks. It is not applicable to translation tasks as it does not measure the quality of text or language but rather the accuracy of numerical predictions.
    
- Root Mean Squared Error (RMSE)
- Recall-Oriented Understudy for Gisting Evaluation (ROUGE)
- Mean Absolute Percentage Error (MAPE)

Which of the following is a primary benefit of fine-tuning a pre-trained foundation model?

- It reduces the time needed for training.
- It significantly decreases the model’s size.
- **It improves performance for specific use cases.**
    
    Fine-tuning is the process of continually training pre-trained models to enhance performance for specific use cases.
    
    The foundation models available through Amazon SageMaker Canvas can assist you with various general-purpose tasks. However, if you have a specific use case and want customized responses based on your own data, you can fine-tune a foundation model.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/fine-tuning-model-091024.png)
    
    To fine-tune a foundation model, you provide a dataset containing sample prompts and corresponding model responses. The model is then trained on this data, enabling it to deliver more precise and relevant responses for your specific needs.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **It improves performance for specific use cases.**
    
    The option that says: **It significantly decreases the model’s size** is incorrect. Fine-tuning does not typically reduce the size of the model. The primary goal of fine-tuning is to improve performance on specific tasks, not to alter the model’s architecture or reduce its size.
    
    The option that says: **It reduces the complexity of the model** is incorrect. Fine-tuning does not primarily reduce the complexity of the model. The complexity remains the same as the original pre-trained model. Fine-tuning adjusts the model’s parameters to better fit the new data, but it does not simplify the model’s structure.
    
    The option that says: **It reduces the time needed for training** is incorrect. While fine-tuning can be faster than training a model from scratch, the primary benefit is not the reduction in training time. The main advantage is the improved performance for specific use cases. Fine-tuning leverages the knowledge already embedded in the pre-trained model, which can indeed save time, but this is a secondary benefit.
    
- It reduces the complexity of the model.

An AI practitioner is developing a chatbot using an FM (Foundational Model) to provide technical support without human intervention. The chatbot’s responses must adhere to the company’s desired tone and style guidelines while being cost-effective.

Which approach will meet these requirements MOST effectively?

- Apply RLHF
- Increase the number of tokens
- Decrease Batch Size
- **Implement prompt engineering techniques**
    
    **Prompt engineering** is the process of designing and crafting prompts (input text) to guide language models to generate desired outputs. Some of the Prompt Engineering Techniques are:
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-prompt-engineerin-12-Aug-2024.png)
    
    Prompt engineering is a highly effective approach for tailoring the chatbot’s responses to adhere to the desired tone and style guidelines. By carefully crafting the prompts with examples and instructions that reflect the company’s guidelines, the model can generate responses that align with those requirements.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Implement prompt engineering techniques.**
    
    The option that says: **Decrease Batch Size** is incorrect. Decreasing the batch size is typically done to improve the model’s performance during training or fine-tuning. It does not directly address the requirements of tone and style adherence or cost-effectiveness.
    
    The option that says: **Increase the number of tokens** is incorrect. Increasing the number of tokens may not directly address the requirements of adhering to tone and style guidelines or cost-effectiveness. While it could potentially improve the model’s performance, it may also increase the computational cost and may not necessarily ensure adherence to the desired guidelines.
    
    The option that says: **Apply RLHF** is incorrect. Reinforcement Learning from Human Feedback (RLHF) is a technique primarily designed to fine-tune LLMs to generate outputs that align with human preferences. However, RLHF can be computationally expensive and may not be the most cost-effective approach for this particular use case.
    

A company is developing an educational app that presents users with math problems involving basic arithmetic operations, such as “Calculate the sum of the following numbers: 7, 14, and 21.”

Which approach provides the most efficient solution while minimizing complexity?

- Train a supervised learning model on a dataset of arithmetic problems and solutions.
- Build a neural network to learn arithmetic operations from a large dataset.
- **Write code that uses predefined rules to calculate solutions based on arithmetic principles.**
    
    In the context of developing an educational app for basic arithmetic, the most efficient solution with minimal complexity involves utilizing straightforward computational methods rather than complex machine learning model.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-arithmetic-code-10-09-2024.png)
    
    Using predefined rules for arithmetic operations is the most efficient and straightforward approach because it directly computes the result based on arithmetic principles. This method requires minimal computational resources and avoids the complexity of machine learning models, which are unnecessary for basic arithmetic problems.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Write code that uses predefined rules to calculate solutions based on arithmetic principles.**
    
    The option that says: **Apply a linear regression model to predict arithmetic problem solutions** is incorrect because linear regression is a machine learning technique typically used for predicting continuous numerical values based on input features. It is not well-suited for solving basic arithmetic problems, which can be solved directly using predefined rules and principles. Using linear regression would introduce unnecessary complexity and require training data, which is not necessary for this task.
    
    The option that says: **Build a neural network to learn arithmetic operations from a large dataset** is incorrect because building a neural network is a complex and resource-intensive approach that is not necessary for solving basic arithmetic problems. Neural networks are powerful machine learning models capable of learning complex patterns and relationships from large datasets, but they are overkill for simple arithmetic operations that can be solved using predefined rules.
    
    The option that says: **Train a supervised learning model on a dataset of arithmetic problems and solutions** is incorrect because it involves using a supervised learning model, which requires a labeled dataset of arithmetic problems and their corresponding solutions. While this approach could potentially work, it is more complex and resource-intensive than just using predefined rules and principles of arithmetic operations. Additionally, creating and maintaining a large dataset of arithmetic problems and solutions would introduce unnecessary overhead.
    
- Apply a linear regression model to predict arithmetic problem solutions.

An AI researcher uses an Amazon Bedrock foundation model to generate summaries of technical support chat sessions. The researcher needs a mechanism to save detailed invocation logs, allowing monitoring of both input and output data for the model.

Which method will meet this requirement?

- Implement AWS Audit Manager to store and track model invocation logs.
- Use AWS CloudTrail to search for API activities related to model invocations.
- Use model invocation logging in AWS Lambda.
- **Configure model invocation logging within Amazon Bedrock.**
    
    Amazon Bedrock offers a seamless experience for deploying and utilizing pre-trained foundation models. By activating **invocation logging** within Amazon Bedrock, the service records both the input and output data for every invocation, providing detailed logs that can be monitored for accuracy, performance, or troubleshooting. This feature is highly beneficial for use cases like summarizing customer service chat sessions, where maintaining a log of model interactions is critical to ensuring model performance and compliance.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-Amazon-Bedrock-Invocation-Logging-11Sept2024.png)
    
    Bedrock’s logging mechanism integrates with other AWS services, such as **Amazon CloudWatch**, which can further enhance monitoring capabilities by offering real-time metrics and alarms. However, invocation logging remains a primary method for capturing detailed information about model interactions, including input and output data.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Configure model invocation logging within Amazon Bedrock.**
    
    The option that says: **Use AWS CloudTrail to search for API activities related to model invocations** is incorrect. Although CloudTrail can track API call history, iy does not provide detailed logs of input and output data for model invocations.
    
    The option that says: **Implement AWS Audit Manager to store and track model invocation logs** is incorrect because it is typically designed for automating audits to simplify compliance reporting. It is not intended to log and monitor the invocation of models. Therefore, it would not fulfill the need for tracking input and output logs of the model.
    
    The option that says: **Use model invocation logging in AWS Lambda** is incorrect. This option is irrelevant since model invocations in Amazon Bedrock do not use AWS Lambda for logging; Bedrock has its own logging mechanisms.

An AI specialist has deployed a deep learning model for object detection on an Amazon SageMaker endpoint. The specialist uploads a new image to the endpoint to identify objects.

Which of the following processes is being described in this scenario?

- Model Deployment
- **Inference**
    
    Amazon SageMaker allows you to start making predictions, or inferences, using your trained machine learning models. It offers a wide range of machine learning infrastructure and model deployment options to cater to all your inference requirements. With SageMaker Inference, you can scale your model deployment, effectively manage models in production, and minimize operational workload. SageMaker offers various inference options, including real-time endpoints for low-latency inference, serverless endpoints for fully managed infrastructure and auto-scaling, and asynchronous endpoints for batches of requests. By utilizing the most suitable inference option for your specific use case, you can ensure efficient model deployment and inference.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/sagemaker-inference-091124.png)
    
    Inference is the process of using a trained model to make predictions or identify objects in new data. In this scenario, the AI specialist is using the deployed model on the SageMaker endpoint to analyze a new image and identify objects within it.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Inference.**
    
    The option that says: **Data Augmentation** is incorrect. Data augmentation involves creating new training examples by applying transformations like rotations, flips, or color changes to existing data. This process is typically used to increase the diversity of the training dataset and improve the model’s robustness. It is not related to using the model for predictions on new data.
    
    The option that says: **Model Deployment** is incorrect. Model deployment primarily refers to making a trained model available in a production environment. This involves setting up the infrastructure, such as an endpoint on Amazon SageMaker, where the model can receive and process requests. While deployment is a crucial step, the scenario described focuses on using the model to make predictions, not on the deployment itself.
    
    The option that says: **Hyperparameter Tuning** is incorrect because Hyperparameter tuning is the process of optimizing the hyperparameters of a model to improve its performance. This is done during the training phase and involves adjusting parameters like learning rate, batch size, and the number of layers. The scenario described does not involve adjusting hyperparameters but rather using the model to make predictions.
    
- Data Augmentation
- Hyperparameter Tuning

A streaming service uses Amazon Bedrock to run a detection model that flags inappropriate content in user comments. The service needs a solution to filter out inappropriate content to comply with its moderation policies and alert it if policy violations occur.

Which is the MOST appropriate approach to fulfill the requirements?

- Use Amazon SageMaker Clarify to detect biases in the model’s output and flag inappropriate content.
- Configure Amazon SNS to send notifications when the model detects inappropriate content.
- **Implement content filtering using Amazon Bedrock's Guardrails. Configure Amazon CloudWatch alarms to trigger notifications when there are policy violations.**
    
    **Amazon Bedrock** is designed to streamline the use of foundation models in applications by eliminating the need for developers to manage infrastructure or model deployment. With access to pre-trained models from leading AI developers, users can focus on building and integrating AI-driven features, such as content moderation or natural language processing, into their applications. One of the capabilities of Amazon Bedrock is **Guardrails for Amazon Bedrock**, which ensures that generative AI outputs remain within specified ethical and business guidelines. These highly customizable guardrails make them useful for automating the moderation of user-generated content, particularly in industries with strict compliance requirements like streaming or social media services.
    
    ![](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-guardrails-for-amazon-bedrock-09-11-2024.png)
    
    By integrating Amazon CloudWatch, applications built on Bedrock can be continuously monitored for performance and adherence to guardrails. Amazon CloudWatch tracks real-time metrics and allows users to set thresholds for triggering alarms when policy violations are detected, such as when the detection model flags inappropriate content. This seamless integration enhances operational efficiency, ensuring that violations are promptly addressed, and helps organizations comply with legal and ethical standards without constant manual oversight. The combination of Bedrock’s generative AI capabilities with robust monitoring from CloudWatch provides a comprehensive solution for content moderation and real-time alerts.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Implement content filtering using Amazon Bedrock’s Guardrails. Configure Amazon CloudWatch alarms to trigger notifications when there are policy violations.**
    
    The option that says: **Use Amazon SageMaker Clarify to detect biases in the model’s output and flag inappropriate content** is incorrect. This option primarily focuses on improving model transparency and fairness by detecting biases in data and machine learning models. While these capabilities are essential in AI ethics, they are not designed to directly detect or filter inappropriate content. Clarify helps ensure that models do not discriminate or produce biased results, but it does not include specific tools for content moderation or policy violation detection.
    
    The option that says: **Configure Amazon SNS to send notifications when the model detects inappropriate content** is incorrect. Although this option is an excellent service for distributing notifications or messages to different endpoints, it only handles notifications and does not play any role in the actual detection or filtering of inappropriate content. Amazon SNS can be integrated into a broader content moderation workflow but would not be a standalone solution for flagging inappropriate content.
    
    The option that says: **Use Amazon Comprehend’s custom classification to detect and filter inappropriate content. Set up Amazon CloudWatch alarms for policy violations** is incorrect. While this option provides custom text classification, significant customization is required to achieve functionality similar to Amazon Bedrock’s Guardrails. Amazon Comprehend excels at NLP tasks like sentiment analysis and keyword extraction but lacks the built-in content moderation tools that Bedrock provides for generative AI models. Although you could implement content filtering with Comprehend, Bedrock is a more efficient and focused solution for moderation since it offers pre-built models and integrated compliance features that directly address the streaming service’s needs.
    
- Use Amazon Comprehend’s custom classification to detect and filter inappropriate content. Set up Amazon CloudWatch alarms for policy violations.

A retail company has augmented a foundation model using Amazon Bedrock to handle customer inquiries about its products and promotions. The company is using a Knowledge Base in Bedrock to enhance the model’s responses with relevant information, and uses Amazon CloudWatch Logs for model invocation logging.

The company now wants to extend the model’s performance on a new batch of queries by updating the information available to the model.

What should the company do to enable Bedrock to access the new dataset?

- Upload the dataset to Amazon DynamoDB and grant full table access to Bedrock.
- Store the dataset on Amazon Elastic File System (Amazon EFS) and link it to the Bedrock model.
- Copy the dataset to an Amazon EC2 instance and configure the Knowledge Base to read from it via SSH.
- **Upload the dataset to the appropriate Amazon S3 bucket and resync the Knowledge Base.**
    
    With Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases, you can build applications enriched by the context received from querying a knowledge base. It enables faster market time by abstracting from the heavy lifting of building pipelines and providing you with an out-of-the-box Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) solution to reduce the build time for your application. Adding a knowledge base also increases cost-effectiveness by removing the need to train your model continually to leverage your private data.
    
    ![Amazon Bedrock Knowledge Bases](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazon-bedrock-knowledge-bases-2025Apr28.png)
    
    One must upload the new dataset to the associated Amazon S3 bucket when using Amazon Bedrock with a Knowledge Base. After that, resyncing the Knowledge Base is necessary to ensure the model can access the latest data. This ensures that the model’s responses are based on updated information.
    
    Thus, the correct answer is: **Upload the dataset to the appropriate Amazon S3 bucket and resync the Knowledge Base.**
    
    The option that says: **Copy the dataset to an Amazon EC2 instance and configure the Knowledge Base to read from it via SSH** is incorrect. Amazon Bedrock does not ingest or directly access data from an EC2 instance over SSH to update the Knowledge Base. Bedrock and Knowledge Base integration is primarily done through Amazon S3 or specific APIs, not EC2 with SSH access.
    
    The option that says: **Store the dataset on Amazon Elastic File System (Amazon EFS) and link it to the Bedrock model** is incorrect. Amazon EFS is not typically used with Amazon Bedrock for dataset ingestion or Knowledge Base storage. EFS is generally used with Amazon EC2 instances or containers for shared persistent file storage, but Bedrock doesn’t support direct integration with EFS for accessing a Knowledge Base.
    
    The option that says: **Upload the dataset to Amazon DynamoDB and grant full table access to Bedrock** is incorrect. DynamoDB is a NoSQL database that is not typically used as the backend for a Knowledge Base in Amazon Bedrock. Bedrock doesn’t interact with DynamoDB to augment the knowledge base. Instead, it relies on S3 as the primary data storage for Knowledge Bases.
    

A data science team is training machine learning models using Amazon SageMaker Notebook instances. The datasets reside in Amazon S3. The team must ensure that the data traffic entirely remains within the AWS network due to security and compliance requirements.

Which solution provides the best and most cost-effective architecture for this requirement?

- **Launch SageMaker Notebook instances in a VPC that includes a Gateway Endpoint for S3.**
    
    Amazon S3 supports both **gateway endpoints** and **interface endpoints**. With a gateway endpoint, you can access Amazon S3 from your VPC, without requiring an internet gateway or NAT device for your VPC, and with no additional cost. However, gateway endpoints do not allow access from on-premises networks, from peered VPCs in other AWS Regions, or through a transit gateway. For those scenarios, you must use an interface endpoint, which is available for an additional cost.
    
    ![VPC Gateway Endpoint](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/vpc-gateway-endpoint-2025Apr29.png)
    
    When you launch SageMaker Notebook instances inside a VPC and configure a private S3 Gateway VPC Endpoint, all S3 traffic flows privately over the AWS backbone. This satisfies both security and compliance requirements to keep AWS traffic internal.
    
    Thus, the correct answer is: **Launch SageMaker Notebook instances in a VPC that includes a Gateway Endpoint for S3**.
    
    The option that says: **Archive the datasets into S3 Glacier Deep Archive to streamline SageMaker Notebook instance retrieval** is incorrect. S3 Glacier Deep Archive is simply used for long-term cold storage, where access is infrequent and retrieval times are hours. It is inappropriate for live training data access during machine learning model development, where low-latency, frequent data access is needed. Also, moving data to Glacier does not affect network routing or traffic security; it simply affects storage cost and retrieval time.
    
    The option that says: **Use AWS PrivateLink to connect SageMaker Notebook instances to S3 securely** is incorrect. AWS PrivateLink provides private connectivity between VPCs and services powered by Elastic Network Interfaces (ENIs), usually for AWS services that expose APIs or for third-party services. Using PrivateLink (an Interface Endpoint) for S3 adds unnecessary complexity and cost when a simpler Gateway Endpoint would do. While S3 technically has an interface endpoint and a gateway endpoint, the standard and recommended approach for connecting to S3 securely is to use an S3 Gateway Endpoint, not PrivateLink.
    
    The option that says: **Establish VPC peering between SageMaker Notebook instances’ VPC and an S3-hosting VPC to facilitate data access**is incorrect. Amazon S3 is a global service that is not tied to any specific Virtual Private Cloud (VPC). VPC peering connects two VPCs (like two application VPCs), not a VPC to an AWS regional service like S3. Even if you set up VPC peering, it would not help you access S3 securely. You would still need a VPC Endpoint for S3 to achieve the goal of keeping traffic private.
    
- Establish VPC peering between SageMaker Notebook instances’ VPC and an S3-hosting VPC to facilitate data access.
- Use AWS PrivateLink to connect SageMaker Notebook instances to S3 securely.
- Archive the datasets into S3 Glacier Deep Archive to streamline SageMaker Notebook instance retrieval.

A technology enterprise plans on leveraging a machine learning (ML) model to detect inappropriate speech in social media comments, filter it out, and ban offending users to maintain a safe environment for its community. The company prefers to train the model without labeled data.

What approach should the company employ to detect inappropriate speech and flag harmful language?

- Use Amazon Rekognition content moderation.
- Use Amazon Transcribe toxicity detection.
- Use Amazon Augmented AI (A2I) content moderation.
- **Use Amazon Comprehend toxicity detection.**
    
    Amazon Comprehend is a natural language processing (NLP) service that **analyzes and extracts insights from a given context**. This service helps users understand content, including language, sentiments (positive, negative, or neutral), and other recurring components.
    
    Additionally, it helps analyze large amounts of unstructured text data to gain useful business insights and make data-driven decisions. For small workloads, real-time analysis can be performed; for larger document sets, asynchronous analysis jobs can be started. There is an option to train unique models for classification and entity recognition, or to use the pre-trained models that Amazon Comprehend offers.
    
    This service also has a **trust and safety feature**that focuses on content filtering by identifying dangerous or offensive information, such as hate speech and threats, using toxicity detection. It also flags content with malicious intent, such as discriminatory or illegal topics, and protects user privacy by recognizing and redacting personally identifiable information (PII).
    
    ![Amazon Comprehend Management Console](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/Amazon-Comprehend_17MAY2025.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Use Amazon Comprehend toxicity detection.**
    
    The option that says: **Use Amazon Rekognition content moderation** is incorrect. Although Amazon Rekognition has a text recognition feature through its advanced image and video analysis, it does not offer direct functionality for text analysis like detecting harmful language in social media comments.
    
    The option that says: **Use Amazon Transcribe toxicity detection** is incorrect. Amazon Transcribe Toxicity Detection is simply a machine learning-powered service designed to detect toxic or harmful spoken language from audio inputs such as phone calls, voice messages, or live conversations. While effective for voice-based scenarios, it is not suitable for detecting harmful text in written formats like comments or chat messages.
    
    The option that says: **Use Amazon Augmented AI (A2I) content moderation** is incorrect. Amazon Augmented AI (A2I) is primarily used to insert human review into machine learning workflows, often for edge cases where human judgment is needed. While A2I can be part of a content moderation pipeline, it does not perform automatic detection of harmful language on its own. It serves as a supplementary tool, not a primary solution for detecting harmful text.
    

A manufacturing company needs to generate product descriptions for a new line of smart home devices in multiple languages (English, Filipino, French, and German). The company wants to automate the process of translating these product descriptions for the online store to save time and ensure consistency across different language versions of the product listings.

What AWS service should be used to automate this process?

- Amazon Lex
- Amazon Comprehend
- Amazon Transcribe
- **Amazon Translate**
    
    Amazon Translate is a **machine translation service** that automatically translates text between various languages. It offers a managed, continuously trained system that simplifies translating user-authored and company content or creating apps that need multilingual support.
    
    One of Amazon Translate’s key features is **batch translation**, which allows for translating large volumes of text stored in Amazon S3. It is ideal for scenarios where real-time translation is not required, such as processing document archives, translating product catalogs, or localizing datasets. You can initiate batch translation jobs via the AWS Management Console, CLI, or SDK, and the results are stored back in S3 in the specified output format.
    
    Batch Translation helps streamline the translation of large datasets, enabling efficient multilingual content management at scale.
    
    ![Amazon Translate Management Console](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/Amazon-Translate_18MAY2025.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon Translate.**
    
    **Amazon Lex** is incorrect because it is primarily a service for building conversational interfaces and chatbots that understand natural language. While it can process and respond in multiple languages, it does not provide translation or text generation across different languages like Amazon Translate.
    
    **Amazon Comprehend** is incorrect because it is a natural language processing (NLP) service that simply analyzes text for insights such as sentiment, entities, and key phrases. Although it’s great for text analysis, it does not perform translation tasks.
    
    **Amazon Transcribe** is incorrect because it is a speech-to-text service that only converts audio input into written text. While effective for transcribing audio content, it does not offer translation capabilities or generate text in multiple languages.
    

A content streaming service wants to evaluate user behavior and demographic data to deliver personalized recommendations. The service plans to deploy a custom machine learning model in its live environment and track its performance over time to detect any changes in model accuracy.

To meet these demands, which AWS feature is the most suitable to use?

- Amazon SageMaker Endpoint
- **Amazon SageMaker Model Monitor**
    
    Amazon SageMaker Model Monitor is specifically designed to **monitor machine learning models in production**. It tracks the model’s performance over time, detecting drift in data or predictions, which is essential for ensuring model accuracy and reliability.
    
    This service allows you to set up continuous monitoring through a real-time endpoint or regularly scheduled batch transform jobs, including asynchronous ones. It enables you to configure alerts that notify you of deviations in model quality, helping you detect issues early and take proactive steps such as retraining models, auditing upstream systems, or addressing quality problems without the need for manual monitoring or custom-built tools. You can leverage its prebuilt, no-code monitoring features or opt for a code-based approach to implement custom analysis as needed.
    
    ![Amazon SageMaker Model Monitor Dashboard](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/Amazon-Sagemaker-Model-Dashboard_23MAY2025.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon SageMaker Model Monitor.**
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Clarify** is incorrect. Amazon SageMaker Clarify is primarily focused on improving fairness and explainability in machine learning models by identifying and mitigating bias. While this is valuable for ensuring ethical model behavior, it does not specifically track performance or model drift over time, which is the key requirement in this scenario.
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Endpoint** is incorrect. Amazon SageMaker Endpoints allow for real-time inference and deployment of machine learning models. However, it does not provide any capabilities for tracking model performance or detecting drift in model predictions over time. While useful for serving models, SageMaker Endpoints are not suited for continuous monitoring of model quality.
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Autopilot** is incorrect. Amazon SageMaker Autopilot simply automates the process of building machine learning models, from data preprocessing to feature selection and model training. While it helps automate the creation of models, it does not focus on monitoring models once deployed in production or tracking drift in performance over time. It’s primarily a tool for creating models, not for observing their ongoing performance.
    
- Amazon SageMaker Clarify
- Amazon SageMaker Autopilot

A recruitment platform developed an AI-based candidate evaluation system. The platform trained the model using a large dataset, but the dataset was skewed, lacking representation from various demographic groups.

What aspect of responsible AI does this scenario emphasize?

- Veracity and Robustness
- Controllability
- **Fairness**
    
    **Fairness** is a key principle of responsible AI, ensuring that AI systems make decisions that are equitable and free from bias, regardless of factors such as race, gender, age, or socioeconomic status. It aims to prevent discrimination and ensure that all individuals are treated fairly when interacting with AI technologies.
    
    Achieving fairness requires careful attention to the data used for training, making sure it is diverse, representative, and does not reinforce historical biases. This involves identifying and mitigating potential sources of bias, regularly evaluating model outcomes, and involving diverse perspectives in the development process to ensure fair and inclusive results.
    
    ![Eight Pillars of Responsible AI](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/RESPONSIBLE-AI_25MAY2025.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Fairness.**
    
    **Controllability** is incorrect. It refers primarily to the ability to guide, constrain, or influence an AI system’s behavior and outputs. While controllability is an important aspect of responsible AI, this scenario focuses on the model’s fairness due to the biased dataset. Controllability involves mechanisms to steer or adjust AI behavior, but it does not directly address the fairness or representativeness of the training data.
    
    **Veracity and Robustness** is incorrect. It simply refers to the ability of an AI system to handle a wide range of inputs and function reliably, even in the presence of errors, adversarial inputs, or changing conditions. While veracity and robustness is an essential quality for AI systems, the core issue in this scenario is the bias in the dataset, not the system’s ability to perform reliably under different conditions.
    
    **Transparency** is incorrect. It involves making AI models and their decision-making processes understandable to users and stakeholders. While transparency is important for building trust, the core issue in this scenario is the fairness of the dataset and ensuring the AI model does not discriminate based on unrepresentative data. Transparency would involve explaining how the model works, but it does not directly address bias or fairness in the data.
    
- Transparency

It offers access to a variety of pre-trained models suitable for a wide range of applications, enabling teams to focus on innovation rather than backend operations.

In addition, Bedrock supports customization through prompt engineering and techniques like Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG), allowing businesses to adapt models to their specific needs and use cases. This flexibility makes Amazon Bedrock a strong choice for companies looking to enhance productivity, improve customer engagement, and automate complex processes through AI.

Therefore, it is not suitable for this scenario.

While it supports creating custom ML models, it is not a fully managed service specifically designed for leveraging foundation models to automate customer service interactions. SageMaker focuses more on developing and managing models rather than directly using pre-built foundation models to automate processes.
    
    **Amazon Polly** is incorrect. Amazon Polly is a text-to-speech service that converts text into lifelike speech. While it could be useful in a customer service setting for converting text-based recommendations into spoken words, it does not automate the process of generating recommendations based on customer location or provide foundation models for automation. Therefore, Polly is not the best fit for automating the recommendation process.

A biotech startup has an ML application on AWS that analyzes real-time patient data to forecast market trends in drug demand. Since the application handles Protected Health Information (PHI), the company must assess compliance requirements under HIPAA and FDA regulations while ensuring secure development.

Which AWS services can help the startup continuously monitor compliance while maintaining security best practices? (Select TWO.)

- AWS Trusted Advisor
- Amazon Comprehend Medical
- **AWS Audit Manager**
- Amazon Macie
- **AWS Config**
- **Evaluation**
    
    **AWS Audit Manager**
    
    is a governance service designed to simplify compliance audits in cloud environments. The service continuously collects evidence from AWS resources and maps configurations to industry standards and regulatory frameworks such as HIPAA, SOC 2, PCI DSS, and ISO 27001. It maintains an audit-ready repository of evidence that demonstrates how resources comply with specific requirements. Organizations can use pre-built frameworks or create custom ones tailored to their compliance needs. A key benefit is the automation of evidence gathering, significantly reducing the manual effort traditionally required for compliance audits. The service also provides detailed reports that help stakeholders understand compliance status and prepare for official audits more efficiently.
    
    ![AWS Audit Manager](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-AWS-Audit-Manager-07-15-2025.png)
    
    **AWS Config** is a configuration management service that records and evaluates changes to AWS resources. It maintains a complete inventory of cloud assets and their configuration history, enabling organizations to track modifications over time. The service assesses resource configurations against predefined rules that reflect security best practices and compliance requirements. AWS Config generates alerts when deviations occur and can trigger automated remediation workflows through integration with AWS Lambda. This capability is particularly valuable for maintaining continuous compliance and enforcing governance policies. The service also provides a detailed view of resource relationships, helping administrators understand how changes in one component might affect others in their environment.
    
    ![AWS Config](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-AWS-Config-07-15-2025.png)
    
    While serving different primary functions, both services contribute significantly to cloud governance strategies. AWS Audit Manager focuses on the documentation and reporting aspects of compliance, providing structured evidence for audits. AWS Config emphasizes operational compliance by monitoring resource states and detecting configuration drift. Organizations often implement both services to establish comprehensive governance, with AWS Config ensuring resources remain properly configured in real-time, and AWS Audit Manager preparing the necessary documentation to prove compliance during audits.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– AWS Audit Manager.**
    
    **– AWS Config.**
    
    **Amazon Comprehend Medical** is incorrect because it is primarily designed for natural language processing (NLP) of medical texts, merely extracting insights from clinical data without any capability to monitor compliance requirements or enforce security policies. While useful for analyzing PHI content, it simply doesn’t address the regulatory assessment needs of HIPAA or FDA compliance frameworks.
    
    **AWS Trusted Advisor** is incorrect because it only provides generic best practice recommendations, typically focusing on cost optimization and basic security checks rather than specialized healthcare compliance monitoring. Its suggestions are too broad and lack the specific regulatory mapping needed for proper HIPAA/FDA adherence, making it insufficient for continuous compliance assessment.
    
    **Amazon Macie** is incorrect because it is a data classification service that identifies where sensitive information like PHI is stored, without evaluating whether the storage methods meet compliance standards. While valuable for data discovery, it doesn’t assess or enforce compliance controls nor generate audit evidence required for regulatory validation.
    

A healthcare provider is building a predictive population health platform to analyze patient demographics and treatment adherence patterns. The goal is to identify the distinct patient groups to optimize outreach programs, such as targeting high-risk groups for flu shots or customizing diabetes management campaigns.

Which machine learning algorithm should the healthcare provider use to segment patients based on these clinical and socioeconomic factors automatically?

- Principal Component Analysis (PCA)
- Random Cut Forest (RCF)
- K-Nearest Neighbors (KNN)
- **K-Means Clustering**
    
    **K-Means Clustering** is a popular unsupervised machine learning algorithm that divides a dataset into a predefined number of groups, or clusters, based on similarity among data points. The algorithm works by initializing K cluster centroids, assigning each data point to the nearest centroid, and then updating the centroids based on the current assignments. This process repeats until the centroids stabilize, effectively grouping similar data points. Because it doesn’t require labeled data, K-Means is ideal for exploratory data analysis where patterns and groupings are not known in advance.
    
    ![K-Means-Clustering](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/K-Means-Clustering-07-15-2025.png)
    
    In healthcare, K-Means Clustering can be an effective tool for patient segmentation. By analyzing patient data—such as age, gender, medical history, income, geographic location, and treatment adherence—healthcare providers can identify distinct population groups with shared characteristics. These clusters may represent high-risk patients needing preventive care, individuals with poor medication adherence, or populations vulnerable to chronic conditions like diabetes or hypertension. This segmentation helps providers design more personalized and targeted health interventions, improving outcomes and optimizing resource allocation.
    
    One of the main advantages of K-Means is its simplicity and scalability, making it suitable for large and complex datasets often found in healthcare systems. However, some domain knowledge is required to choose the appropriate number of clusters (K) and interpret the results meaningfully. It’s also sensitive to outliers and works best when the clusters are well-separated and roughly equal. Despite these limitations, K-Means remains one of the most widely used clustering techniques in population health analytics.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **K-Means Clustering.**
    
    **Random Cut Forest (RCF)** is incorrect because it is primarily designed for anomaly detection, which aims to identify rare or unusual data points in a dataset. It works by building a collection of decision trees that simply isolate data points, and those that are easier to isolate are flagged as anomalies. This is only useful when the objective is to detect patients whose clinical or behavioral patterns deviate significantly from the norm, such as a sudden spike in emergency visits or a sharp decline in medication adherence.
    
    **K-Nearest Neighbors (KNN)** is incorrect because it is typically used for supervised learning tasks such as classification or regression, where the goal is to predict a label for new data based on known labels in the training set. It simply finds the most similar data points (neighbors) and makes predictions based on them. Since the healthcare provider’s goal is to automatically discover patient segments without predefined categories, KNN is not appropriate, it requires labels, which are not typically available in unsupervised segmentation tasks.
    
    **Principal Component Analysis (PCA)** is incorrect because it is only a dimensionality reduction technique, not a clustering method. It is used to simplify datasets, often with many variables, by transforming them into a smaller number of principal components that capture the most variance. PCA is typically used to improve performance or visualization, especially when dealing with high-dimensional data.
    

A support team at a global e-commerce business aims to deploy a chatbot powered by a Large Language Model (LLM) to deliver real-time, relevant replies to client questions.

The chatbot will leverage internal policy documents, indexed by Amazon Kendra, as the knowledge base to ensure that responses are accurate and aligned with guidelines.

Which approach will achieve these objectives in the most cost-efficient way?

- Use Amazon Lex to build conversational interfaces and integrate the chatbot with the policy data.
- Enable Amazon Kendra's query suggestion feature to help refine the chatbot's search results.
- Retrain the LLM using the policy documents to adjust its understanding.
- **Employ Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) to retrieve relevant information dynamically for in-context responses.**
    
    **Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG)** is a technique that combines the capabilities of Large Language Models (LLMs) with external data retrieval to improve the quality and relevance of generated responses. RAG works by first retrieving relevant information from an external source, such as a database or knowledge base, and then using an LLM to generate a response based on that data. This method enhances the model’s ability to provide accurate and contextual responses by leveraging both the generative power of the LLM and the factual accuracy of the external data source.
    
    ![RAG with LLM](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-RAG+with+LLMs-15July2025.png)
    
    One of the key advantages of RAG is that it allows the system to dynamically pull in relevant information without the need for costly and resource-intensive fine-tuning or retraining of the LLM. Since the LLM generates responses based on real-time data retrieval, the process is not static; it adapts to new information as it becomes available. This makes RAG a highly cost-effective and scalable approach for implementing language models in applications that require up-to-date and accurate information, as it avoids the need to rebuild or retrain large models for each new dataset.
    
    Furthermore, RAG can seamlessly integrate with data indexing and retrieval systems, such as Amazon Kendra, which is designed to index and search vast amounts of unstructured data. This integration ensures that the information retrieved by RAG is both relevant and accurate, providing reliable answers to user queries. The combination of RAG and an efficient retrieval system like Kendra makes this approach ideal for applications where real-time access to a growing knowledge base is required, while maintaining high performance and minimizing operational costs.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Employ Retrieval-Augmented Generation (RAG) to retrieve relevant information dynamically for in-context responses.**
    
    The option that says: **Use Amazon Lex to build conversational interfaces and integrate the chatbot with the policy data** is incorrect because Amazon Lex is primarily used for building conversational interfaces, such as chatbots, but it doesn’t address the issue of dynamically retrieving contextually relevant information from an extensive knowledge base. Lex is typically focused on managing dialogues and user interactions, rather than the real-time retrieval of policy data needed for accurate and context-aware responses in the chatbot scenario.
    
    The option that says: **Retrain the LLM using the policy documents to adjust its understanding** is incorrect because retraining it with specific data, such as policy documents, would require significant computational resources and time. This process is not just costly but also inefficient, especially when the goal is to provide real-time, context-aware responses without altering the core capabilities of the model. The scenario requires a more scalable and cost-effective solution that doesn’t involve the extensive retraining of the model..
    
    The option that says: **Enable Amazon Kendra’s query suggestion feature to help refine the chatbot’s search results** is incorrect because Kendra’s query suggestion feature is simply used to suggest possible search terms based on user input, but it doesn’t provide real-time data retrieval or contextual responses. The query suggestion feature is useful for improving search accuracy, but it doesn’t help the chatbot generate contextually relevant responses based on dynamic retrieval of information from the policy documents.
    

A company is developing machine learning models to make automated decisions based on large datasets. The company needs to provide transparency into how the models make predictions and offer clear explanations for the outcomes. This is essential to understand the factors influencing the model’s decisions.

Which of the following will meet the given requirements?

By creating model cards, you can achieve the following:

    – Support audit activities with detailed descriptions of the model’s training and performance.

    ![Amazon SageMaker Model Cards](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/sagemaker-model-cards15JUL2025.png)
    
    Model cards offer clear guidance on what information to document and include custom fields for additional details. Once you create a model card, you can export it as a PDF or download it for sharing with relevant stakeholders. If any edits are made to a model card—except for updates to its approval status—new versions of the model card will be created. This ensures that there is an immutable record of all changes made to the model.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon SageMaker Model Cards.** Model cards are crucial for registered machine learning (ML) models, offering a standardized method to document and communicate essential metadata. This includes information about the model’s intended use, performance, risks, and business implications. Transparency is essential for registered models, which are frequently used in high-stakes or regulated industries such as financial services and healthcare. While powerful for text analytics, it does not provide transparency or explanations for how machine learning models make decisions.
    
    **Amazon Polly** is incorrect because it is only a text-to-speech service that converts written content into spoken audio. It has no functionality related to machine learning model documentation or explainability.
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Ground Truth** is incorrect because it is primarily used for building and managing labeled datasets for training machine learning models. Although it helps improve model accuracy by providing quality training data, it does not offer insights into how models make predictions or explain their outcomes.
