SageMaker Studio is ideal for developing and deploying models but may require more setup and expertise than PartyRock, which is specifically designed for quick experimentation with foundation models in a no-code, interactive setting.
    
    **Amazon SageMaker AI endpoints** is incorrect because it is simply used for deploying machine learning models into production environments where they can serve real-time predictions. While they are useful for serving models after training, they are not suited for quick experimentation with foundation models in a non-production setting. The team would need to first develop and deploy a model before using SageMaker AI endpoints, making it a less ideal option for the scenario described.
    
- Amazon SageMaker endpoints

A company is using Amazon Bedrock for machine learning and AWS Lambda for event-driven workflows. The company must log all requests to the Bedrock API and Lambda invocations, ensuring the logs are securely retained for 3 years with minimal storage costs.

Which of the following will meet the given requirements? (Select TWO.)

- **AWS CloudTrail**
- Amazon CloudWatch
- Amazon S3 Glacier Deep Archive
- **Amazon S3 Intelligent-Tiering**
- AWS Config
- **Evaluation**
    
    Amazon Bedrock is integrated with AWS CloudTrail, which records actions taken by users, roles, or AWS services within Amazon Bedrock. CloudTrail captures all API calls related to Amazon Bedrock as events. These calls include actions performed from the Amazon Bedrock console as well as code calls to the Amazon Bedrock API operations. If you create a trail, you can enable continuous delivery of CloudTrail events to an Amazon S3 bucket, including events related to Amazon Bedrock.
    
    ![AWS CloudTrail](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/cloudtrail-bedrock-api-18JUL2025.png)
    
    With AWS CloudTrail, you can monitor your AWS deployments in the cloud by obtaining a history of API calls made to your account. This includes calls made through the AWS Management Console, AWS SDKs, command line tools, and various higher-level AWS services. You can identify which users and accounts initiated these API calls for services that support CloudTrail, as well as the source IP addresses from which the calls originated and the timestamps of these calls. Additionally, you can integrate CloudTrail into applications using its API, automate the creation of trails for your organization, check the status of your trails, and manage how administrators enable or disable CloudTrail logging.
    
    **S3 Intelligent-Tiering is an Amazon S3 storage class designed to optimize storage costs by automatically transferring data to the most cost-effective access tier, without affecting performance or creating operational overhead. It is the only cloud storage class that provides automatic cost savings by moving data at a granular object level between access tiers as access patterns change. S3 Intelligent-Tiering is ideal for storing data with unpredictable or changing access patterns. Additionally, there are no retrieval fees associated with S3 Intelligent-Tiering.**
    
    ![S3 Intelligent-Tiering](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/s3-storage-classes-18JUL2025.png)
    
    For a small monthly fee for object monitoring and automation, S3 Intelligent-Tiering tracks access patterns and automatically moves objects that haven’t been accessed to lower-cost storage tiers. This service offers automatic savings on storage costs across three low-latency, high-throughput access tiers. If you have data that can be accessed asynchronously, you can choose to enable automatic archiving features within the S3 Intelligent-Tiering storage class. S3 Intelligent-Tiering is designed for 99.9% availability and 99.999999999% durability.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– AWS CloudTrail.**
    
    **– Amazon S3 Intelligent-Tiering.**
    
    **Amazon CloudWatch** is incorrect because it does not capture API-level events from Amazon Bedrock. It only focuses on metrics and application logs, not detailed API call history.
    
    **Amazon S3 Glacier Deep Archive** is incorrect. Although it offers the lowest storage cost among all S3 classes, it is not ideal for storing logs that may require frequent access for auditing or compliance investigations. Retrieval times can range from hours to days
    
    **AWS Config** is incorrect because it primarily tracks resource configuration changes, such as security group updates or IAM policy changes. It does not log API calls or function invocations, which are required in this scenario.
    

An e-commerce company has a customer support team that helps shoppers by providing personalized product recommendations based on its location and browsing history. The company wants to automate this process by using foundation models (FMs) to deliver real-time, location-specific suggestions to customers while shopping.

Which AWS service is best suited for this task?

- Amazon Lex
- **Amazon Bedrock**
    
    Amazon Bedrock is a fully managed service from AWS designed to provide access to a variety of powerful foundation models (FMs) from leading AI companies. These models support tasks including text and image generation, allowing users to build, customize, and scale generative AI applications without requiring advanced machine learning knowledge. Bedrock makes it easier to integrate these models into practical applications through a straightforward API and a broad choice of pre-trained models.
    
    ![Amazon Bedrock](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-AmazonBedrock-18July2025.png)
    
    For use cases such as automating personalized product suggestions based on a customer’s location, Amazon Bedrock serves as an ideal solution. Its library of pre-trained foundation models can be used to create systems that understand and generate content tailored to a customer’s preferences, behavior, and location. With Bedrock’s flexibility and scalability, e-commerce businesses can adapt and fine-tune models to deliver timely, location-aware recommendations that evolve with ongoing customer interactions.
    
    The service also supports advanced AI capabilities like natural language processing (NLP), which is crucial for generating human-like, context-aware product suggestions. By using Amazon Bedrock, companies can streamline the recommendation process, making it both efficient and highly personalized, without the need for complex manual workflows.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon Bedrock.**
    
    **Amazon Rekognition** is incorrect because it primarily focuses on image and video analysis, such as facial recognition, object detection, and activity recognition. While it is powerful for visual processing tasks, Rekognition does not specialize in processing textual or location-based data for generating personalized recommendations. The service is typically used for security or media analysis, not for the real-time, location-specific recommendations needed in this scenario.
    
    **Amazon Transcribe** is incorrect because it is designed for automatic speech recognition (ASR), converting audio into text. Although it can transcribe customer service calls or voice interactions, it does not provide capabilities for generating personalized product recommendations based on customer location and browsing history. Transcribe focuses on speech-to-text conversion, which is simply not aligned with the requirements for location-based recommendation generation.
    
    **Amazon Lex** is incorrect because it is only used to enable the creation of conversational interfaces, such as chatbots and voice assistants. While it excels in building natural language understanding and facilitating interactions through text or speech, it does not have the capability to handle personalized, location-based product recommendations. Lex is typically used for building conversational applications and cannot directly automate personalized suggestions based on customer behavior and location as required by the scenario.
    
- Amazon Transcribe
- Amazon Rekognition

A company using generative AI in a regulated industry wants to review outputs for bias and toxicity before release.

Which technique should be used during the post-processing machine learning lifecycle?

- **Human-in-the-loop**
    
    **Human-in-the-loop** involves integrating human judgment into the AI workflow, allowing human reviewers to assess and correct AI-generated outputs. This approach is particularly effective in the post-processing phase, where human evaluators can identify and mitigate biased or toxic content that automated systems might overlook. AWS provides services like Amazon Augmented AI (A2I) to facilitate HITL workflows, enabling seamless integration of human review into machine learning applications.
    
    ![Human-in-the-Loop](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-human-in-the-loop-18July2025.jpg)
    
    In the post-processing phase of the machine learning lifecycle, it is important to ensure that the outputs generated by AI models are free from bias and toxicity. While several techniques can be employed to achieve this, Human-in-the-loop (HITL) stands out as a direct and effective method.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Human-in-the-loop.**
    
    **Data preprocessing** is incorrect because it only occurs before the model is trained, not during the post-processing phase. While preprocessing is important for preparing clean and unbiased data, it is not directly concerned with reviewing and correcting outputs after the AI model has generated them. Thus, it is not a suitable technique for reviewing model outputs for bias and toxicity post-release.
    
    **Adversarial training** is incorrect because it is a technique used during the training phase to make the model more robust to adversarial inputs or data manipulations. It primarily focuses on improving the model’s performance by exposing it to challenging inputs during training, rather than reviewing or correcting outputs for bias or toxicity after they are generated. Therefore, it is not a post-processing technique.
    
    **Fairness constraints** is incorrect because this option are applied during the model training process to guide the model toward generating more equitable results by enforcing certain fairness criteria. While important for bias mitigation, fairness constraints are part of the model design and training, not the post-processing phase. They do not directly address reviewing the outputs once generated.
    
- Adversarial training
- Data preprocessing
- Fairness constraints

A healthcare technology company is developing a virtual assistant with conversational search for medical queries. It will use a generative AI model to process questions and return relevant responses through a database service that can store embeddings as vectors and supports fast retrieval.

Which AWS service is best suited for this feature?

- Amazon RDS
- Amazon OpenSearch Service
- **Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL**
    
    **Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL** is a fully managed relational database service that provides high availability, scalability, and robust performance. One of the key features of Amazon Aurora with PostgreSQL compatibility is its support for advanced database extensions, such as `pgvector`, which enables the efficient handling of vector data. With `pgvector`, users can store embeddings generated by machine learning models as vectors and perform fast similarity searches based on these vectors. This makes Aurora PostgreSQL an excellent choice for applications requiring real-time querying of AI-generated embeddings.
    
    ![Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-AmazonAuroraPostgreSQL-19July2025.png)
    
    In scenarios where large-scale, high-performance search capabilities are required, such as for conversational search applications, Aurora PostgreSQL provides a scalable, reliable, and cost-effective solution. By leveraging the power of PostgreSQL’s advanced indexing and the pgvector extension, Aurora PostgreSQL enables fast retrieval of AI-generated embeddings, facilitating the smooth operation of systems like virtual assistants or search engines that rely on generative AI models. It can efficiently store and query large amounts of data, making it suitable for applications where low-latency responses are critical.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon Aurora PostgreSQL.**
    
    **Amazon OpenSearch Service** is incorrect because while it is a powerful search engine service, it is primarily designed for full-text search and log analytics. Although OpenSearch can support vector search capabilities through k-nearest neighbor (k-NN) algorithms, it is not a relational database service and lacks the transactional capabilities of a database like Aurora PostgreSQL. OpenSearch is typically used for unstructured data and is not ideal for managing structured data like vector embeddings in the context of a relational database environment.
    
    **Amazon RDS** is incorrect because while it is a managed relational database that supports multiple database engines, including PostgreSQL, it does not natively support the pgvector extension, which is essential for efficiently storing and querying vector embeddings generated by machine learning models. Without this extension, RDS simply lacks the necessary functionality for vector similarity searches, making it unsuitable for applications requiring such capabilities.
    
    **Amazon Redshift** is incorrect because it is primarily a data warehouse service designed for large-scale analytics and business intelligence workloads. While Redshift supports machine learning models for regression and classification tasks, it does not provide native support for vector embeddings or similarity searches.
    
- Amazon Redshift

A business has observed that its foundation model (FM) is generating irrelevant images in response to its input prompts.

The business aims to refine its prompt strategies to reduce the occurrence of such unrelated outputs. Additionally, the business is also considering using Amazon Rekognition for image analysis.

Which solution will help meet these requirements?

- Employ mask prompts.
- Apply outpainting prompts.
- Apply positive prompts.
- **Utilize negative prompts.**
    
    Image generation models require prompts to be phrased like image captions, rather than commands or conversational statements, because they don’t have the ability to reason or interpret explicit instructions. It’s important to include specific details such as the subject, action, environment, lighting, style, and camera position to guide the model in generating relevant images.
    
    **Negative prompts** are a direct and effective solution to refining a model’s output by specifying what should not be included in the generated images. By providing clear instructions that exclude certain content or characteristics, negative prompts help steer the model away from generating irrelevant or undesirable images. For example, a prompt could instruct the model to “Generate an image of a landscape without any animals” or “Create a portrait, excluding any background elements,” effectively minimizing unrelated outputs. This approach guides the model’s behavior by excluding certain variables, leading to more relevant and focused results.
    
    Prompt: “*Magical forest scene: A young girl with a lantern walks along a glowing forest path, surrounded by tall trees and soft mist.*”
    
    **Without Negative prompt:**
    
    ![Without negative prompt](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-without-negative-prompt-19July2025.png)
    
    **With Negative prompt:**
    
    ![With Negative Prompt](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-with-negative-prompt-19July2025.png)
    
    This method directly addresses the issue at hand: reducing the occurrence of irrelevant outputs by modifying how the model interprets and responds to the input prompt. As such, negative prompting is a well-established technique for controlling the relevance of generated content in image-based models.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Utilize negative prompts.**
    
    The option that says: **Apply outpainting prompts**is incorrect because it is a technique typically used in image generation to expand or extend an existing image beyond its original borders, often by filling in the blank spaces in a creative way. While outpainting can be useful for enhancing or adding context to an image, it does not address the core issue of generating irrelevant images in response to the prompt.
    
    The option that says: **Employ mask prompts** is incorrect because it is primarily used to guide the model in modifying specific parts of an image, either by focusing on a particular area or creating a mask that directs attention to certain elements. While masking can help refine image content by focusing on certain features, it does not directly solve the issue of generating irrelevant images.
    
    The option that says: **Apply positive prompts** is incorrect because this prompt simply focuses on specifying what should be included in the generated image, such as “Generate an image of a beach with a sunset.” While positive prompts are useful for directing the model to produce specific content, they do not directly address the issue of irrelevant images. Positive prompts are good for reinforcing desired output, but are not as effective as negative prompts in actively reducing undesired or irrelevant content in the model’s output.
    

A retail company needs an AI system to automatically scan product images for defects, such as scratches or dents, before listing products online.

Which of the following types of AI applications would best suit this need?

- Image segmentation
- Optical character recognition (OCR)
- **Computer vision**
    
    Computer vision is a technology that enables machines to automatically recognize and accurately describe images. Today, computer systems can access a vast amount of images and video data from sources like smartphones, traffic cameras, security systems, and other devices. Applications of computer vision utilize artificial intelligence (AI) and machine learning (ML) to process this data effectively for tasks such as object identification, facial recognition, classification, recommendations, monitoring, and detection.
    
    The introduction of machine learning and artificial intelligence enhances visual inspection capabilities through computer vision (CV) ML models. By complementing human inspection with CV-based ML, organizations can reduce detection errors, speed up production, lower quality costs, and ultimately improve customer satisfaction. Traditionally, building CV ML models requires expertise in data science and coding, which are often scarce in manufacturing organizations. However, with the availability of no-code ML services, quality engineers and other personnel on the shop floor can now create and evaluate these models, thus accelerating their exploration and adoption throughout manufacturing operations.
    
    ![Computer Vision Defect Detection Model](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/cv-defect-detection-model-21JUL2025.png)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Computer vision.** It refers to the AI field dedicated to enabling machines to interpret visual information intelligently. It’s ideal for detecting defects like scratches or dents in product images, using techniques such as object detection, anomaly detection, and deep learning-based classification.
    
    **Optical character recognition (OCR)** is incorrect because OCR is primarily designed to identify and extract text from images, such as printed labels or serial numbers. It doesn’t analyze physical defects or visual abnormalities in non-textual parts of an image.
    
    **Image segmentation** is incorrect because while it helps divide an image into multiple segments, or sets of labeled pixels, it doesn’t inherently detect or evaluate defects. It’s a supportive technique used within broader computer vision applications.
    
    **Image processing** is incorrect because it typically involves manipulating image attributes like brightness or contrast to enhance visibility. It does not include intelligent defect detection or context-aware analysis.
    
- Image processing

A team checks data privacy laws and confirms ethical guidelines for patient data.

Which phase of the ML lifecycle is this?

- Data preprocessing
- Model development
- Feature engineering
- **Business goal identification**
    
    The ML lifecycle is an iterative, cyclic process with **guidelines** and **best practices** to follow throughout the defined phases of developing a machine learning workload. This lifecycle provides clarity and structure, enhancing the chances of a successful machine learning project.
    
    – Business goal identification
    
    – ML problem framing
    
    – Data processing (data collection, data preprocessing, feature engineering)
    
    – Model development (training, tuning, evaluation)
    
    – Model deployment (inference, prediction)
    
    – Model monitoring
    
    The phases of the ML lifecycle are not strictly sequential and may include feedback loops.
    
    ![ML lifecycle](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/ml-lifecycle-21JUL2025.png)
    
    Identifying business goals is the most crucial phase in the machine learning (ML) lifecycle. Organizations considering ML must have a clear understanding of the problem they aim to solve and the business value they hope to gain. It is important to measure this business value against specific objectives and success criteria. While this principle applies to any technical solution, it is especially challenging for ML solutions because the technology is constantly evolving.
    
    Once you have established your success criteria, assess your organization’s capacity to reach that target. The target should be realistic and provide a clear pathway to production. Involve all relevant stakeholders from the start to ensure alignment with this target and any new business processes that may emerge from the initiative.
    
    Begin by determining whether ML is the appropriate approach for achieving your business goal. Evaluate all available options for reaching this goal and consider the accuracy of the potential outcomes, as well as the costs and scalability of each option.
    
    For an ML-based approach to succeed, it is essential to have access to a sufficient amount of high-quality, relevant training data. Carefully evaluate the data to ensure that the correct sources are available and accessible.
    
    **Best practices:**
    
    – [Operational excellence pillar – Best practices](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/machine-learning-lens/operational-excellence-pillar-best-practices.html)
    
    – [Security pillar – Best practices](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/machine-learning-lens/security-pillar-best-practices.html)
    
    – [Reliability pillar – Best practices](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/machine-learning-lens/reliability-pillar-best-practices.html)
    
    – [Performance efficiency pillar – Best practices](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/machine-learning-lens/performance-efficiency-pillar-best-practices.html)
    
    – [Cost optimization pillar – Best practices](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/machine-learning-lens/cost-optimization-pillar-best-practices.html)
    
    – [Sustainability pillar – Best practices](https://docs.aws.amazon.com/wellarchitected/latest/machine-learning-lens/sustainability-pillar-best-practices.html)
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Business goal identification.** This phase sets the strategic direction of the ML project, including reviewing regulatory requirements, ethical standards, and data governance policies before any technical work begins.
    
    **Feature engineering** is incorrect because it focuses on creating or selecting input variables for model training. It doesn’t involve evaluating legal or ethical considerations.
    
    **Model development** is incorrect because it typically refers to training, tuning, and validating models. Compliance decisions should already be established before this phase begins.
    
    **Data preprocessing** is incorrect because it primarily involves cleaning and transforming data for modeling. While it may apply privacy techniques, it doesn’t define the ethical or legal boundaries for using the data.
    

A tech startup is building a customer support system using Amazon Bedrock and a large language model (LLM) to produce inferences. The startup is using Amazon S3 for data storage and wants to optimize costs while evaluating what affects the overall costs for its generative AI applications.

What primarily affects the inference costs for the system?

- **Amount of tokens processed**
    
    **Amazon Bedrock** is a fully managed service from AWS that provides access to a variety of powerful foundation models (FMs) developed by leading AI companies. These models support a range of generative AI tasks, including text generation and language understanding, which are essential for building sophisticated applications like customer support systems. With Amazon Bedrock, developers can integrate pre-trained models and fine-tune them to meet specific application requirements, without needing extensive machine learning expertise. Its straightforward API and broad selection of models make it simple to build, customize, and scale generative AI applications.
    
    ![Amazon Bedrock Token Management](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-AmazonBedrockTokenManagement-21July2025.png)
    
    In use cases involving a high volume of real-time, personalized interactions, such as customer support systems powered by large language models (LLMs), the **amount of tokens processed**directly affects the cost of generating inferences. **Tokens** represent individual units of text that the model analyzes, and a higher token count requires more computational resources during inference. This impact becomes more significant in large-scale deployments where each user interaction may involve substantial data processing. As a result, optimizing token usage plays a critical role in managing costs while maintaining service quality.
    
    By focusing on the number of tokens processed, businesses can better control inference costs in generative AI systems that use Amazon Bedrock. This enables the development of scalable, cost-efficient solutions that still deliver responsive and high-quality user experiences.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amount of tokens processed.**
    
    The option that says: **Frequency of model fine-tuning** is incorrect because fine-tuning typically affects training costs rather than inference costs. While fine-tuning adjusts the model to improve its performance, it does not influence the cost of generating inferences once the model is deployed. Fine-tuning is just a process that happens periodically and is not tied to the scale of real-time inferences being processed.
    
    The option that says: **Model batch size** is incorrect because batch size primarily impacts training efficiency, specifically by determining how many examples are processed simultaneously during training. This does not affect the inference process, where the costs are driven by the number of tokens processed, not how many data points the model handles during training.
    
    The option that says: **Size of training data utilized**is incorrect because the size of training data primarily affects the model’s training phase and overall quality, but does not impact inference costs directly once the model is deployed. While a larger dataset can lead to better model performance, the inference costs are simply driven by how much data (in the form of tokens) the model processes during each request, not the volume of data used for training.
    
- Size of training data utilized
- Model batch size
- Frequency of model fine-tuning

A retail tech startup is leveraging a Retrieval Augmented Generation (RAG) workflow using Amazon Bedrock and Stable Diffusion to produce custom product visuals from textual prompts. Despite its setup, the visuals often appear generic and miss precise product attributes.

The startup needs the AI to generate more specific and detailed images.

Which enhancement best achieves this

- Apply the `MASK_IMAGE_BLACK`technique during image generation.
- Increase the number of training epochs to improve model performance.
- **Set a higher classifier-free guidance (CFG) value to refine image details.**
    
    **Classifier-free guidance (CFG)** is a technique used in generative models like Stable Diffusion to refine the quality and fidelity of the generated images. Increasing the CFG value improves the model’s adherence to the textual prompts, leading to more specific and accurate image generation. This approach works by balancing the model’s focus between creativity (based on the prompt) and its adherence to the guiding classifier, enhancing the precision of the generated visuals. Accordingly, adjusting the CFG value is a well-established way to control image fidelity in generative workflows like RAG and is particularly effective when the goal is to achieve highly detailed and specific outputs.
    
    ![Classifier-free guidance](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-classifier-free-guidance-21July2025.png)
    
    In this context, the `cfgscale` parameter determines how much the final image portrays the prompt. A higher `cfgscale` value means the model will stick closely to the provided description, resulting in more detailed and specific images. Conversely, a lower `cfgscale` value introduces more randomness in the image generation, which might lead to more creative, but less predictable results.
    
    By increasing the CFG value, the model refines the generated output based on the input description, ensuring that the visuals are not only more accurate but also richer in detail. This directly addresses the issue the startup faces of producing generic images with missing specific product attributes. This enhancement effectively ensures that the generated visuals closely align with the textual prompts, making the imagery more tailored and relevant to the startup’s needs.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Set a higher classifier-free guidance (CFG) value to refine image details.**
    
    The option that says: **Increase the number of training epochs to improve model performance** is incorrect because increasing the number of training epochs typically improves the model’s overall learning and performance, but does not directly affect the image’s alignment with specific textual prompts. It may enhance general model capabilities, but not necessarily generate more detailed or specific visuals based on the input description.
    
    The option that says: **Apply the `MASK_IMAGE_BLACK` technique during image generation** is incorrect because this technique is generally used to mask certain areas of an image or focus on specific regions, but it does not enhance the overall detail or accuracy of the generated image in relation to the textual prompts. This technique primarily addresses specific regions rather than the image’s fidelity to the description.
    
    The option that says: **Modify the architecture of the model to include more hidden layers** is incorrect because this option increases the model’s capacity for general learning but does not directly target improving image detail or specificity. This approach would be a more generic improvement to the model, but would not directly address the issue of generating more precise and detailed visuals based on the prompts.
    
- Modify the architecture of the model to include more hidden layers.

A company is developing a generative AI application through custom models in Amazon Bedrock and managing metadata for the training process with Amazon DynamoDB. As part of the model customization jobs, the company aims to ensure that the model artifacts are secured with a company-managed encryption key.

Which of the following would fulfill these conditions?

- AWS Secrets Manager
- Amazon S3 Managed Keys (SSE-S3)
- AWS CloudTrail
- **AWS Key Management Service (KMS)**
    
    **AWS Key Management Service (KMS)** is a fully managed service that allows users to create, control, and manage encryption keys to protect data across AWS services. It simplifies the process of securing sensitive data by providing seamless integration with AWS services like Amazon S3, Amazon RDS, and Amazon EBS, ensuring that data is encrypted at rest and in transit. With KMS, organizations can generate both symmetric and asymmetric encryption keys, manage them securely, and define policies for who can access these keys. This helps ensure that sensitive information, such as model artifacts, remains protected throughout its lifecycle.
    
    ![AWS Key Management Service](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-aws-kms-21Aug2025.png)
    
    By using KMS, businesses can ensure compliance with data protection regulations and implement best practices for cryptographic key management. KMS integrates with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) for fine-grained access control and provides logging capabilities via AWS CloudTrail, allowing businesses to track and audit key usage. This level of control and security is critical when handling sensitive assets, such as model artifacts created during AI model customization. By managing encryption keys through KMS, organizations can ensure that only authorized users and services have access to these critical data assets, maintaining confidentiality and security throughout the process.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **AWS Key Management Service (KMS).**
    
    **AWS Secrets Manager** is incorrect because it is primarily designed to securely store, manage, and rotate sensitive information such as API keys, database credentials, and other secrets. While it plays a vital role in securing access to credentials and other sensitive data, it does not handle encryption key management for securing model artifacts.
    
    **Amazon S3 Managed Keys (SSE-S3)** is incorrect because it provides encryption with AWS-managed keys, which do not allow customers to manage or control the encryption keys. This level of control is needed for securing model artifacts with company-managed encryption keys.
    
    **AWS CloudTrail** is incorrect because it is typically used for logging and auditing API calls across AWS services to provide visibility into account activity. While it helps track access to AWS resources and ensures compliance, it does not provide encryption key management or secure model artifacts. CloudTrail’s role is in monitoring actions and changes, not in protecting or managing data through encryption.
    

A company built an AI translation service using multiple language APIs, each needing unique keys rotated quarterly. The company needs a secure way to store, rotate, and access these keys at runtime without exposing it in code. The solution must integrate with AWS Lambda.

Which AWS service offers secure storage, automatic rotation, and programmatic access for these API keys?

- Amazon Polly
- **AWS Secrets Manager**
    
    **AWS Secrets Manager** is a fully managed service designed to securely store, manage, and rotate sensitive information such as API keys, database credentials, and other secrets. It eliminates the need to hard-code secrets into applications, reducing the risk of accidental exposure. Secrets Manager enables developers to retrieve secrets programmatically using AWS SDKs, CLI, or Lambda functions, ensuring that secrets are only accessible to authorized services and users.
    
    ![AWS Secrets Manager](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-AWS-Secrets-Manager-07-22-2025.png)
    
    One of the key features of Secrets Manager is automatic secret rotation, which helps maintain security best practices by regularly updating credentials without requiring manual intervention or application downtime. It supports integration with AWS Lambda to define custom rotation logic for various types of secrets. This makes it especially useful for applications that rely on external services or APIs requiring periodic key updates.
    
    Secrets Manager also provides built-in integration with AWS Identity and Access Management (IAM) for fine-grained access control and auditing through AWS CloudTrail. With its encryption-at-rest using AWS Key Management Service (KMS) and secure transmission protocols, it ensures that secrets remain protected throughout their lifecycle.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **AWS Secrets Manager.**
    
    **AWS Artifact** is incorrect because it is primarily used for retrieving compliance reports and audit documents related to AWS services. It is only meant for legal and regulatory documentation, such as SOC reports or ISO certifications. It does not provide any functionality for storing, rotating, or programmatically accessing secrets like API keys.
    
    **Amazon Polly** is incorrect because it is a service that only converts text into lifelike speech using deep learning. It is designed for text-to-speech applications and has no capabilities related to security, credential storage, or key rotation.
    
    **AWS Key Management Service (AWS KMS)** is incorrect because it is typically used to create and manage cryptographic keys for data encryption. It is excellent for encrypting secrets but does not handle the storage, rotation, or retrieval of those secrets on its own. KMS works best when used in combination with services like AWS Secrets Manager, which leverages KMS to encrypt stored secrets. While KMS is an important component of AWS’s security infrastructure, it is not by itself a full solution for secret management.
    
- AWS Key Management Service (AWS KMS)
- AWS Artifact

A global e-commerce company stores billions of behavioral and transactional events in Amazon S3 to train fraud detection models. The data science team needs to:

- Run distributed feature extraction with PySpark
- Perform iterative feature engineering
- Schedule scalable, fault-tolerant ETL jobs

Which AWS service best supports large-scale, distributed feature engineering using open-source tools?

- AWS Glue DataBrew
- Amazon OpenSearch Service
- Amazon Redshift
- **Amazon EMR**
    
    **Amazon EMR (Elastic MapReduce)** is a cloud-native big data platform that enables large-scale data processing using popular open-source tools like Apache Spark, Hadoop, Hive, and Presto. It’s designed to efficiently run distributed data workloads such as ETL, machine learning, log analysis, and interactive analytics. With EMR, you can easily provision and scale clusters across Amazon EC2, Amazon EKS, or AWS Fargate, depending on your infrastructure preferences and cost optimization goals.
    
    ![PySpark script for EMR](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-PySpark-script-for-EMR-07-22-2025.png)
    
    One of EMR’s key strengths is its native support for PySpark, allowing data scientists and engineers to run complex feature engineering workflows, data enrichment, and transformation tasks in a highly distributed manner. It integrates seamlessly with Amazon S3 for storing input and output data, and supports iterative processing, which is essential for tuning feature generation logic in machine learning pipelines. Additionally, EMR provides features like automatic scaling, spot instance support, and job scheduling, which make it a reliable and cost-effective solution for building scalable and fault-tolerant ETL pipelines.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon EMR.**
    
    **Amazon OpenSearch Service** is incorrect because it is primarily used for search, log analytics, and real-time monitoring. It is optimized for indexing and querying large volumes of semi-structured data, not for distributed data processing or feature engineering. While it can store and search data, it simply doesn’t support frameworks like PySpark or iterative transformation jobs needed for machine learning workflows.
    
    **AWS Glue DataBrew** is incorrect because it is a low-code, visual data preparation tool that is typically used by data analysts for interactive data cleaning and transformation. It only supports a limited set of operations and is not intended for running distributed, large-scale workloads. It lacks the flexibility and power of PySpark, and cannot efficiently handle complex or iterative feature engineering tasks across billions of records.
    
    **Amazon Redshift** is incorrect because it is a fully managed data warehouse that is primarily optimized for SQL-based analytics and reporting. While it can handle large datasets and supports some machine learning features via Redshift ML, it is not designed for PySpark-based workflows or distributed processing across multiple data sources. It only provides a limited environment for feature engineering and lacks the native support for iterative data transformations required in this scenario.
    

A healthcare company has developed a machine learning model to analyze medical imaging and predict potential diseases. The goal is to deploy the model for real-time analysis of incoming images without the need to manage any servers or infrastructure.

Which solution will best meet this requirement?

- Use AWS Batch to run inference jobs on incoming medical images.
- **Use an Amazon SageMaker AI endpoint to deploy the model.**
    
    Real-time inference is ideal for workloads that require interactive, low-latency responses. You can deploy your model using Amazon SageMaker’s AI hosting services to obtain an endpoint for inference. These endpoints are fully managed and support autoscaling.
    
    ![SageMaker AI Endpoints configurations](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/sagemaker-endpoint-config-22JUL2025.png)
    
    After creating a model, the next step is to set up an endpoint configuration. This configuration allows you to deploy your model according to your specified requirements. In the configuration, you can choose between a real-time endpoint or a serverless endpoint. To create a serverless endpoint configuration, you can use the Amazon SageMaker AI console, the CreateEndpointConfig API, or the AWS CLI.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Use an Amazon SageMaker AI endpoint to deploy the model.** It provides a fully managed service for deploying machine learning models with real-time inference capabilities. SageMaker AI handles server and infrastructure management behind the scenes, supports autoscaling, and is optimized for low latency predictions, making it ideal for medical imaging analysis. It also supports compliance frameworks like HIPAA, which is crucial in healthcare applications.
    
    The option that says: **Use AWS Batch to run inference jobs on incoming medical images** is incorrect because AWS Batch is primarily designed for asynchronous, large-scale batch processing, not for real-time inference. It introduces latency that is unsuitable for scenarios requiring immediate analysis, such as diagnosing medical conditions from incoming images.
    
    The option that says: **Launch the ML model within a Kubernetes cluster managed by Amazon EKS to serve real-time predictions** is incorrect because while technically feasible, it requires significant infrastructure management, including cluster provisioning, scaling, and monitoring. This contradicts the requirement to avoid managing servers or infrastructure.
    
    The option that says: **Deploy the ML model using AWS Lambda functions for real-time predictions** is incorrect because while AWS Lambda can be typically used for serverless computing, it is not designed for serving machine learning models that require continuous or high-performance inference. Lambda functions are typically limited in execution time and memory, which makes them less suitable for real-time model predictions with high processing demands.
    
- Deploy the ML model using AWS Lambda functions for real-time predictions.
- Launch the ML model within a Kubernetes cluster managed by Amazon EKS to serve real-time predictions.

What is the key advantage of leveraging Amazon SageMaker Model Cards for documenting machine learning models?

- **Ensuring standardized information on a model’s function, performance, and boundaries.**
    
    **Amazon SageMaker Model Cards** provide a structured and standardized way to document machine learning models. This includes essential information like the model’s purpose, its performance across various datasets, and its limitations. By using Model Cards, organizations ensure that there is consistency in the way this crucial information is documented across all models, which is important for maintaining transparency, improving model governance, and enabling responsible AI practices. It facilitates easier model comparisons, better communication between teams, and ensures that stakeholders are aware of the models’ intended use cases, performance metrics, and any potential risks.
    
    ![Amazon SageMaker Model Cards](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-sagemaker-model-cards-22July2025.png)
    
    This structured documentation can help reduce biases, improve compliance with regulatory requirements, and enhance trust in machine learning systems by making the model’s details accessible and understandable. Furthermore, it aligns with AWS’s emphasis on responsible AI practices, which encourage organizations to document and share models transparently.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Ensuring standardized information on a model’s function, performance, and boundaries.**
    
    The option that says: **Delivering an easy-to-read report of a model’s strengths and weaknesses** is incorrect because Model Cards simply do not focus on providing a simplified or easy-to-read report. Instead, they offer a more comprehensive and structured format for documenting the technical and performance aspects of a model. While it does highlight limitations and performance, the primary purpose is not to create a report but to standardize model documentation.
    
    The option that says: **Minimizing the overall computational resources required by the model** is incorrect because SageMaker Model Cards are primarily focused on documentation and transparency, not on optimizing computational resources. The reduction of computational requirements is a different concern addressed by other AWS services, like the AWS Compute Optimizer, which helps in optimizing EC2 instance configurations.
    
    The option that says: **Storing models for future access in a centralized repository** is incorrect because Model Cards are not designed for storing or archiving models. They simply serve to document the model’s attributes, not for physical storage.
    
- Delivering an easy-to-read report of a model's strengths and weaknesses.
- Storing models for future access in a centralized repository.
- Minimizing the overall computational resources required by the model.

A company is looking to build an analytics pipeline using AWS Glue Studio. However, the company’s team has limited programming experience and would prefer a solution that simplifies the use of Glue Studio without requiring complex coding skills.

Which of the following will meet the given requirement?

- **Amazon Q Developer**
    
    Amazon Q Developer offers real-time code recommendations. As you write, it automatically generates suggestions based on your existing code and comments. These personalized recommendations can take various forms, from a single line of code to fully developed functions.
    
    ![Amazon Q Developer](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/amazonq-devevloper-22JUL2025.png)
    
    When you begin typing single lines of code or comments, Amazon Q provides suggestions based on your current input as well as your previous entries. It also takes filenames into account.
    
    Inline suggestions are automatically enabled once you download the Amazon Q extension. To start using it, simply write some code, and Amazon Q will begin generating code suggestions for you.
    
    Additionally, you can customize the suggestions that Amazon Q offers to align with your software development team’s internal libraries, proprietary algorithmic techniques, and enterprise coding style.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Amazon Q Developer.** It provides AI-powered assistance for developers working with AWS services, including AWS Glue Studio. It allows users to interact using natural language to generate, troubleshoot, and optimize Glue ETL jobs. This makes it ideal for teams with limited programming experience. Amazon Q Developer simplifies the development process by handling complex code and offering intelligent suggestions directly within the AWS console.
    
    **Amazon Lex** is incorrect because Lex is primarily designed for building conversational interfaces using voice and text, such as chatbots. It does not assist with data analytics pipelines or simplify AWS Glue Studio workflows.
    
    **Amazon Bedrock** is incorrect because Bedrock is a service for building and scaling generative AI applications using foundation models. While powerful for AI use cases, it does not directly support or simplify Glue Studio tasks for analytics pipelines.
    
    **Amazon Comprehend** is incorrect because Comprehend is a natural language processing (NLP) service typically used to extract insights from text, such as sentiment analysis or entity recognition. It’s not designed to help users build or manage Glue Studio pipelines.
    
- Amazon Bedrock
- Amazon Lex
- Amazon Comprehend

A business is utilizing a generative AI model to create a virtual assistant. From time to time, the model’s outputs may contain inappropriate or unsafe content.

Identify the correct Amazon Bedrock Guardrails for each mitigation step. Each filter policy is to be used only once. (Select FOUR.)

- Filter out prompts or model responses containing offensive content such as violence, insults, or the prompt attack.
    - Contextual grounding check
    - **Content Filters**
    - Word filters
    - Denied Topics
- Refrain from discussing topics related to illegal investment guidance or legal advice.
    - **Denied Topics**
    - Word filters
    - Content Filters
    - Contextual grounding check
- Identify and filter out the use of particular harmful terms.
    - Content Filters
    - Denied Topics
    - **Word filters**
    - Contextual grounding check
- Identify and remove data in the model’s responses that is not supported by the given source material.
    - **Content Filters**
    - **Denied Topics**
    - **Contextual grounding check**
    - **Word filters**
- **Evaluation**
    
    **Amazon Bedrock Guardrails** provide a set of safeguards that can be configured to ensure the responsible use of generative AI applications. These guardrails are designed to align with various use cases and responsible AI policies, offering a consistent safety framework for managing risks across multiple foundation models (FMs). The primary function of these guardrails is to filter out harmful or sensitive content, ensuring that AI-generated responses remain aligned with privacy, safety, and ethical standards. They can be applied across model prompts and responses in natural language, making them highly versatile for various applications such as chatbots, customer support systems, and even financial services.
    
    ![Amazon Bedrock Guardrails](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-amazon-bedrock-guardrails-22July2025.jpg)
    
    Amazon Bedrock Guardrails offers several safeguards, or policies, to detect and filter harmful content in generative AI applications:
    
    **– Content Filters**: These filters identify and block harmful text or image content in input prompts or model responses. The filtering process focuses on predefined categories, including Hate, Insults, Sexual Content, Violence, Misconduct, and Prompt Attacks. Additionally, users can fine-tune the strength of these filters for each category, allowing for more customized protection against undesirable content.
    
    **– Denied Topics**: This policy enables the definition of a set of topics that are considered undesirable in the context of a specific application. The guardrails will block any detected instances of these topics in user queries or AI-generated responses, helping to maintain relevance and safety in the conversation.
    
    **– Word Filters**: These filters are designed to block undesirable words, phrases, or profanity based on exact matches. This can include offensive language, competitor names, or other inappropriate terms. By configuring these filters, users can prevent the use of problematic language in AI interactions.
    
    **– Sensitive Information Filters**: These filters aim to block or mask sensitive information, such as personally identifiable information (PII), from user inputs and model responses. This is accomplished through probabilistic detection of data in recognized formats like social security numbers (SSN), dates of birth, addresses, and other identifiers. Users can also implement custom regular expressions to detect specific patterns for sensitive data.
    
    **– Contextual Grounding Check**: This feature helps detect and filter out hallucinations in AI-generated responses by ensuring that the model’s outputs are grounded in the provided source material and are relevant to the user query. This safeguard is crucial for maintaining factual accuracy and reliability in the responses generated by the AI model.
    
    Hence, the correct answers are:
    
    **– Content Filters** – Filter out prompts or model responses containing offensive content such as violence, insults, or the prompt attack.
    
    **– Language processing** – Refrain from discussing topics related to illegal investment guidance or legal advice.
    
    **– Word filters** – Identify and filter out the use of particular harmful terms.
    
    **– Contextual grounding check** – Identify and remove data in the model’s responses that is not supported by the given source material.
    

An AI specialist is tasked with predicting the type of seashell based on attributes such as shell size, shape, texture, color pattern, and the presence of ridges or spines.

Which algorithm is best suited for this classification task?

- **K-Nearest Neighbors (k-NN)**
    
    **K-Nearest Neighbors (k-NN)** is a supervised machine learning algorithm used for classification tasks. It works by classifying data points based on the majority class of their k nearest neighbors, measured using a distance metric like Euclidean distance. k-NN is ideal for problems where data points with similar characteristics are expected to belong to the same class. This algorithm is particularly effective for tasks with clear distinctions between categories, especially when the dataset has relatively few features and does not require complex model training.
    
    ![k-NN visual diagram](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-k-Nearest+Neighbors-22July2025.png)
    
    In Amazon SageMaker AI, the k-NN algorithm is provided as a fully managed solution, making it easy to train and deploy models for classification tasks. SageMaker’s support for k-NN allows users to quickly implement this algorithm on both structured numeric and categorical data without extensive infrastructure setup. The simplicity of k-NN, coupled with its ability to handle various data types, makes it a practical choice for a wide range of classification problems.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **K-Nearest Neighbors (k-NN).**
    
    **Object Detection – MXNet** is incorrect because it is primarily designed for detecting and localizing objects within images. Object detection algorithms, like MXNet, are typically used for image-based tasks where the goal is to find and classify objects, not for classification based on structured data like size, shape, or texture. This makes it unsuitable for tasks involving numerical and categorical features without image data.
    
    **K-Means** is incorrect because it is an unsupervised learning algorithm used for clustering, not for classification tasks. k-Means simply groups similar data points into clusters based on distance, but it does not predict specific class labels, which is a requirement in the given scenario.
    
    **Factorization Machines** is incorrect because it is used for high-dimensional, sparse datasets, such as in recommendation systems, and is not suited for classification tasks involving a small number of attributes. This algorithm is typically applied in situations where interactions between features are crucial, which is not the case for simple classification tasks with limited, clear features.
    
- Object Detection – MXNet
- K-Means
- Factorization Machines

A data scientist needs the model to break down a complex problem into smaller tasks and solve each sequentially.

Which prompt engineering technique does this?

- Generated knowledge prompting
- Complexity-based prompting
- **Least-to-most prompting**
    
    **Least-to-Most Prompting** is a technique where a complex problem is first broken down into simpler subproblems, and then these subproblems are solved sequentially. This approach ensures that each step builds upon the previous one, facilitating a more structured and manageable problem-solving process.
    
    ![Least-to-most prompting](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/td-least-to-most-prompting-23July2025.jpg)
    
    In the context of the given scenario, where a data scientist needs the model to break down a complex problem into smaller tasks and solve them sequentially, Least-to-Most Prompting is the appropriate technique. It allows the model to decompose the problem into manageable parts and solve them in order, leading to a coherent and accurate final solution.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Least-to-most prompting.**
    
    **Complexity-based prompting** is incorrect because this technique typically involves selecting examples based on their reasoning complexity to improve performance on multi-step reasoning tasks. While it can enhance reasoning abilities, it does not specifically focus on decomposing problems into subproblems and solving them sequentially.
    
    **Generated knowledge prompting** is incorrect because this option primarily involves generating prompts that guide the model to produce specific knowledge or information. It does not inherently involve breaking down a problem into smaller tasks or solving them in sequence.
    
    **Directional-stimulus prompting** is incorrect because it simply uses specific cues or stimuli to guide the model’s responses. It is more about directing the model’s attention rather than decomposing problems into subproblems and solving them sequentially.
    
- Directional-stimulus prompting

A company is building a machine learning model to forecast customer churn. Although the model shows strong performance with the training dataset, it struggles to estimate churn effectively for new, unseen data.

Which approach would help improve the model’s performance?

- Change the loss function to one that better suits the data.
- **Raise the regularization coefficient to reduce the model's complexity.**
    
    **Amazon SageMaker AI** is a fully managed service that provides users with the tools to build, train, and deploy machine learning models. It includes built-in features for model optimization, one of which is regularization. **Regularization** is a technique used to minimize overfitting, a situation where the model becomes too closely aligned with the training data, including noise and outliers, making it less effective when working with new, unseen data. If a model performs well during training but fails to deliver accurate results on new input, it is often a sign that the model is overfitting.
    
    ![Regularization Parameter](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/AmazonSageMaker-RegularizationParameter%28Alpha%29-23July2025.png)
    
    **Increasing the regularization coefficient** within Amazon SageMaker AI can help address this challenge by making the model simpler. This is achieved by adding a penalty term to the loss function, which discourages the model from becoming too complex. With a higher regularization value, the model becomes less dependent on the fine-grained details specific to the training data. This shift allows the model to generalize more effectively and improves its ability to make accurate predictions on unfamiliar datasets.
    
    Therefore, modifying the regularization parameter is a direct way to combat overfitting in machine learning models. By intentionally reducing the model’s complexity, the model gains a stronger capacity to make reliable predictions on new data, without being overly swayed by specific patterns present only in the training dataset. Amazon SageMaker’s regularization capabilities offer users a straightforward way to fine-tune and control the complexity of their models, ultimately improving their performance on data that the model has not seen before.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **Raise the regularization coefficient to reduce the model’s complexity.**
    
    The option that says: **Use a simpler model architecture to avoid overfitting** is incorrect because simplifying the model architecture may help prevent overfitting, but it can also reduce the model’s capacity to learn from the data. In some cases, a simpler model might not be able to capture the underlying patterns in the data adequately. Simply reducing the architecture size could lead to underfitting, where the model fails to learn important trends..
    
    The option that says: **Change the loss function to one that better suits the data** is incorrect because the loss function primarily impacts how the model optimizes during training, but it does not directly address overfitting. While adjusting the loss function can be beneficial in some cases, it will not resolve the issue of the model memorizing the training data. Overfitting is best managed by controlling model complexity through techniques like regularization, which is specifically designed to mitigate the risk of overfitting by penalizing overly complex models.
    
    The option that says: **Lower the regularization coefficient to allow the model to capture more complexity** is incorrect because lowering the regularization parameter reduces the penalty on model complexity, which can lead to overfitting. A model that is too complex may perform well on the training dataset but fail to generalize effectively on new, unseen data.
    
- Lower the regularization coefficient to allow the model to capture more complexity.
- Use a simpler model architecture to avoid overfitting.

An AI company accesses third-party cybersecurity and compliance datasets from independent software vendors (ISVs) to improve its AI model audits. These include compliance reports, risk metrics, and threat intelligence feeds. The company wants to be receive notifications when new dataset revisions are published.

What AWS service can be utilized to enable this setup?

- Amazon Simple Notification Service
- AWS Artifact
- **AWS Data Exchange**
    
    **AWS Data Exchange** is a service that allows organizations to securely access, subscribe to, and make use of third-party data from a wide range of providers. It supports the full process of discovering, subscribing to, and managing datasets across diverse industries, including areas such as cybersecurity, compliance, and financial services. This service helps ensure that organizations remain informed with the most current information by automatically providing notifications whenever new dataset revisions are released.
    
    ![AWS Data Exchange](https://media.tutorialsdojo.com/public/TD-AWSDataExchange-24July2025.png)
    
    Through AWS Data Exchange, organizations can smoothly incorporate external datasets into their data processing workflows without requiring complex integration efforts. Once a subscription to a dataset is in place, the service manages the automatic delivery of data updates, eliminating the need for manual tracking and ensuring that users always have access to the latest, most relevant data. In addition, the service simplifies the task of integrating third-party datasets into AWS-based systems and applications, making it an essential solution for organizations that rely on timely, data-driven insights to support decision-making and compliance needs.
    
    Hence, the correct answer is: **AWS Data Exchange.**
    
    **AWS Artifact** is incorrect because it only provides access to AWS compliance reports and security documentation related to AWS services. It does not handle third-party data subscriptions or notifications for new dataset revisions, which is required in this scenario. AWS Artifact is focused on providing compliance resources, not on integrating third-party datasets.
    
    **AWS Config** is incorrect because it primarily focuses on tracking and monitoring the configuration compliance of AWS resources. It ensures that AWS resources adhere to internal and external compliance standards, but it does not manage subscriptions to third-party datasets or provide notifications when external datasets are updated. AWS Config is designed for resource configuration management, not for handling dataset revisions from external sources.
    
    **Amazon Simple Notification Service** is incorrect because it typically functions as a messaging service for sending notifications to subscribers. While SNS can deliver messages such as email alerts, it does not natively manage the subscription and update notifications for third-party datasets. To use SNS in this scenario, additional services like Lambda or EventBridge would be needed for integration.
    
- AWS Config

It also supports ongoing optimization of workflows involving natural language instructions like code generation or document drafting by offering insights into how models respond to various types of input.

    **Text classification** is incorrect because it simply involves assigning labels or categories to text. It is typically used for sorting or filtering, not for generating new content.
    
    **Guardrails for Amazon Bedrock** is incorrect because it is only designed to help control and restrict the output of foundation models to ensure safety, privacy, and compliance. It just filters or moderates content, rather than evaluating a model’s ability to perform complex tasks like generating code.
